# Docs Changelog

A running log of documentation-affecting changes. Add a dated entry whenever a doc is materially updated, and note the code commit/migration that prompted it.

2026-09-19 - Phase 5 Task 6: the campaign page's stages table splits by event type. `GET /api/campaigns/[campaignId]/stages` now returns `event_types` once per response and `keitaro_events` / `keitaro_unmapped` per stage; the Results cell renders one generated segment per registry type between `Checkout` and `Sales` with the amber `⚠ N unmapped` marker beside them, and the totals card gains one tile per type plus the shared badge. `Checkout` (keitaro_type `lead`) stays and is NOT relabelled. The two new surfaces are `StageEventBreakdown` / `EventTotalsTiles` in [components/reports/event-columns-view.tsx](../components/reports/event-columns-view.tsx) — each renders its figures AND its residual, neither half separately exported, which is the same structural rule Task 5 applied to `EventColumnsBar` in a shape that has no column set. The endpoint's grouped `keitaro_stage_results` query moved to [lib/reporting/stage-keitaro-aggregate.ts](../lib/reporting/stage-keitaro-aggregate.ts) so its bars execute the real statement: the scalars group in their own CTE and the per-event object is joined afterwards, because `jsonb_each('{}')` yields no rows (a clicks-only stage must still get one) and PostgreSQL has no `min(jsonb)` (42883). `pending_revenue` (0182) is carried explicitly and has its own bar. Suites: +5 bars in [scripts/test-report-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-report-event-columns-db.ts) (28/0 on camman-v2), +7 in [scripts/test-event-columns-view.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns-view.ts) (28/0), the no-hardcoded-keys gate widened to 20 files (32/0). Docs updated: docs/04-features/conversion-events.md, docs/07-conventions.md.

2026-09-19 - Phase 5 Task 4 review fixes: **three defects the task left behind, one of which Task 5 had already had to work around.** ⭐ **(1) A safety helper defeated by the change that widened its job.** [scripts/_require-migration.ts](../scripts/_require-migration.ts) checked ONE column (`keitaro_stage_results.pending_revenue`, 0182) while the reporting read path had widened to need `events` + `unmapped_conversions` (0185) as well — so against a database at 0182–0184 a guarded script **passed its own precondition and died on a raw `42703` one line later**, which is exactly the failure the helper exists to prevent. `requirePendingRevenueColumn` becomes `requireReportingColumns`, refusing on `REPORTING_READ_COLUMNS` — every column `getStageMetricsInRange` names, each with the migration that adds it — and naming EVERY missing one, not the first. New pure suite [scripts/test-require-migration.ts](../scripts/test-require-migration.ts) **7/0**, no database: **G1** extracts `keitaro_stage_results.<col>` from `stage-funnel.ts`'s own source and asserts SET EQUALITY with the guard's list in both directions (17 = 17), so a column added to the projection and forgotten here goes red before it meets a database that lacks it; G3 drives the real `requireReportingColumns` against a stubbed 0182-era catalogue and reads the refusal it prints. Callers re-checked: the three that had the guard carry the widened one, and **five read-path scripts that never had it** (`verify-creative-report`, `verify-epc-surface-grains`, `verify-lifetime-display`, `verify-operator-grading`, `test-performance-report`) now do. ⭐ **(2) One row, two answers for pending money — FIXED BY COMPUTING IT.** `dimension=hourly` hard-coded the scalar `pending_revenue: 0` ("deliberately not computed") while `ledgerHourEventQuery` computed REAL `pending_n`/`pending_revenue` into the same row's `events`; Task 5 established the map is correct, kept the two apart on screen, and said plainly that a consumer workaround is not a fix. `getHourlyReport` now runs a pending series off the SAME ledger, the SAME `ce.occurred_at` hour bucket and the SAME shared clause family as its approved revenue (`pendingRevenueClause()` beside `approvedRevenueClause()`), and the override is deleted. **Computed rather than made explicitly "unknown"**: the rows were already being scanned for the per-event map, so it costs one aggregate rather than a new access path, and a nullable scalar would have propagated an unknown through `addMetrics`/`scaleMetrics`/the By-Group split for a figure that is not unknowable — held money on an hour was merely unasked for. The scalar is defined exactly as its stage-path twin, so `pending_revenue = Σ events[k].pending_revenue + cross-org strays` holds on hourly as on the projection. ⭐ **(3) A commit that documented an invariant it did not keep.** `FunnelTally` gained `events` and `unmapped` but NOT `manual_topup`, which sat on `StageMetrics` beside the tally — so `mergeFunnel` carried two of the three terms and `/api/keitaro/reports` re-rolled the third BY HAND at three grains, correctly, with nothing failing had it missed one (an unsummed `manual_topup` reads as a blank footnote, not a wrong number), while `07-conventions.md` claimed the read layer "carries all three, on every dimension and on the totals". **Carried, structurally**: `manual_topup` is now a `FunnelTally` field riding the same merge and the same `withFunnelDerived` spread as the breakdown it explains, `StageMetrics.manual_topup` is gone, and the route's three hand-sums are deleted. `addRowToFunnel` deliberately does not touch it (a stored Keitaro row has no manual column); `getStageMetricsInRange` assigns it per stage and copies it onto `grand` explicitly, as it already does for `sales`. Every dimension re-verified, not just Overview. **Minors, all fixed:** the hourly path emitted **all-zero** per-event entries where the projection FILTERs the key out — it now applies the same test, in JS, AFTER the row's `unmapped` count is taken off those same rows (**R14**, one-sided against R9); the By-Group footing bars ran a `1e-6` epsilon over values `round2()`d per row and passed only by fixture luck — the tolerance is now derived from the rounding (`0.005 × rows`) and the comment says agreement-within-the-rounding is all that is guaranteed; the bars' accessors take `(map, key)` so a red proof that DELETES `events` prints a wrong number instead of a TypeError (**demonstrated**: the identical mutation crashes the old form and reports `G1:0 G2:0` under the new one); `/api/keitaro/results` emitted a false `events: {}` / `unmapped: 0` for columns it deliberately does not select — new `withoutEventBreakdown()` DELETES all three fields, because an absent field and an empty map are different claims (**W20** pins all three bodies); the DB suite's teardown leftover count was org-wide and is now scoped to the run's tag (**demonstrated** with a concurrent decoy org: the old predicate reports 1 false leak, the new one 0). Verified: [scripts/test-require-migration.ts](../scripts/test-require-migration.ts) **7/0**, [scripts/test-event-tally-merge.ts](../scripts/test-event-tally-merge.ts) **23/0** (new **F8** enumerates `FunnelTally`'s keys through `mergeFunnel` — the bar whose absence let `manual_topup` be missed; F9 pins it through the spread; F10–F12 pin `withoutEventBreakdown`), [scripts/test-event-columns-view.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns-view.ts) **21/0**, [scripts/test-reports-no-hardcoded-event-keys.ts](../scripts/test-reports-no-hardcoded-event-keys.ts) **31/0**, [scripts/test-report-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-report-event-columns-db.ts) **23/0** on camman-v2 (`Target DB: camman-v2 (preview)`) with new one-sided fixtures — an hour holding `$40` PENDING and `$0` approved, and an hour holding one REJECTED purchase and nothing else — so **R13** cannot read the sentinel and the truth as the same number. **Eight red proofs, each mutated to a WRONG RESULT (never a crash), restored from a byte copy and `cmp`/md5-verified**: the guard's list losing `events` (⇒ G1 `unguarded=[events]`), the sentinel `pending_revenue: 0` put back (⇒ R13/R13b `16:0/40`, W19), the all-zero filter removed (⇒ R14 `h11 keys=["purchase"]`), `grand.manual_topup` + `mergeFunnel`'s carry dropped (⇒ R12 `grand=0 Σstages=3`, R12b, F8, F9), one results body unwrapped (⇒ W20 `wrapped=2 bare=1`), the By-Group rows' map deleted (⇒ R8/R8b report, with the OLD accessors crashing on the identical mutation as the control), the By-Group split made to lose 10% (⇒ R8/R8c — the new tolerance still fails), and a concurrent decoy org for the teardown scoping. `tsc --noEmit -p .` **0**, eslint clean on changed files, `check:guards` and `check:docs` green. No migration, no schema change. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 04-features/reports-rollup.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-19 - Phase 5 Task 5: **the report tables render the generated per-event columns — the first task of the phase that is on a screen.** Both endpoints now emit `event_types` (the registry, via `loadEventTypes`) beside the rows: [app/api/reports/performance/route.ts](../app/api/reports/performance/route.ts) on **all three** response bodies (the `lifetime` branch, the `creative` branch and the default — miss one and `dimension=creative` renders no event columns while By Offer does) and [app/api/keitaro/reports/route.ts](../app/api/keitaro/reports/route.ts) on its one. New shared client half [components/reports/event-columns-view.tsx](../components/reports/event-columns-view.tsx) (`eventColsFor`, `tierBColumnCount`, `fmtEventCell`, `EventColumnsBar`), consumed by BOTH [components/reports/performance-report.tsx](../components/reports/performance-report.tsx) and [components/reports/keitaro-report.tsx](../components/reports/keitaro-report.tsx), so the two tables cannot disagree about what a "Registrations" column is. The block is spliced **after `Redir %`, before `Sales`**, located by the neighbouring column's **id** rather than by an index, so the row reads as one funnel and no existing column moves relative to its neighbours. With production's registry (0181) that is seven always-visible columns — Registrations · Registration rate · Registration pending · Purchases · Purchase rate · Purchase pending · Registration→Purchase % — plus three behind a per-browser **Event breakdown** toggle (Purchase $ · Purchase pending $ · Purchase EPC), off by default because each duplicates an aggregate column already on screen. ⭐ **ALL NINE COLUMNS THE OWNER NAMED ARE VISIBLE BY DEFAULT**: Clicks (human), Revenue (approved), Pending $ and EPC were already default-visible aggregates, and the generated counts, rates and funnel ratio are tier A. ⭐ **THE UNMAPPED BADGE CANNOT BE HIDDEN WHILE THE BREAKDOWN IS SHOWN, STRUCTURALLY.** `sales`/`revenue` resolve their flags through non-org-scoped id lists while `events` comes from an org-scoped join, so a cross-organisation event type is counted by the SCALAR and placed under no key — it surfaces only as `unmapped`, and a readable breakdown beside an unreadable stray count under-explains its own total (`sales = Σ (is_purchase) n + manual_topup + strays`). So `EventBreakdownToggle` and `UnmappedBadge` are **not exported**; the only export is `EventColumnsBar`, which renders both, and it is mounted unconditionally — never inside a `showEvents` branch, and **outside the empty and error states**, because a wholly unmapped conversion resolves to no stage, appears in no row, and therefore exists precisely in the ranges whose table is empty (seen live: By Group showed zero rows and the amber badge). ⭐ **`tierBColumnCount()` takes no `showTierB` argument**, so the "count reads 0 while the toggle is ON ⇒ the control unmounts ⇒ tier B can never be switched off" bug is unrepresentable rather than merely tested. ⚠️ **HOURLY CARRIES TWO ANSWERS FOR PENDING MONEY, and the investigation is recorded rather than papered over**: the scalar `pending_revenue` is zeroed by hand on hourly ("deliberately not computed rather than half-computed"), while `ledgerHourEventQuery()` DOES compute `pending_n`/`pending_revenue` into `m.events`, off `conversion_events`, on the same `ce.occurred_at` ET hour as that row's `sales` and `revenue`. **The per-event map is the correct one** — the scalar's 0 is a NOT-COMPUTED sentinel, not a measurement — so the per-event pending columns stay on hourly; hiding a true number to agree with a placeholder would be backwards. That is safe only while no hourly column renders the scalar, which was implicit and is now bar **W20** (with **W19** as its positive control: the same extractor must FIND a scalar pending column in `FULL_COLS`). Making the two AGREE would mean a pending series in the hourly aggregation — Task 4's layer, not this one, and left there deliberately. Related: hourly emits **all-zero** entries where the projection FILTERs the key out; both still produce the same column set because `visibleEventTypes()` keys on a non-zero field, pinned by **W21** one-sided against W4. `/api/keitaro/reports` also **sums `manual_topup` itself** per campaign and for the grand total — unlike `events`/`unmapped` it rides no `withFunnelDerived` spread (it lives on `StageMetrics`, not `FunnelTally`), so declaring it without summing would put `undefined` on every row — and its sort whitelist gained a SHAPE test for generated ids through `eventColumnById()`, the ONE parser of that grammar, rather than a second regex; a null (unknown ratio) sorts LAST in both directions, server-side and client-side alike. The `Sales` column header and stat card now say Sales is tracker conversions PLUS the manual tally, and the card shows `N from the manual tally` when `manual_topup > 0`. ⚠️ **MEASURED TABLE WIDTH, because the owner gets a choice at the phase's STOP**: on the live app at 1440px (1111px of container beside the 248px sidebar), before and after on ONE DOM, `/reports/offer` goes from **18 columns / 1405px** (294px of overflow) to **25 columns / 2235px** (1124px). **It already scrolled horizontally before this change**; this widens an existing scroll rather than introducing one. No column the owner asked for was hidden to avoid it. Verified: [scripts/test-event-columns-view.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns-view.ts) **21/0** pure (W13–W16 render the real `EventColumnsBar` through `renderToStaticMarkup` and read the markup, rather than asserting about source text), [scripts/test-reports-no-hardcoded-event-keys.ts](../scripts/test-reports-no-hardcoded-event-keys.ts) **31/0** — the gate on the phase's central claim over 19 files, three needle forms per key (quoted, dot/optional-chain property, object-literal key or hard-coded generated id), matched against WHITESPACE-COLLAPSED source so CRLF and LF files are treated alike and no needle can contain a newline it would never find; `\b` spares `t.is_purchase` and `is_purchase:` (the registry FLAGS every module is meant to read) and G0g pins that it also spares `repurchaseRate`; **G0a–G0h are negative controls on the matcher itself**, **G1** fails when a listed path does not exist so a rename cannot drop out of coverage, and **G3b** keeps any rendering surface from fetching `/api/keitaro/results`, whose explicit projection omits `events` so its `{}` means "not selected" and not "zero of everything" (G3a is its positive control). **Seven red proofs, each mutated to a WRONG RESULT rather than a crash, restored from a byte copy and `cmp`/md5-verified**: a quoted key array added to the view module (⇒ G2 names the file), unquoted `row.events.purchase.n` appended to the campaign page (⇒ G2), a surface made to fetch `/api/keitaro/results` (⇒ G3b), `tierBColumnCount` given back its `showTierB` parameter (⇒ W12 reads 0), the badge made conditional on the toggle (⇒ W13 with the breakdown on screen and the badge absent), the column set discovered from the DATA instead of the registry (⇒ W1/W2/W7/W12/W17/W18, six red), and a scalar pending column added to `HOURLY_COLS` (⇒ W20). G1 was additionally observed red naturally, before the view module existed. **Seen on screen**, not inferred: a local `next dev` against **camman-v2** (its `.env.development.local` the only env file loaded — production's `.env.local` was moved out of the process for the duration and restored md5-identical with its 10 hard links intact), a THROWAWAY user with exactly one membership, and two seeded `keitaro_stage_results` days carrying a breakdown and four unmapped conversions. Overview rendered 27 columns with the amber **4 unmapped** badge, Registrations 45 / Purchases 9 / Registration→Purchase % 20.0%, `Registration rate` as **—** (counted_clickers 0 on preview — the null-renders-as-em-dash rule, live); ticking the toggle gave 30 columns, the three money columns, and **the badge still there**; `/reports/hourly` rendered all seven generated columns reading 0 for a registry with no conversions and **no scalar pending column**. Every fixture torn down **by id** — camman-v2 back to its exact baseline (ksr 0, orgs 2, campaigns 3, stages 4, sends 500, user gone). `tsc --noEmit -p .` **0**; eslint clean on the new view module, both new suites and both API routes — the 2 errors + 2 warnings on `keitaro-report.tsx`/`performance-report.tsx` are **pre-existing**, confirmed by linting HEAD's copies of both files and getting the identical rules at shifted line numbers; `check:guards` and `check:docs` green. No migration; production stays at 0181 and this code cannot run there until 0182–0185 are applied. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 04-features/reports-rollup.md, 07-conventions.md, operator-api.md.

2026-09-19 - Phase 5 Task 4: **the per-event breakdown rides the shared metrics through every report dimension and the hourly tab** — still inert for the UI. `FunnelTally` ([lib/keitaro/funnel.ts](../lib/keitaro/funnel.ts)) and `PerfMetrics` ([lib/reporting/performance-report.ts](../lib/reporting/performance-report.ts)) gain `events` (the per-`event_types.key` split of sales / revenue / pending_revenue, migration 0185), `unmapped` (conversions that matched no mapping and count as nothing anywhere) and `manual_topup`. ⭐ **`events` ALONE DOES NOT EXPLAIN SALES, so no path carries it alone.** `sales` resolves `is_purchase` through the non-org-scoped `PURCHASE_EVENT_TYPE_IDS` while the breakdown comes from an org-scoped join, and a stage's `sales` also carries the manual tally, so the identity is `sales = Σ (is_purchase) events[t].n + manual_topup + cross-org strays` — the strays being what `unmapped` holds. `manual_topup` was already computed inside `getStageMetricsInRange()` and **discarded**; it is now recorded on `StageMetrics`, because the per-event columns count TRACKER events only and it is exactly the gap. Every accumulator carries all three: `addRowToFunnel`, `mergeFunnel`, `stageMetrics()` (which COPIES the map — one `getStageDimensionReports()` call hands the same `StageMetrics` to several dimensions), `addMetrics()`, `scaleMetrics()`, the By-Group fractional split and Hourly. **By Group splits the map on SALE weights** (the same basis, and the same documented `?? sentW` re-attribution, as the `sales` it breaks down) via `scaleEventMap`; `unmapped` and `manual_topup` have no finer weight by construction and split on SENT, so only the page total is exact. **Hourly does not read `keitaro_stage_results` at all** and gets its own pass, the new exported **`ledgerHourEventQuery()`**, bucketed on `ce.occurred_at` exactly like `ledgerHourQuery()` so the split lands in the same hours as the series it breaks down; one query answers both questions because `event_key IS NULL` IS the unmapped bucket, and the `event_types` join carries `org_id` as well as the id. ⭐ **The unmapped FILTER keys on `et.key IS NULL`, never `ce.event_type_id IS NULL`** — the same choice, for the same reason, as the projection: the raw-column form leaves a hole exactly the width of the cross-org stray, which the scalar counts and the breakdown cannot place. Bar R10b seeds a second org and fails on the raw-column form. ⭐ **New `zeroMetrics()`, and `Object.freeze(ZERO.events)`**: `{ ...ZERO }` is a SHALLOW spread, so the eight accumulators that mutate their result would have shared ONE `events` map across requests and leaked one org's breakdown into another's response — silently. All eight converted, including two that were safe only by coincidence of how `addMetrics` happens to be written. `dimension=creative&range=lifetime` reads a **stored blob that is cast, not parsed**, so `normaliseLifetimeRow()` ([lib/reporting/creative-lifetime.ts](../lib/reporting/creative-lifetime.ts)) now runs `parseEventMap` + `?? 0` over its rows, its totals and each `offer_totals` entry — a pre-0185 blob degrades to an empty breakdown instead of `undefined` reaching the renderer. ⚠️ **DEPLOY NOTE: that endpoint serves the pre-deploy blob for up to an hour** (the refresh is an hourly cron) and reads 0 for every generated column meanwhile, which is indistinguishable from a configured-but-idle event type — trigger `/api/cron/refresh-creative-lifetime` right after the deploy and read the response's `stale_seconds` to tell the two apart. ⚠️ **The funnel's select now needs 0185 as well**; it already needed 0182, and production is still at 0181, so `getStageMetricsInRange()` cannot run there until 0182–0185 are applied. Verified: [scripts/test-event-tally-merge.ts](../scripts/test-event-tally-merge.ts) **18/0** pure — A1/A2 ENUMERATE `PerfMetrics`' keys rather than naming them, so a sixth field added later without touching `addMetrics`/`scaleMetrics` goes red here instead of on one tab; M1–M3 pin money through scaling and merging (a 4-decimal revenue survives both mergers exactly, Σ scale(m, fracᵢ) = m, and scale-then-sum equals sum-then-scale); U1 pins `unmapped`/`manual_topup` through all four accumulators. [scripts/test-report-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-report-event-columns-db.ts) **17/0** on camman-v2 — a throwaway org with COMMITTED fixtures and explicit teardown, because `getStageMetricsInRange` binds the module-level `db` and cannot see a rolled-back transaction; every number is seeded by the script and the fixtures are one-sided (the registrations carry no revenue and no purchase flag, the top-up exists only in `stage_manual_sales`, and the cross-org stray sits in its own hour). **Nine red proofs, each restored from a byte copy and `cmp`/md5-verified**: `addMetrics` dropping `b`'s events (A1/A5/M2), `scaleMetrics` dropping `manual_topup` (A2/U1), `addMetrics` mutating its first input (A2/A5), `mergeFunnel` aliasing the source map (F6), the freeze removed (A4), the `?? 0` fallbacks dropped (A6), the By-Group split removed (R8/R8b — By Group goes to zeros while every other tab stays green, which is the exact failure mode the bar exists for), `ledgerHourEventQuery` pointed at `ce.created_at` (R9/R9b/R10b/R11), and the unmapped FILTER keyed on the raw column (R10/R10b). Every bar reads through a sentinel rather than dereferencing the key a mutation would remove, so a red proof prints which bar failed instead of dying on a TypeError. [scripts/test-report-rollup.ts](../scripts/test-report-rollup.ts) **11/11 on production** (read-only over the matviews, untouched by this change). [scripts/test-stage-funnel.ts](../scripts/test-stage-funnel.ts) and [scripts/test-performance-report.ts](../scripts/test-performance-report.ts) gained the per-event footing and now BOTH name their world-state: they are PROD scripts, the Jul 18-19 window is empty on camman-v2, and an empty window is not a verification — each prints NOT A VERIFICATION and exits 1 with **0 structural failures**, instead of four reds that read like regressions. Neither can run against production at all until 0182 lands, which is pre-existing (HEAD's funnel already selects `pending_revenue`). `tsc --noEmit -p .` 0 — the same invocation caught three real errors earlier in this change, so it is checking — eslint clean on all eight changed files (five pre-existing `no-explicit-any` in the two suites fixed in passing), `check:guards` and `check:docs` green. No migration. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 04-features/reports-rollup.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-19 - **Phase 5 Task 3 review fixes: a production tool's dry run is the predicate again, and a false invariant is retired from the three places it was read.** ⭐ **THE RESYNC'S PREVIEW NO LONGER DESCRIBED THE RUN.** [scripts/resync-stage-day-conversions.ts](../scripts/resync-stage-day-conversions.ts) is the manual PRODUCTION repair path — an operator reads the diff and then says yes to `--apply` — and its header asserted "THE DIFF IS THE PREDICATE `--apply` USES, not an approximation of it". It was a hand-retyped copy of four things, and it fell behind **twice**: Task 6 redefined a sale and the script kept deriving one from `keitaro_type IN ('lead','sale','rejected')` (counting refunds, so the printed numbers were already wrong before this commit); Phase 5 added `events` / `unmapped_conversions` to the upsert's change test AND to the zeroing UPDATE's content test, and the script tested neither in either branch — nor `pending_revenue`, ever. A stage-day whose only content was a breakdown was zeroed or rewritten by `--apply` and invisible in the preview. **The script now holds no SQL at all.** [lib/keitaro/stage-day-conversions.ts](../lib/keitaro/stage-day-conversions.ts) gains `PROJECTED_COLUMNS` (the seven derived columns, one list), `projectionChangedClause(a, b)` (the upsert's `WHERE`, now also the diff's rewrite test), `projectionNonEmptyClause(a)` (the zeroing UPDATE's content test, now also the diff's zero test), `stageDayLedgerCtes(scope)` (the `per_event` → `ledger_raw` → `ledger` derivation, shared by the write and the preview — the ledger CTE names its payout column `payout_at_conversion` so one builder can emit the test for `keitaro_stage_results` vs `EXCLUDED` and for `k` vs `l`) and the exported read-only `readStageDayResyncDiff()`. The diff re-applies the INSERT's `JOIN campaign_stages` (a cross-org ledger row writes nothing, so it must not preview as an insert) while the coverage floor deliberately does not, exactly as the two write statements differ. The script also prints its **target host** on line 1 and widens its INPUT SCOPE count to every projected column. ⭐ **Proven by execution, not by comparing SQL text**: new bars **R0–R10** run `readStageDayResyncDiff()` and `syncStageDayConversions()` against ONE world and require the changed-row sets to be equal **in both directions**, with six one-sided breakages a side (a pre-0185 row whose scalars are right and whose breakdown is empty; an `unmapped_conversions`-only difference; a `pending_revenue`-only difference; a rejected-purchase day carrying the pre-Task-6 sale count — which the old diff called correct; and two covered days the ledger cannot explain whose only content is one of the two new columns). ⭐ **"THE SCALARS ARE LITERALLY THE SUM OF THIS OBJECT'S ENTRIES AND THE TWO CANNOT DRIFT" WAS FALSE**, and it was stated without qualification in `db/schema.ts` (the first thing a Phase 5 UI implementer reads), in 0185's header and in the entry below. The drift is deliberate: `sales`/`revenue` resolve the flags through the NON-org-scoped `PURCHASE_EVENT_TYPE_IDS`/`REVENUE_EVENT_TYPE_IDS` while the entries come from the ORG-SCOPED join, so a cross-org `event_type_id` is counted by the scalar and placed under no key — the suite pins that residual at **1 row / $70** on purpose. The corrected text states the identity WITH its residual (`sales = Σ (is_purchase) n + strays`), names where the stray comes from, names the bars that pin it non-zero (P23c/P14b + P23d/P14c), and tells a screen not to render `events` as an explanation of Sales without `unmapped_conversions` beside it. 📌 **Migration 0185's header is deliberately NOT edited**: drizzle records a SHA-256 of the migration's content and `scripts/verify-migration-integrity.ts` compares it, so a comment-only edit to a file already applied on camman-v2 is a false drift and makes `PgDialect.migrate` treat it as unapplied. `db/schema.ts` + the docs are the authoritative text, and both say so. Minors: `unmapped_conversions` is documented as **broader** than `conversion_events_unmapped_idx`'s predicate (it keys on the join result), with the consequence recorded at BOTH definitions — it and `lib/conversions/monitor.ts`'s `unmapped`/`status_only_unmapped` Telegram combos disagree by the stray, permanently and correctly, and the column is the more truthful of the two; the `ledger_behind_history` probe's comment no longer claims `keitaro_stage_results_campaign_date_idx` serves `stat_date < …` (it is `(campaign_id, stat_date)`, so the range is a **seq scan plus filter** — cheap at ~17.6K rows, and now honest); **P12** reads its `is_purchase` key set from the REGISTRY instead of `k === "purchase"`, which is the one thing the `deposit` fixture exists to forbid; **P23** names the stored day it requires to be unexplained, because `every` over an empty array is `true`; the pre-existing A9 payout bar is renumbered **P1 → A9a**, since Phase 5 opened a second P-series and "FAIL P1" named two bars. Verified: [scripts/test-stage-day-conversions.ts](../scripts/test-stage-day-conversions.ts) **119/0** on camman-v2 inside a rolled-back transaction (108 before), [scripts/test-conversion-monitor.ts](../scripts/test-conversion-monitor.ts) 53/0 and [scripts/test-conversion-monitor-db.ts](../scripts/test-conversion-monitor-db.ts) 58/0 unchanged, [scripts/test-ledger-predicates.ts](../scripts/test-ledger-predicates.ts) 17/0, the resync DRY RUN on camman-v2 (never `--apply`, never production). **Five red proofs, each restored from a byte copy and `cmp`/md5-verified** (a shared predicate cannot be broken by editing the shared object — both sides move together — so each mutation reproduces the historical regression by BREAKING the sharing): the diff's rewrite test hand-written back to the pre-Phase-5 four columns ⇒ **6 red**, R1 naming the three rows that changed and were not listed; the diff's zero test hand-written back to the four-column content list ⇒ **5 red**, R1 naming the two zeroed-but-unlisted days; the diff given its own pre-Task-6 `ledger` CTE ⇒ **8 red**, R7 showing the rejected purchase previewed as a sale and R2 listing seven phantom rows; `SALES_FILTER` reverted to `keitaro_type IN ('lead','sale','rejected')` ⇒ **14 red** including P12 (`3 vs 2 over keys [purchase]`), so the registry-derived footing still guards what the hard-coded one did; and the S7 stale day deleted before the recomputation ⇒ **P23 red with `uncovered=[]`** — the exact vacuity the pin exists for, which the old bar would have passed. Neighbouring suites unchanged on camman-v2: `test-event-columns` 55/0, `test-stage-event-columns-db` 19/0, `test-ledger-predicates-db` 16/0. `tsc --noEmit -p .` 0, eslint clean on every changed file, `check:guards` and `check:docs` green. No migration; production stays at 0181. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 03-data-model.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-19 - **Production-safety fixes on the four unreleased migrations, and a claim of ours that was false.** ⭐ **THE LOCK GAP.** Drizzle applies every pending migration in ONE transaction and `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` covers that transaction, so **only the migration that LEADS the pending batch sets it**. `0181` carried one and protected the batch it led — and once `0181` shipped, the next production batch began at `0182`, which set nothing, so the protection evaporated between two deploys with no file changing. `0182`, `0184` and `0185` now carry the same `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` first statement that `0181` and `0183` do (`0182` because it leads the next production batch; the other two so a solo apply or roll-forward is equally protected). **The risk is QUEUEING, not duration**: the added columns use PG 11+'s catalog-stored default (`atthasmissing`), so there is no rewrite at ~17,600 rows and each ALTER is milliseconds — but an unbounded ACCESS EXCLUSIVE request parks every reader and writer of the table behind it, and `/api/keitaro/poll` allows `maxDuration = 230`. Journal `when` bumped for each (`0182` `1792108800000 → 1792195200000`, `0184` `1792368000000 → 1792454400000`, `0185` `1792454400000 → 1792540800000`; 186 entries, strictly ascending, no duplicate `idx`/`when`), re-runnability checked statement by statement first: `0182` two `IF NOT EXISTS`, `0184` `DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS` **followed by** the `ADD CONSTRAINT` it re-adds (`ADD CONSTRAINT` has no `IF NOT EXISTS` form, so the preceding drop is what makes it re-runnable), `0185` two `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`. ⭐ **A bump only re-applies if it clears the target's NEWEST recorded `created_at`, not merely its own old value** — `PgDialect.migrate` compares every entry against ONE row. camman-v2's newest was `1792454400000`, so of the three only `0185`'s bump re-applied there; `0182`'s new value sits below the mark **by construction**, since raising it past `0185` would break the journal's order. Harmless here because a `SET LOCAL` has no catalog effect and preview already holds the right shape — but recorded, because a SHAPE fix to a middle entry cannot be delivered by a bump at all. ⭐ **"Re-runnable" is not "shape-repairing"**: `IF NOT EXISTS` matches on the column NAME ALONE, so a re-apply over a column of the wrong type is a silent no-op; `S8b`'s label now says what it does and does not assert. ⭐ **OUR OWN "COMPLETELY INERT" WAS FALSE OF THE SCHEMA MIRROR.** `db.select()` with no projection expands to every column `db/schema.ts` names, so `/api/keitaro/results` was selecting `events` and `unmapped_conversions` and would have answered **42703** against production (still at 0181) — from an endpoint that reads neither. It now names its 14 columns; `pending_revenue` (0182) stays, because `addRowToFunnel` genuinely reads it, which is the point: the explicit list removes the ACCIDENTAL dependency, not the deliberate one. It was the only bare select over that table. ⭐ **The migration probe could have ALTERed the real table**: `scripts/test-stage-event-columns-db.ts` rewrote only the `public.`-qualified name — correct by coincidence, since that is every reference 0185 happens to make — so an unqualified `ALTER TABLE keitaro_stage_results` in a future migration would have passed through untouched. `public.` is now optional in the pattern and new bar **S8a** refuses to replay ANYTHING if one occurrence survives. **S4** renamed to name the catalog fact it asserts (the DEFAULT; the NOT NULL half is S3's bar). Verified: [scripts/test-event-columns.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns.ts) **55/0** pure (was 53 — new `N1` pins the premise that a bare select really does expand to the mirror, `N2` scans 674 files under `app/` and `lib/` for a recurrence), [scripts/test-stage-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-stage-event-columns-db.ts) **19/0** on camman-v2 inside a transaction that always rolls back (was 18). **Three red proofs, each restored byte-identical (`cmp` + md5)**: the rewrite made to miss ⇒ S8a + S8 + S8b red with `safe=false` and nothing executed against the real table; the mirror's column renamed ⇒ N1 red; the route reverted to `db.select()` ⇒ N2 red, naming the file. Plus a positive demonstration that the rewrite is total — an unqualified `ALTER` in 0185 still lands on the probe fixture, S8a/S8/S8b green. `tsc --noEmit -p .` 0, eslint clean on every changed file, `check:guards` and `check:docs` green. No new migration; production stays at 0181. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 03-data-model.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-19 - Phase 5 Task 3: **the stage-day projection writes the per-event breakdown**. [lib/keitaro/stage-day-conversions.ts](../lib/keitaro/stage-day-conversions.ts) now groups its single pass over `conversion_events` one level finer — by `event_types.key` — in a `per_event` CTE and rolls it back up in a `ledger` CTE that builds `events` with `jsonb_object_agg`, so `keitaro_stage_results.sales`/`revenue`/`pending_revenue` (0185) come out of one statement with the object and there is no second statement to drift from; `pending_revenue` travels the whole way with them. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-09-19 — this sentence originally read "are literally the SUM of the object's entries and the two cannot drift", and that was false.** They differ by the cross-org stray this same entry describes two sentences on; the identity that holds is `sales = Σ (is_purchase) n + strays`, which the suite pins with a deliberately non-zero residual (1 row / $70). See the 2026-09-19 review-fix entry at the top. Two CTEs because there is no `min(jsonb)` — the scalars group in one, the object builds in the other, and they join after grouping. The `event_types` join carries **`org_id` as well as the id** (CLAUDE.md §3), and it is a **LEFT** join on purpose: an unmapped row must still reach the statement, where it is counted into `unmapped_conversions` and FILTERed out of the object — stored, surfaced, counted as nothing. An all-zero entry is omitted, so a rejected-only day reads `'{}'` and not a row of zeros. The upsert's `WHERE` gained both columns, or a stage-day whose scalars did not move but whose BREAKDOWN did would freeze at its first value. ⭐ **The unmapped bucket keys on the JOIN RESULT (`et.key IS NULL`), never the raw column**: `purchasedClause` resolves `is_purchase` through a NON-org-scoped subquery while the join is org-scoped, so a ledger row carrying another org's `event_type_id` would otherwise land in `sales`/`revenue`, in no `events` entry, and in no badge — invisible everywhere. Keying on the join result makes the buckets a partition, asserted rather than assumed: the footing identity is `sales = Σ (is_purchase) n + strays` with `strays` computed independently and made **non-zero (1)** by a fixture, plus its money twin `revenue = Σ per-event revenue + strays` (**$70**). ⚠️ **The zeroing anti-join widened from "no row that makes a number" to "no row at all"** — every ledger row now makes a number, so the old four-filter list would have wiped the breakdown of any stage-day whose only conversions are registrations; the widening strictly reduces the rows it touches, so it can only preserve a value, never invent one (`SET` and the change test gained the two columns too). ⚠️ **`readProjectionCoverage`'s `ledger_behind_history` probe learned the two columns in the same commit** — without them a historical stage-day carrying only registrations, or only unmapped rows, could never trip the global refusal and the projection would zero it; moot on day one and asymmetric for ever after. The empty-ledger refusal and the per-stage coverage floor are untouched, and **all three refusals are red-proved against this version of the file**. [lib/sale-attribution.ts](../lib/sale-attribution.ts) gains `countedClause()` — the status half of `purchasedClause`, with the `is_purchase` half moved to the join — so the per-event aggregate carries no second, uncoupled copy of "counted". Inert for the UI: no screen renders either column. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-09-19 — "nothing selects the two columns" was false**: `/api/keitaro/results` selected them, because `db.select()` with no projection expands to the whole schema mirror. That route now names its columns. Verified: [scripts/test-stage-day-conversions.ts](../scripts/test-stage-day-conversions.ts) **108/0** on camman-v2 inside a rolled-back transaction (70 before) — eleven one-sided fixtures each on its OWN `stat_date` (registration-only, approved/pending/rejected purchase each ALONE, one deliberate mixed day, all three unmapped shapes, a CROSS-ORG `event_type_id`, and a THIRD event type that exists in no other database), the partition and footing bars, a flat independently-shaped recomputation of every scalar, `parseEventMap` against a REAL jsonb row, and the two `ledger_behind_history` bars run LAST in savepoints because that refusal is global; [scripts/test-ledger-predicates.ts](../scripts/test-ledger-predicates.ts) **17/0** (P16 pins `purchasedClause` ⊇ `countedClause`). **Seven red proofs, each restored byte-identical (`cmp` + md5)**: `countedClause` narrowed to `approved` (⇒ P16), the raw-column unmapped predicate (⇒ P18/P23b), the old zeroing anti-join (⇒ P1/P3/P6), an inner join (⇒ 12 bars), `counts_revenue` dropped from the per-event revenue filter with a $5 approved registration seeded (⇒ P14b), the per-stage floor removed (⇒ C1b/S9), and the empty-ledger test defeated (⇒ C1c/C1c3). Plan shape on camman-v2: `HashAggregate → Hash Left Join` with `event_types` on the build side, not a re-scan. `tsc --noEmit -p .` 0 with a control (a deliberate `string`/`number` swap IS caught), eslint clean on all four changed files, `check:guards` and `check:docs` green; `test-ledger-predicates-db` 16/0, `test-conversion-monitor-db` 58/0, `test-stage-event-columns-db` 18/0, `test-event-columns` 53/0 unchanged. No migration: 0185 leads this code and is already on camman-v2; production stays at 0181. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 04-features/keitaro-poll.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-18 - Phase 5 Task 2: **migration 0185** gives the stage-day projection somewhere to record per-event figures. `keitaro_stage_results` gains `events jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb` — the same numbers as `sales`/`revenue`/`pending_revenue` split per `event_types.key` (`{"purchase":{"n":12,"pending_n":1,"revenue":540.0000,"pending_revenue":60.0000}}`; `n` = `pending`+`approved`, `pending_n` ⊂ `n`, money for `counts_revenue` types only) — and `unmapped_conversions integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, the rows with no event type or no status, which count as nothing anywhere and are today visible only in a Telegram alert. **The MIGRATION is inert: two `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` with catalog-stored defaults, no rewrite, no index, nothing dropped, no reader and no writer** — the projection does not populate them until a later task. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-09-19 — "completely inert" was wrong about the SCHEMA MIRROR.** Naming the columns in `db/schema.ts` makes every projection-less `db.select()` over `keitaro_stage_results` expand to include them, so the CODE in this commit requires 0185 applied even though no feature reads either column; `/api/keitaro/results` would have answered 42703 on a database still at 0181. Fixed by giving that route an explicit projection. Keyed by `key` and never `id` (`event_types.id` is a global serial, the natural key is `(org_id, key)`, and the cross-org Telegram report needs a key that means the same thing in two orgs); `unmapped_conversions` is deliberately a scalar OUTSIDE the object so no future loop over it can sum an uncounted conversion into a total; no index because neither column is ever a predicate and a GIN index would cost writes on every 5-minute tick. ⭐ **The jsonb round trip is now measured against the REAL column instead of a hand-built object**, which is what Task 1's M5 promised to re-assert: one row carries `1234567.8901` in both places at once, and postgres-js returns a JS **number** for a `numeric(12,4)` inside `jsonb_build_object` (exact, and exact at `0.0001` too, with `jsonb` keeping the scale so `events::text` shows `0.0000`) while the **top-level** `pending_revenue` column on that same row returns the **string** `"1234567.8901"` — so `parseEventMap()` needs both branches and neither is dead (bars S13–S15; the "re-assert when the migration lands" notes in [lib/reporting/event-columns.ts](../lib/reporting/event-columns.ts) and [scripts/test-event-columns.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns.ts) are retired). ⚠️ **One bar in the brief would have passed for the wrong reason**: "no `keitaro_stage_results` row is NULL in either column" is vacuously true on camman-v2, which holds **0** rows of that table, so it would have gone green against a migration that omitted the `DEFAULT` entirely. S8/S8b instead BUILD the world-state — three rows in a `TEMP … ON COMMIT DROP` fixture, then the migration's **own two statements read off disk** replayed over them — and also prove the file re-runnable (a `when` bump can re-apply it on preview). Verified: [scripts/test-stage-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-stage-event-columns-db.ts) **18/0** on camman-v2 inside a transaction that always rolls back, with the catalog printed verbatim (`events jsonb NO '{}'::jsonb`, `unmapped_conversions integer NO 0`); the migration confirmed applied by querying `information_schema`, not by deploy status; **12 red proofs covering all 18 bars**, each restored byte-identical (`cmp` + md5) — including the silently-rounded cent (`numeric(12,2)` ⇒ S11/S14/S15 red), money stored as JSON strings (⇒ only S13 red, which is the bar that pins the driver's behaviour), the migration losing its `DEFAULT` (⇒ S8), losing `IF NOT EXISTS` (⇒ S8b), and the probe being allowed to COMMIT (⇒ S16/S17, whose row was then deleted from the preview DB by id). `tsc --noEmit -p .` 0, eslint clean on every changed file, `check:guards` green (the new script enrolled, 138 required), `check:docs` green, [scripts/test-event-columns.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns.ts) 53/0 unchanged; journal 186 entries, strictly ascending, no duplicate `idx`/`when`, gap 86400000, snapshot `prevId` chain OK. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 03-data-model.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-18 - Phase 5 Task 1 review fixes — [lib/reporting/event-columns.ts](../lib/reporting/event-columns.ts) only; still **inert, no migration, no behaviour change** for anything that ships. ⭐ **`EMPTY_TALLY` was a mutable shared singleton.** It is what `eventCellValue()` returns for a missing key, so the obvious consumer mistake — `acc[key] = EMPTY_TALLY` and then `addEventMaps(acc, …)` — mutated the one object process-wide: the review measured an unrelated missing-key count cell reading **5 instead of 0** while the whole 45-bar suite stayed green. It is now `Object.freeze`d and typed `Readonly<EventTally>`, with a new `emptyTally()` factory for a fresh mutable zero, and bars S1–S4 fail if anyone re-shares it (red-proved: with the freeze removed, S2 shows `EMPTY_TALLY={"n":5,…}` and S3 the reviewer's 5-instead-of-0, while E12 above them still passes). ⚠️ **The freeze is the load-bearing half, not the type**: TypeScript ignores `readonly` modifiers when checking assignability, so `Readonly<T>` assigns into a `Record<string, T>` with no error — measured, and now documented — and only the freeze catches the aliasing case. **The funnel cross product had no `s.key !== p.key` guard**: a row carrying both `is_purchase` and `is_retarget_signal` (nothing in 0181 forbids it) produced `evtfunnel:<k>:<k>`, a "X→X %" column that is `n/n = 1` by construction; the generator now skips the self-pair while its other pairings still generate (bars C9/C10/B5). **Tier B's premise is pinned by a bar instead of prose**: new **R1** in [scripts/test-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns-db.ts) goes red the moment a second `counts_revenue` type is configured in any org, with a message naming what to reconsider — `REVENUE_EVENT_TYPE_IDS`/`approvedRevenueClause` ([lib/sale-attribution.ts](../lib/sale-attribution.ts)) carry no per-type filter, so a second revenue type makes those hidden per-type columns the only decomposition of the on-screen aggregate. **Three docs corrected: `event_types.key` is NOT free text** — `event_types_key_format_check` (`0181:43`) constrains it to `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, so no legal key contains a `:`, the three-segment id split is exact, and the "a colon-bearing key fails closed" open item is withdrawn rather than carried forward; the parser's arity check stays, rejecting a malformed id from a URL or localStorage. Also: a funnel column with no `toKey` now returns `null` (unknown) instead of a confident `0` (E12b); the DB suite's L15 became a **before/after delta** instead of asserting `deposit` has zero rows *today* — a countdown that would have passed for the wrong reason (red-proved by writing the probe row outside the transaction: L14 still passed while L15 reported `before=0 after=1`, then cleaned up); **B1 was vacuous** (`[].every(…)` is `true`) and now asserts the exact 22-column shape as well as the round-trip; and the "numerics arrive as strings" comment was **wrong** — postgres-js `JSON.parse`s jsonb so numbers inside the json arrive as numbers, while a top-level `numeric` column arrives as a string, which is why neither parser branch is dead (M5 re-asserts both, with a note to re-measure against the real column when the migration adding `keitaro_stage_results.events` lands). Verified: [scripts/test-event-columns.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns.ts) **53/0** pure (was 45), [scripts/test-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns-db.ts) **16/0** on camman-v2 inside a transaction that always rolls back (was 15); **8 red proofs, every new or changed bar covered, each restored byte-identical (`cmp` + md5)**; `tsc --noEmit -p .` 0 with a control (a deliberate `string`/`number` swap in the module IS caught, so the file is genuinely in the program), eslint clean on all three files, `check:guards` and `check:docs` green. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-18 - Phase 5 Task 1: the `event_types` registry becomes a report column spec. New [lib/reporting/event-columns.ts](../lib/reporting/event-columns.ts) is the one place that knows what a column set made out of `event_types` looks like — everything later in Phase 5 consumes it rather than re-deriving it. **Completely inert: nothing imports it, no migration, no behaviour change.** It loads the registry (per org, or merged **by `key`** across orgs for the scheduled Telegram report, which has no session — `event_types.id` is a global serial while the natural key is `(org_id, key)`; lowest `display_order` wins and carries its label, flags are OR-ed, `archived` only when EVERY org archived it), orders it totally (retarget signals → everything else → purchases, `display_order` then `key` as tie-breaks — the deliberate divergence from 0181's seed, which puts `purchase=10` ahead of `registration=20` while the funnel reads registration → purchase), and generates the columns. Tier A, always visible, is the owner's specified list: a count, a rate and a held count per type plus the signal→purchase ratio; Tier B, behind the toggle, is ONLY the three per-event money columns, and only because each duplicates an aggregate column already on screen while exactly one `counts_revenue` type exists. ⭐ **Nothing branches on a key** — `is_retarget_signal` / `is_purchase` / `counts_revenue` / `display_order` decide everything, so adding an event type stays config. **An event type with zero conversions still gets its column and reads 0** (the spec comes from the registry, not the data); an archived type keeps its column only while displayed rows still carry a non-zero number for it, and the loader has no `status = 'active'` filter for the same reason archiving never erases revenue history. Ratios return `null` ("—") over a zero denominator and are deliberately NOT clamped at 100%: a registrant whose click was never scored human sits outside the `counted_clickers` denominator, and a purchase can arrive with no preceding registration. Headers are pluralised in the GENERATOR and only for the count column (0181 seeds labels singular, and the same label is what the campaign-activity badge renders for one conversion); the pluraliser is dumb and total because `event_types.label` is free text with no CHECK and no UI. Column ids `evt:<key>:<kind>` / `evtfunnel:<signal>:<purchase>` are the persisted sort key and are built from `key`, never `label`; `eventColumnById()` parses one back **through `buildEventColumns()`** so the grammar cannot fork. Verified: [scripts/test-event-columns.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns.ts) 45/0 pure (its registry holds a `deposit` type and a second signal that exist in no database, so a generator hard-coding the two seeded keys fails), [scripts/test-event-columns-db.ts](../scripts/test-event-columns-db.ts) 15/0 on camman-v2 inside a transaction that always rolls back (L15 asks the DB, outside the transaction, whether the probe row survived — L14 alone only proves this process threw); **54 mutations red-proved all 60 bars, every bar covered, every restore byte-identical (`cmp`/md5)** — the sweep also found a real gap the brief left, an `eventCellValue` `pending_n` case no bar asserted, now E13. `tsc --noEmit` 0 (control: a deliberate return-type change is caught, so the new files are genuinely in the program), eslint clean on all three files, `check:guards` green with the new DB script enrolled (red-proved by removing its `_require-preview-db` import), `check:docs` green. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-18 - `main` merged into `feat/conversion-events-p4` (Phase 3 readers + Phase 4 lanes). **No migration and no application behaviour change from the merge itself** — 0182/0183/0184 are the branch's own and are byte-unchanged; 0181 is untouched; the journal keeps all 185 entries with strictly ascending `when` (0183 stays at the deliberately-bumped 1792281600000, 0184 at 1792368000000). 27 conflicted files. **The preview-DB write guard existed on both sides and `main`'s copy won**: [scripts/_require-preview-db.ts](../scripts/_require-preview-db.ts) and [scripts/test-preview-db-guard.ts](../scripts/test-preview-db-guard.ts) are now byte-identical to `main` (the branch's variants differed only in prose and one refusal string; `main`'s bar additionally derives its population from a WRITE signal, carries a reasoned `EXCLUSIONS` list and proves five mutations red). Enrolment is the UNION and nothing was dropped: 127 scripts enrolled on `main` + 10 only on the branch (8 of them new Phase 3/4 suites) + `scripts/_conversion-fixture.ts`, which the merged bar flagged as an unguarded writer and which is now enrolled — **138 scripts import the guard, none twice**. `LEGACY_REF_LITERALS` is now EMPTY: the branch had already stripped the inline prod-ref literal from all 14 legacy scripts (their API-side halves kept, reading the ref from `requirePreviewDb()`), so check 1 covers every file in `scripts/` with no exemption. `resync-stage-day-conversions.ts` joins `EXCLUSIONS` with a reason — prod tooling behind `--apply` whose writes go through `lib/keitaro/stage-day-conversions`, so the source scan cannot see them. ⭐ **One real merge defect, caught and fixed:** both sides added one key to `CONVERSION_ALERT_KEYS` and each bumped `Object.keys(K).length` from 5 to 6, so the auto-merge kept SEVEN keys and a `=== 6` assertion — `scripts/test-conversion-monitor.ts` K1 would have gone red; it now asserts 7. [lib/conversions/monitor.ts](../lib/conversions/monitor.ts) keeps both sides whole: `main`'s `status_only_unmapped` family (key, prefix, `StatusOnlyCombo`, `STATUS_ONLY_COMBOS_SQL`, the predicate fragments) and the branch's `projection_failed` key with `ProjectionOutcome` / `decideProjectionAlert` / `evaluateProjectionAlert`; the fixed-key paragraph now names `projection_failed` AND the three `combo_cap_exceeded` keys. Docs keep both sets, newest first — `main`'s three 2026-09-18 entries (written 14:33–17:46) above the branch's fourteen (to 13:32), and `main`'s new conventions section above the branch's six. Verified: `check:guards` green (376 scanned, 177 write-capable, 44 exclusions, 136 required), `tsc --noEmit` 0 (unchanged from the pre-merge branch), eslint clean on every merge-touched file and unchanged (15 pre-existing problems, all in files byte-identical to the pre-merge branch), `check:docs` and `check:authz` green; on camman-v2 test-ledger-predicates-db 16, test-p3-task4-reader-switch-db 76, test-report-matviews-from-ledger-db 15, test-stage-day-conversions 70, test-segment-purchase-rules-db 34, test-registered-lane-db 14, test-registered-lane-tier-db 8, test-registered-lane-consumers 9, test-conversion-events-upsert 17, test-conversion-lookups 7, test-conversion-monitor-db 58 (was 48 on main + the branch's C1–C9); pure test-conversion-monitor 53 (was 44 branch / 45 main), test-conversion-ledger-rows 38, test-ledger-predicates 15, test-campaign-tier-scale 31, test-funnel-pending-exclusion 10, test-behavioral-split-lane-picker 33, test-campaign-tier 17, test-recipients-lanes 14 — every count equal to or above its pre-merge baseline, none red. — docs updated: CHANGELOG.md, 04-features/conversion-events.md, 04-features/keitaro-poll.md, 04-features/behavioral-lanes.md, 04-features/crons.md, 05-flows.md, 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-18 - Conversion ledger: a conversion FIRST seen through a status-only mapping now fails loudly instead of landing unclassified. A `conversion_event_mappings` row with `event_type_id` NULL is a **status-only rule** — "keep this conversion’s existing event type, just move its status" (PsychoBook’s seeded `rejected`). With no prior row there is no type to keep, so the conversion was stored with a status and `event_type_id` NULL — the unmapped shape, counted as nothing everywhere — and **no later postback heals it**, because the rule that classified it has no type to give. The batch counter `unmappedInBatch` tests `status === null`, so it missed the case; the table-level `unmapped` alert did see the row but reported it as an ordinary unmapped combo, whose advice ("add a mapping row for this Keitaro type") cannot fix it. **Zero such rows exist today** — the point is that the first one would have been invisible. Detection: `findStatusOnlyFirstSeen` ([lib/conversions/ingest.ts](../lib/conversions/ingest.ts)) returns, per batch, the rows a status-only rule resolved whose `keitaro_event_id` has no ledger row, read BEFORE the write so a dry run reports it too; `IngestResult` gains `statusOnlyFirstSeenInBatch` + `statusOnlyFirstSeenSamples`, which the poll response carries and the backfill prints per window, totals and exits 1 on (its `--apply` table check now counts the two shapes apart, and `verify-conversion-events.ts` gains V3b). Alert: a new Tier-2 combo family `conversion_events:status_only_unmapped:<offer>:<keitaro_type>` with its own `conversion_events:combo_cap_exceeded:status_only_unmapped` key, latched through the same `notifyOnTransition`/`clearAlert` path and the same per-problem-combo rules (one page per new combo, repeats silent, cleared when the combo is gone, 10 combos per kind by recency). Its page names the offer, the offer’s **network** (where the missing mapping row usually belongs) and the Keitaro type, says the rows count as nothing and that nothing will heal them, and gives the fix as a config row rather than code. The existing `unmapped` kind narrows to `status IS NULL`, so the two **partition** what `conversion_events_unmapped_idx` covers — disjoint (nothing pages twice) and total (nothing goes unreported) — and each half still implies that index’s predicate, so **no migration**. No mapping’s meaning changed: PsychoBook `rejected` stays status-only. Also fixed: the DB suite’s partial-index assertions were meaningless and then flaky against the EMPTY preview ledger (un-ANALYZEd, 25/25 misses for every predicate including main’s; ANALYZEd at ten rows the planner picked `conversion_events_offer_event_occurred_idx` or the partial index run to run) — they now build production’s shape in a rolled-back savepoint (2,000 healthy rows + `ANALYZE`) and report the index actually chosen. Also enrolled: `scripts/test-conversion-monitor-db.ts`, written on this branch before the preview-DB write guard landed on main, carried a hand-copied prod-ref literal and no `_require-preview-db` import — `npm run check:guards` caught it on the merge. It now imports the helper second and takes its banner from `requirePreviewDb()`, with the literal gone (the allowlist also refuses a raw IP or a pooler alias, which the literal did not). Verified: test-conversion-monitor 45 (pure, was 39), test-conversion-monitor-db 48 (camman-v2, rolled back, was 43), test-conversion-ledger-rows 38 / test-conversion-events-upsert 17 / test-conversion-lookups 7 re-run; four red proofs (status-only predicate reading the wrong half, the page dropping the network, the first-seen filter inverted, the unmapped predicate left un-narrowed) each turned the bars red and restored byte-identical; tsc; eslint on changed files. — docs updated: 04-features/conversion-events.md, 04-features/crons.md, 04-features/keitaro-poll.md, 05-flows.md (flow G), 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-18 - Preview-DB write guard: a test-fixture script ran against PRODUCTION and created live campaign rows before tearing them down (no damage). New [scripts/_require-preview-db.ts](../scripts/_require-preview-db.ts) is a side-effect import that refuses to run unless `DATABASE_URL` names an **allowlisted** preview database — not a denylist on the prod project ref, which 14 scripts had hand-copied and which passes a raw IP, a custom host, a pooler alias or a future prod ref; it also refuses an empty or missing `DATABASE_URL` (postgres() would silently fall back to the `PG*` variables) and exits 1 before any connection is opened. Imported **second** (after `_env-preload`) or **first** (ahead of a `dotenv` `config()` statement, which runs after all imports) so a module-scope query cannot outrun it. **126 write-capable scripts enrolled**; 43 deliberate exclusions each carry a one-line reason (prod-facing `apply-*`/`backfill-*` tooling, the conversion backfill + verify, the `verify-*-production` proofs, read-only diagnostics). New [scripts/test-preview-db-guard.ts](../scripts/test-preview-db-guard.ts) (`npm run check:guards`) derives the population from the source tree, so a new writing script is covered without editing a list; deliberately NOT wired into `vercel-build` (a source-scan false positive must not block a deploy). **No migration.** Verified: refuses a prod-shaped URL and a raw-IP URL (exit 1 in 1.9s vs an 8.1s CONNECT_TIMEOUT when the same URL is dialled directly — no connection is opened), refuses empty and missing `DATABASE_URL`, `db-cleanup.ts` with no `DATABASE_URL` now refuses instead of loading `.env.local`; 5 enrolled suites green on camman-v2 across both import positions (test-conversion-events-upsert 17, test-campaign-tier 11, test-lane-preview-count 10, test-resolve-key-for-stage, test-credential-phone-links); the bar goes red when an import is removed and green on byte-identical restore; tsc; eslint on changed files. — docs updated: 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-18 - Behavioural lane counts: the stages-list `N LIVE` number now previews the CAMPAIGN-WIDE source set while the split group is still `pending`. `campaign_stage_split_groups.source_stage_ids` is empty until the T-15 recompute (gated on `send_approved` + `scheduled_at`), and `alivenessKey()` fell back to `parent_stage_id` — counting only contacts who received the ANCHOR stage, routinely one half of an A/B split with its own creative and its own CTR. On campaign 1342 the confirm modal showed 39 clicked / 3 reached offer across all 4 completed stages while the list showed 13 / 1. The SEND was never affected (kickoff resolves the group first and refuses with `split_group_not_ready` rather than materializing the single-parent audience), but the narrow number showed exactly where the operator decides whether a lane is worth sending. [lane-counts/route.ts](../app/api/campaigns/%5BcampaignId%5D/stages/lane-counts/route.ts) now passes the live completed-stage set from the shared `resolveCompletedStages()` helper when a lane has a `split_group_id` and the group's set is empty (one query per request, not per lane). Precedence: resolved set → live completed set → `parent_stage_id`. Legacy pre-0174 lanes (`split_group_id IS NULL`, 560 live) keep the `parent_stage_id` fallback — their send really does use single-parent aliveness. **No migration.** Verified: new `scripts/test-lane-count-pending-fallback.ts` 7/7 against prod (lane 4457 13→39, lane 4458 1→3, both now equal to `previewSplitLanes`); verify-campaign-level-split 58; verify-lane-batch 25 campaigns identical; test-behavioral-split-lane-picker 26; tsc; eslint on changed files. — docs updated: 04-features/behavioral-lanes.md.

2026-09-18 - Conversion events ledger, Phase 4 hardening (review close-out): **the hand-copied production refusal becomes one allowlist helper, and something finally checks that scripts use it.** **No migration** (prod stays at 0180) and no application behaviour change — scripts, one shared helper and docs only; `FOLLOWUP_TIERS` (`[0,1,2]`), `FollowupTier`, `DEFAULT_LANE_TIERS` (`[1,2]`) and `DEFAULT_SELECTED_TIERS` are untouched. ⭐ **The refusal was a DENYLIST and three things were wrong with it.** It refused one spelling of production, so a raw IP, a custom host, a CNAME'd pooler alias or a future prod project with a new ref all ran; `DATABASE_URL=""` passed it (`""` contains no prod ref) and `postgres()` then falls back to the `PG*` variables; and it was a module-body STATEMENT, which runs only after every import has been evaluated — it worked purely because postgres-js connects lazily. New [`scripts/_require-preview-db.ts`](../scripts/_require-preview-db.ts) allowlists the target (camman-v2 only), refuses empty/unset `DATABASE_URL` by name, matches the ref in the connection string's **user or host** (not anywhere in the string, so a ref in a password is not a target), never echoes the URL, and is imported **for its side effect, second** — after `./_env-preload`, before any app module — so the process exits before `db/client` is required. **36 scripts now import it** (the review said 13; the byte-identical five-line copy was in fact in 21 files, plus 15 more in four other shapes — two banner variants, seven in-function `ref === PROD_REF` checks, five `!includes(PREVIEW_REF)` allowlists and two that also check `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, whose API-side half is kept and now reads the ref from the helper). Identity was confirmed by hashing each block before replacing, not assumed. **Proofs:** fires on a production-shaped URL (exit 1, refusal printed, `db/client` never reached); fires on the raw-IP URL the old denylist explicitly let through (demonstrated side by side — `includes(PROD_REF) => false => old guard would RUN`); fires on `DATABASE_URL=""` and on unset; does NOT fire against `.env.demo`, where a module-scope query in the probe connected and returned `postgres`. ⭐ **New bar [`scripts/test-preview-db-guard.ts`](../scripts/test-preview-db-guard.ts) (`npm run check:guards`, no database)** asserts the idiom: no 20-letter project-ref literal outside the helper, every preview-only DB script importing it, and that import ahead of every import that can open a connection. Its population is DERIVED (touches a database AND declares a preview target) so a new fixture script is enrolled automatically; it carries four in-memory can-go-red controls and was additionally proved red on disk by stripping the import from `test-lane-send.ts` (FAIL, exit 1), restored `cmp`/md5-identical. **Its limit is documented rather than implied:** a new writer that neither imports the helper nor mentions `.env.demo` is invisible to it — deliberate, because ~150 scripts here are read-only diagnostics that are SUPPOSED to hit production. The import-ordering check is what closes the module-scope-query hazard: with the guard ahead of every app module, a module-scope query in the script or in anything it imports cannot outrun it (and top-level `await` does not compile under tsx's CJS output at all). **Three review findings in [`verify-campaign-level-split.ts`](../scripts/verify-campaign-level-split.ts):** (a) the `PINNED_TIERS` bar compared the split's output against the constant the split was CALLED with, so both sides moved together — MEASURED: widening the pin to `[0,1,2,3]` left that check GREEN (`split created EXACTLY the pinned lanes {0,1,2,3}`); a new bar anchored to the literal `[0,1,2]` goes RED, and the file was restored byte-identically; (b) the `createdTiers` query gained the missing `org_id` filter (same rows before and after — the suite is 55/0/1 either way); (c) the `expectedFor`/`expectLane` throws are unreachable today and now PINNED in `test-lane-send.ts` and `test-lane-preview-count.ts` by calling them with an unmapped tier and requiring the throw. **Two doc claims that would have rotted:** "zero registration rows in production" is now dated (**as recorded 2026-09-18**) and marked a measurement rather than a standing fact, in both the doc and the script comment, with a pointer to the run's printed counts; and Phase 5's per-event columns are re-worded from "registrations **will** get their own" / "**will be** the fourth consumer" to *proposed, not built, not ratified*. The send-time re-check line now says **out of scope** with "no follow-up card for it is known to exist" instead of asserting a card. Two new conventions in `07-conventions.md`: **allowlist the target, never denylist production**, and **a bar whose two sides move together cannot fail — anchor one to a literal**. Checks: `tsc --noEmit -p .` 0; eslint 0 errors on all 39 touched files (one pre-existing unused-param warning in `test-drip-routing-schema.ts`, untouched code); `check:docs` 0; `check:guards` green (36 enrolled); suites re-run on camman-v2 — `test-registered-lane-tier-db` 8/0, `test-conversion-events-upsert` 17/0, `test-p3-task4-reader-switch-db` 76/0, `test-lane-preview-count` 13/0, `test-lane-send` 29/0, `verify-campaign-level-split` 55/0/1, each printing `Target DB: camman-v2 (preview)`. ⚠️ `check:guards` is NOT wired into `vercel-build`: a source-scan false positive would block a production deploy, so it stays a hand/CI-run bar for now. — docs updated: docs/04-features/conversion-events.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-18 - Conversion events ledger, Phase 4 Task 6: **the docs describe the five-value tier scale, and two script-safety holes are closed.** **No migration** (prod stays at 0180) and no application behaviour change — `FOLLOWUP_TIERS` (`[0,1,2]`), `FollowupTier`, `DEFAULT_LANE_TIERS` (`[1,2]`) and `DEFAULT_SELECTED_TIERS` are untouched, so nobody is messaged differently. **Docs:** `03-data-model.md`'s `campaign_stages` row now spells the lane scale out and drops the stale "the app does not create one yet" (Task 3 made Registered tickable); `05-flows.md`'s D2 diagram inserts **the selected** lanes, not a fixed trio, with a note that tier 4 is refused by both the validator and the CHECK and that a registrant with `Registered` unticked lands in **no lane at all** (ticking it is what gives them a message, not what removes them from the Reached-offer one); `conversion-events.md` gains the three real consumers of `registeredClause()` — `campaignTierExpr`'s tier-3 branch and **both** inline copies in `drip/lifecycle.ts` — all behavioural, none reporting, and drops the now-built "Phase 4" line from *Not built yet*; `drip-campaigns-routing.md` records that **no Registered drip follow-up exists** and why the `ELSE NULL` ladder is right, that a registrant's journey now completes, and that a journey closed at tier 3 silently loses its remaining follow-ups; `operator-api.md` widens the documented `behavioral_tier` contract to `{0,1,2,3,null}` (additive; never 4); `REPORTS-ROLLUP-RECON.md:66` adds `3=registered`. ⭐ **A new convention: a behavioural tier is INSERTED at its rank, never appended** (`07-conventions.md`) — `MAX(tier)` makes the number the precedence, so appending Registered as 4 would have ranked a $0 registration above a sale and re-messaged a buyer; it is cheap only because the tier is computed and never stored (0184 widened the CHECK with **no** backfill: 0 rows at tier 3 across `campaign_stages` 2,100 / `report_stage_hour` 514 / `report_group_hour` 3,431). The now-backwards history line ("read as a BUYER outright — tier 3") is reworded to keep BOTH facts rather than renumbered, which would have falsified the history it records. `markLaneSkippedEmpty` gains the missing-`org_id` reasoning **at the function**, not only in its sibling's comment. **Safety 1:** six more scripts gained the branch's production-ref refusal — `test-lane-sibling-exclusion`, `verify-lane-batch`, `test-split-manual-prepare`, `test-stage-copy-invariants`, `test-drip-lifecycle`, `test-drip-followup-timing` — each PROVED to fire (exit 1, refusal printed, no connection attempted) against a fake `DATABASE_URL` carrying the prod ref, and proved NOT to fire against `.env.demo`. Four of them seed fixtures outside a transaction; `verify-lane-batch` is SELECT-only but fans an unbounded audience scan per lane across every campaign, and `test-drip-followup-timing` is pure today (the refusal is defence-in-depth, and its comment says so). `test-campaign-tier-scale.ts` correctly has none — no `_env-preload`, no `db/client`, no `.execute`, and it runs 31/0 with `DATABASE_URL` unset entirely. ⭐ **Safety 2: `verify-purchase-rule-definition.ts`'s refusal was in the wrong place and is REVERTED.** That script is *designed* to read production — A/C/E compare the ledger against the legacy columns on real accumulated conversions and are trivially `0 == 0` against an empty database — so refusing prod refused its only purpose. The guard moved onto the write side and onto the server: `main()` now runs inside ONE transaction opened with `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, every helper takes that executor (no `db` default, so omitting it is a type error), and section **B** was rewritten from "synthesize a rejected conversion, roll back" into a live proof of the guard — it issues `UPDATE … WHERE false` inside a savepoint and requires SQLSTATE **25006**. The probe is harmless if the guard is gone (no row matches) and fires on an empty database (Postgres refuses at executor start, before any row), so it is not an assertion about today's data; red-proved by deleting the READ ONLY line (**"the UPDATE was ACCEPTED — the READ ONLY transaction guard is gone"**) and restored md5-identical. The "rejected is not a purchase" predicate B used to prove stays pinned on fixtures by `test-ledger-predicates-db.ts` D2/D5. Checks: `verify-purchase-rule-definition` 4 passed / 2 failed on camman-v2 — B green, and C ("strictly more buyers") + D ("a campaign with conversions exists") red for the PRE-EXISTING environmental reason its default org holds 0 `stage_sends` and 0 conversions; `test-campaign-tier-scale` 31/0; `test-drip-followup-timing` all green; tsc 0 on every touched file; `check:docs` 0. — docs updated: docs/03-data-model.md, docs/04-features/conversion-events.md, docs/04-features/drip-campaigns-routing.md, docs/05-flows.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/operator-api.md, REPORTS-ROLLUP-RECON.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-18 - Conversion events ledger, Phase 4 Task 5: **the pre-existing lane suite stops encoding "3 = converted".** **No migration** (prod stays at 0180), no application behaviour change — seven verification scripts only. Lane loops now iterate `LANE_TIER_VALUES` and "converted" assertions name `EXIT_TIER`/`TIER_REGISTERED` instead of the digit, so the bars track the scale rather than a literal. ⭐ **Three of the five scripts the renumber should have broken were GREEN for the wrong reason** — `converted (tier 3) contact in NO lane (0/1/2)` still passes when the contact is tier 4, because a tier-4 contact is also in no lane 0/1/2. New one-sided registration fixtures (a `registration` ledger row with `sale_status` left NULL) make the Registered lane actually exercised in [`scripts/test-campaign-tier.ts`](../scripts/test-campaign-tier.ts) (17/0), [`test-recipients-lanes.ts`](../scripts/test-recipients-lanes.ts) (14/0), [`test-lane-send.ts`](../scripts/test-lane-send.ts) (28/0), [`test-lane-preview-count.ts`](../scripts/test-lane-preview-count.ts) (12/0) and [`verify-campaign-level-split.ts`](../scripts/verify-campaign-level-split.ts) (54/0/1). Two silent tier-indexed lookups are gone: `test-lane-send.ts`'s `expected` table and `verify-campaign-level-split.ts`'s `tier === 0 ? L0 : tier === 1 ? L1 : L2` ladder, whose `else` claimed the Reached-offer set for **any** other tier — left alone, a tier-3 lane would have been compared against the tier-2 set and **passed for the wrong reason**. Both now go through a total lookup that THROWS on an unmapped tier. `test-campaign-tier.ts`'s `REGISTRATION … → 0, not 3` control was split rather than renumbered: its fixture is two-sided (ledger registration + legacy `'lead'` row), so once the expectation left 0 it could no longer tell the tier-3 branch from a `sale_status` reader — the one-sided `legacy_only` bar now carries that half. `verify-purchase-rule-definition.ts`'s section D asserts `EXIT_TIER` via `tierLiteral`. **Safety:** all seven scripts, plus `test-behavioral-split-lane-picker.ts`, gained the branch's production-ref refusal — `.env.local` is PROD and `_env-preload`/`dotenv` load it whenever `DATABASE_URL` is unset, and `test-campaign-tier.ts` writes fixtures **outside** a transaction. ⭐ **`markLaneSkippedEmpty` was reviewed for the missing-`org_id` shape its sibling `notifyLaneSkippedEmpty` was given, and deliberately NOT changed** — see docs/07-conventions.md: the caller only has `campaigns.org_id`, nothing enforces it equals `campaign_stages.org_id`, and a zero-row UPDATE there strands the lane's group and holds its siblings (the 2026-09-05 freeze). The sibling's justifying comment, which claimed the org_id came from the same `campaign_stages` row, is corrected. `FOLLOWUP_TIERS`, `FollowupTier`, `DEFAULT_LANE_TIERS` and `DEFAULT_SELECTED_TIERS` are untouched — nothing here changes who receives a message. Checks: 13 scripts run on camman-v2, all green except `verify-purchase-rule-definition` (4/3), whose three reds are PRE-EXISTING and environmental — its default org has zero rows in the preview DB — identical to the pre-change baseline. Eight red proofs, each a wrong RESULT then restored `cmp`/md5-clean. — docs updated: docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-18 - Conversion events Phase 4, Tasks 3+4 REVIEW FIXES: **two unguarded guards closed, and the follow-up hole closed semantically.** **No migration** (prod stays at 0180). ⭐ **A guard whose deletion is invisible is not a guard.** `AND pe.status IS NOT NULL` in BOTH inline copies in [`lib/drip/lifecycle.ts`](../lib/drip/lifecycle.ts) is what stops an UNMAPPED purchase row from evicting a registrant — and deleting it from both left all 35 bars GREEN, because no fixture carried a purchase row of any status at all. [`scripts/test-drip-lifecycle.ts`](../scripts/test-drip-lifecycle.ts) gains, PER COPY, a registrant who also carries a purchase-type row with `status` NULL; [`scripts/_conversion-fixture.ts`](../scripts/_conversion-fixture.ts) learns an explicit `status: null` for that shape (mapped TYPE, unmapped STATUS — not an omitted `eventKey`, whose NULL `event_type_id` is already harmless because `NULL IN (…)` is NULL). ⭐ **Nothing coupled the two copies to [`lib/campaign-tier.ts`](../lib/campaign-tier.ts)** — `docs/07-conventions.md` prescribed a manual grep, the weakest form of the very guard it argues for two paragraphs earlier, and that drift IS the bug Phase 4 fixed. `P20`–`P26` in [`scripts/test-campaign-tier-scale.ts`](../scripts/test-campaign-tier-scale.ts) (PURE, no DB) extract the tier-3 rule (registration test + purchase-eviction `NOT EXISTS`) from the shared fragment by balanced parens — comments stripped, whitespace collapsed so CRLF and LF both match — and assert it **byte-identical** in both copies, that there are **exactly two** copies (a third added later would be unguarded by construction), that each copy's UNION arms emit `TIER_REGISTERED` / `TIER_PURCHASED` (bare `SELECT 3` / `SELECT 4` literals no type checker sees), and that both ledger arms stay org-scoped.
