The SCOPING is excluded from the comparison by construction — literal ids vs. correlated columns is the whole reason the copy exists. ⭐ **The stage PATCH hole is closed by TIER, not by `NON_UPDATABLE`.** New [`lib/api/followup-tier-guard.ts`](../lib/api/followup-tier-guard.ts): a patch setting `drip_followup_minutes` (non-null) or `drip_active: true` on a stage whose stored `behavioral_tier` is outside `FOLLOWUP_TIERS` is refused **400** + `details.reason = "followup_tier_unsupported"`. Adding the field to `NON_UPDATABLE` — the previous entry's own suggestion — would have been a **SILENT FAILURE**: that route drops `NON_UPDATABLE` keys without an error, so the live follow-up timer `<Select>` in `drip-followup-children.tsx` would have returned 200, toasted "Follow-up updated", reloaded and shown the old value, freezing every drip child at its default for ever. Three carve-outs, each asserted: `behavioral_tier` NULL is **not a lane** and stays allowed (the drip FIRST-SEND stage is NULL with `drip_active: true` — refusing it breaks drip itself), clearing a timer (`null`) is always allowed, and `drip_active: false` is always allowed, or a stage armed before the guard existed could never be disarmed. One bar reads the route's source and asserts it actually CALLS the guard. **Task 3 minors:** the lane picker's "not a hard-coded string" bar now compares against `LANE_TIERS` (the literal `includes("0, 1, 2, 3")` form was green against a hard-coded message AND against a grown registry, so it could not fail for the reason its label claimed); the `LANE_TIERS` ↔ `LANE_TIER_VALUES` coupling bar MOVED from the DB-requiring script to the pure suite (`P17`–`P19`), where `split-group.ts`'s own comment says this class of guard belongs; `LANE_TIER_VALUES` is now anchored to migration 0184's CHECK itself by parsing `pg_get_constraintdef` (`C1`/`C2` in [`scripts/test-registered-lane-tier-db.ts`](../scripts/test-registered-lane-tier-db.ts)) — before it, adding a tier to both TS lists passed every static bar and failed only at INSERT time; the fourth hard-coded `[0,1,2]` derives from what its own case asked for; `page.tsx`'s lane-count tooltip says **Purchased** instead of "converted exit" (user-visible), `DEFAULT_SELECTED_TIERS` is `readonly` + `Object.freeze`d + copied at both use sites (it was one array shared by reference between `useState` and the reset), and the whitespace comment no longer asserts a cause that was never isolated — what was established is the DOM symptom in a DEV build; the `{" "}` fix is transform-independent and stays. Two PRE-EXISTING `org_id` gaps closed, both provably result-preserving: `behavioral-split.ts`'s tracking-id UPDATE (every row is one the same transaction just inserted with that `org_id`) and `notifyLaneSkippedEmpty`'s SELECT + its `campaigns` join (its only caller reads `org_id` from the same `campaign_stages` row as `stage_id`). **Task 4 minors — documented, not redesigned:** ⭐ **tier 3 is the ONE NON-MONOTONIC value on the scale** — a rejected purchase arriving after a registration REVOKES tier 3 and drops that contact back to their click/offer tier, so a drip journey already closed at 3 silently loses its remaining 0/1/2 follow-ups; the same final ledger state yields two outcomes depending on postback order vs. sweep timing, and the "high-water" wording is now qualified where the expression is defined; the sweeper's converted-vs-completed race is **wider** since tier 4 (a buyer with unsent children can now close as `completed`) and is written down at [`lib/drip/lifecycle-sweep.ts`](../lib/drip/lifecycle-sweep.ts) — it costs a funnel bucket, never a contact; the `expireJourneysPastEndDate` twin's coverage is now symmetric with its sibling (the `end_at` gate in all three states — NULL, future, past — plus the every-child-sent and high-water-clicker twins, the `end_at IS NULL` branch having been wholly unasserted); the drip funnel docs now say why there is **no Registered step** (only `converted` moved, `>= 3` → `>= 4`; `reached offer` keeps counting registrants because they did reach it); and the `ce2` alias rationale in the entry below is corrected — sibling `UNION ALL` arms do not share scope, so a bare `ce` would have been an unresolved reference, not a silent mis-bind. Checks: `test-drip-lifecycle.ts` **63/0**, `test-campaign-tier-scale.ts` **31/0**, `test-registered-lane-tier-db.ts` **8/0**, `test-behavioral-split.ts` **27/0**, `test-behavioral-split-lane-picker.ts` **33/0**, `test-drip-followup-timing.ts` **27/0**, `test-drip-unengaged-close.ts` green; `tsc --noEmit` 0; eslint 0 new on every changed file; `check:docs` 0. **Eight mutation red proofs, every one a WRONG RESULT rather than a crash:** delete the status filter from BOTH copies (4 DB bars, 2 per copy); from copy 1 ONLY (`P22.1` alone, and the completion fixture alone); from `campaign-tier.ts` (`P10b` + `P22.1` + `P22.2`); renumber copy 1's tier-3 arm to 2 (`P24.1`); widen `LANE_TIER_VALUES` past the CHECK (`C1`, `check=[0,1,2,3]` vs `[0,1,2,3,4]`); make the tier guard permissive (both refusal bars AND the NULL carve-out, so it is not vacuous); de-wire its call site (the route-wiring bar); hard-code the refusal message behind a grown registry (the picker bar — which the OLD form left green). Every mutated file restored from a byte copy, `cmp`-silent and md5-identical. ⚠️ `FOLLOWUP_TIERS` `[0, 1, 2]`, `FollowupTier` `0 | 1 | 2`, `DEFAULT_LANE_TIERS` `[1, 2]` and the client's `DEFAULT_SELECTED_TIERS` `[1, 2]` are all UNCHANGED — no Registered follow-up exists and none was added. — docs updated: 04-features/behavioral-lanes.md, 04-features/drip-partner-reporting.md, 04-features/drip-campaigns-routing.md, 07-conventions.md, CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-18 - Conversion events ledger, Phase 4 Task 2: the behavioural tier scale is RENUMBERED — Registered is tier 3 and the purchased EXIT moves to 4. **No migration** (0184 already admits a stored 3; prod stays at 0180). [`lib/campaign-tier.ts`](../lib/campaign-tier.ts) gains named constants (`TIER_IGNORED`..`TIER_PURCHASED`, `EXIT_TIER`, `LANE_TIER_VALUES`) and `tierLiteral()` — the only way a tier number reaches SQL — plus TWO ledger branches: tier 4 for a counted purchase (`purchasedClause()`) and tier 3 for a counted registration (`registeredClause()`) carrying a `NOT EXISTS` over purchase-type events at any KNOWN status, so a REJECTED purchase does not return a contact to Registered while an UNMAPPED (NULL-status) row — which counts as nothing everywhere else — does not evict them either. 2026-09-18 - Conversion events ledger, Phase 3 Task 6 (review fixes): the stage-day projection lost its IDEMPOTENCE, and the invariant the task exists to create had no guard. **No migration** (prod stays at 0180). (1) **CRITICAL** — [`lib/keitaro/stage-day-conversions.ts`](../lib/keitaro/stage-day-conversions.ts)'s zeroing UPDATE tested `SALES ∨ REVENUE ∨ PENDING` for "does the ledger still explain this row?", which silently relied on the OLD `SALES_FILTER` (`keitaro_type IN ('lead','sale','rejected')`) being a strict SUPERSET of `CHECKOUT_FILTER` (`keitaro_type = 'lead'`). Task 6's flip to `purchasedClause()` broke that containment: a stage-day whose only ledger rows are lead-TYPE NON-purchases — a $0 registration posted as `lead`, or an UNMAPPED row — had `checkouts` written by the INSERT and zeroed by the UPDATE in the SAME run, then rewritten and re-zeroed on every `*/5` tick forever, mirroring the flap into `campaign_stages.checkout_click_count`. The test is now `SALES ∨ CHECKOUT ∨ REVENUE ∨ PENDING` — the exact set the INSERT writes from, so the UPDATE is the complement of the INSERT. Zero impact today; it fires the day a registration lands, which is the point of the card. New PB1–PB4 in [`scripts/test-stage-day-conversions.ts`](../scripts/test-stage-day-conversions.ts) run the projection TWICE over a registration-only fixture and require run 1 == run 2, both non-zero, second run writing 0 / zeroing 0, with `checkout_click_count` holding — **proven RED against the pre-fix filter** (4 failed; `rowsWritten: 1, rowsZeroed: 1` on every run and `checkouts` 0 instead of 2). (2) **I1** — new PURE guard [`scripts/test-funnel-pending-exclusion.ts`](../scripts/test-funnel-pending-exclusion.ts): pending money is carried and never spent. A pending-only tally must produce the same `epc`, `sales_cr` and `profit` as one with no money at all, asserted as a whole-object diff so a metric added later cannot quietly start spending it, plus the two accumulators (`addRowToFunnel`, `mergeFunnel`) keeping the sums apart and an anchor proving the same $500 APPROVED does move EPC and profit. 10/0, **red-proved** against a `funnel.ts` mutated to fold pending into all three (6 failed), restored `cmp`-identical. Until now the invariant was a comment. (3) **I2** — two more prod diagnostics silently needed 0182 (`getStageMetricsInRange` selects `pending_revenue` unconditionally): [`scripts/verify-clickers-fallback.ts`](../scripts/verify-clickers-fallback.ts) and [`scripts/verify-epc-denominator.ts`](../scripts/verify-epc-denominator.ts) now fail with a named precondition instead of a raw `42703` — new shared [`scripts/_require-migration.ts`](../scripts/_require-migration.ts) (`requirePendingRevenueColumn`), which also guards `test-stage-funnel.ts`. The complete list of affected prod-facing scripts is in Task 8's precondition block, together with the sharpened blast radius: **0182 must be applied before this branch deploys, or `/reports`, the performance report and the campaign stages API return 42703 for EVERY org — ledger or not**, not merely "ledger screens read empty". Documented deliberate trades: a coverage GAP inside the covered range is still reducible (the backfill's own verify owns that, not the projection), a stage whose ledger rows were all deleted is never corrected, and the Checkout Clicks mirror overwrites a hand-entered value within 5 minutes on any in-scope stage (0 when the stage has no ledger conversions; the resync `--apply` does it for every stage with any row). 2026-09-17 - Conversion events ledger, Phase 2: kept live + Tier-2 alerts. **No migration** (uses 0181, `alert_state` 0154, `cron_locks` 0103). `/api/keitaro/poll` now ingests the last 7 ET days of Keitaro conversions into `conversion_events` after the aggregate poll and the counted-clicker refresh: own try/catch, response adds `conversion_events` / `conversion_events_error`, and `keitaro_stage_results`, `stage_sends` and every reader are unchanged. On the cron path, seven latched Telegram alerts: on fixed keys, `conversion_events:fetch_failed` (a refused — HTTP error, timeout, malformed or truncated page — or thrown ingest, debounced: fires only when the last complete ingest is over 15 min old or never recorded, so one transient failure never pages; the age reads `N min ago` / `X.Y h ago`), `:invalid_rows`, `:org_mismatch` (rows resolved to another org and not written; no debounce); on one key per problem combo, `conversion_events:unmapped:<offer>:<keitaro_type>` (all-time) and `conversion_events:type_conflicts:<offer>:<locked_key>><conflicting_key>`, evaluated on every tick, failed ones included. Each new combo pages once, including a conflict or an unmapped type that an UPDATE puts on an existing row. Repeats of the same combo don't re-page, a combo key clears when its combo disappears, and at most 10 combos per kind are listed and paged — the most recently changed, so a brand-new small combo pages even against ten bigger ones. A combo key part that needed sanitising carries a `~<6 hex of sha256>` suffix, so types that sanitise alike keep separate alerts. Past the cap, the fixed keys `conversion_events:combo_cap_exceeded:unmapped` / `:type_conflicts` page once (and clear at 10 or fewer), while no combo key of that kind clears. Then the `conversion-events-ingest` heartbeat, stamped last and only for a complete window; `/api/cron/tracking-monitors` pages on `heartbeat:conversion-events-ingest` when it is over 1h old. Unresolved conversions and `statusOnlyInBatch` are counted, not alerted (a brand-new status-only row is already a table-level unmapped row). New [lib/conversions/monitor.ts](../lib/conversions/monitor.ts); `liveIngestRange` (DST-safe calendar window) in [lib/conversions/keitaro-row.ts](../lib/conversions/keitaro-row.ts). `app/api/keitaro/poll/route.ts`'s `maxDuration` is now 230 (was 300), below the 240s cron lease (`CRON_LEASE_MS`) so the route dies before its own lease expires. Checks: test-conversion-monitor 39 (pure), test-conversion-monitor-db 43 (camman-v2, rolled back), Phase 1's test-conversion-ledger-rows 38 / test-conversion-events-upsert 17 / test-conversion-lookups 7 re-run; tsc; eslint on changed files. — docs updated: 04-features/conversion-events.md, 04-features/keitaro-poll.md, 04-features/crons.md, 05-flows.md (flow G), plan.

2026-09-17 - Conversion events ledger, Phase 1 (migration 0181, additive; nothing reads it yet). 0181 starts with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` (Drizzle runs all pending migrations in one transaction; a blocked lock fails instead of queueing the drain) and takes the `offers` column + index before any FK lock. New `event_types` (seeded purchase + registration), `conversion_event_mappings` (Keitaro conversion type → event + pending/approved/rejected, per network or offer; seeded for Property Leads, Sweeply, Secco, PsychoBook; a mistyped event key seeds nothing), `conversion_events` (one row per Keitaro conversion, keyed on event_id, original-time `occurred_at`, locked event type with a recorded `conflicting_event_type_id` when a later type maps elsewhere; `org_id` fixed at insert — a conversion later resolved in a different org is skipped and counted as `orgMismatch`, never merged), `offers.keitaro_offer_id`. New `lib/conversions/` (fetch all conversion types — a truncated or malformed page is refused; pure parse/attribute/map; idempotent upsert), `scripts/backfill-conversion-events.ts` (dry-run default; per-window unmapped/status-only/org-mismatch counts; the table-level unmapped/conflict check runs with `--apply` only), `scripts/verify-conversion-events.ts` (ledger vs live Keitaro, live pull deduped by event_id; prints the accepted +$715 / 26 conv $1,463 / −$100 deltas). Checks: test-conversion-ledger-rows 38, test-conversion-events-upsert 17, test-conversion-lookups 7 (camman-v2, rolled back). — docs updated: 04-features/conversion-events.md (new), 03-data-model.md (+ERD), 06-integrations.md, 07-conventions.md, spec + plan.

2026-09-15 - Creatives list: Sales CR fixed + "Sales, qty (all time)" column. Sales CR read 0.0% on every creative: [lib/creatives/metrics-cache.ts](../lib/creatives/metrics-cache.ts) summed `campaign_stages.sales_count`, which is only the operator's manual tally (the Keitaro poll never writes it) — 0 in the last 30 days against 549 Keitaro conversions across 111 creatives. A stage's sales are now `max(sales_count, Σ keitaro_stage_results.sales)`, the `combineSales` rule the campaign page and reports use. New sortable column "Sales, qty (all time)" (`metrics.sales_lifetime`, `sortBy=sales_lifetime`). In the same query: Keitaro totals are aggregated once per stage and joined instead of a correlated subquery per stage (stage part 1,535 ms → 17 ms on prod), and the final SELECT is driven by the lifetime aggregates — it was driven by the 30-day ones, so the 60 of 405 creatives idle for 30+ days had no row and their "Clicks (all time)" / "EPC (all time)" read 0 / "—" (1,873 counted clickers, $452). **No migration.** Verified: `scripts/verify-creatives-sales.ts` on a cold cache against prod data (475/475 creatives exact vs a raw-row recount for 30-day sales, all-time sales, 30-day and all-time revenue; `sales_cr` = sales ÷ clean clicks; 111 creatives with Sales CR > 0, 196 with all-time sales, max 175; sort asc/desc + id tiebreak); tsc; eslint on changed files (no new problems); check:docs; UI on local dev (Sales CR filled with truthful tooltips, column placement, header sort). — docs updated: 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives.md, operator-api.md, 07-conventions.md, spec + plan.

2026-09-15 - Creatives list "Used Campaigns" column. `/creatives` shows, per creative, the number of distinct campaigns with a **sent** stage using it, all time — the same rule as `GET /api/creatives/[id]/usage` (archived campaigns count, drafts don't; a stage that fired and then had every message cancelled still counts: 19 campaigns across 17 creatives today). `/api/creatives/list` returns `used_campaigns` on every row regardless of `include_metrics` — a correlated subquery on `campaign_stages_creative_id_idx`, ~3 ms org-wide, no cache, **no migration** — sortable via `sortBy=used_campaigns` with the `id` tiebreaker. Caught before shipping: Drizzle rendered `${creatives.id}` unqualified in the single-table SELECT list, so the subquery bound to `campaign_stages.id` and returned 0 for every row on the `include_metrics=false` path (new 07-conventions section). Verified: `scripts/verify-creatives-used-campaigns.ts` 15/15 on local dev against prod data (475/475 exact vs an independent count on both query paths; most-used creative #126 = 80 on the list, the usage endpoint and the independent count; asc/desc order + id tiebreak; no page overlap), re-run after rebasing onto #186; tsc; eslint on changed files (no new problems); check:docs; UI (column placement, tooltip, header sort asc/desc). — docs updated: 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives.md, operator-api.md, 07-conventions.md, spec + plan.

2026-09-14 - Audience Stats report (ClickUp 869eydqn0). New `/reports/audience` tab (sidebar Reports → Audience Stats): pick a contact group, one row per offer ever sent to it — **archived offers included** — with the offer report's metrics, plus the org benchmark and a "This group · all offers" total. Offer rows are the existing `offer_group_report_mv` cells. Migration **0180** (applied to prod, integrity OK) adds `audience_report_group_totals_mv`: additive columns summed from the cells; clicks and opt-outs `COUNT(DISTINCT)` at group grain (summing cells overcounted clickers 25–50%); opt-out recipient via `opt_outs.contact_id` (0.57s vs 36.8s through `stage_sends`); revoked from anon/authenticated; refreshed last by the existing twice-daily cron (`audienceTotalsMs`). `GET /api/reports/audience[?group_id]` (campaigns.view; route map GET + token GET, 40 token pairs / 277 routes). `readOrgBenchmark` / `readGroupReportRefreshedAt` shared with the offer report; the offer report page's pure helpers moved to `components/reports/report-metrics.tsx`. Verified: `scripts/verify-audience-report.ts` on prod 8/8 (sums exact for 16 groups; shipped definition within [largest cell, sum of cells] in one REPEATABLE READ snapshot, dedup active in 11 groups; 0 of 124,725 recipient mismatches; helper = matview for 138 rows incl. 16 archived; org scoping; grants); tsc; eslint on changed files (no new problems); check:authz; check:docs; next build; preview DB applied 0180; UI against prod data (Memory: 17 offers / 3 archived / totals = matview; picker switch + `?group=` reload; API 200/400/400/404; offer report unchanged). — docs updated: 04-features/audience-report.md (new), 03-data-model.md, 04-features/crons.md, 04-features/offer-group-report.md, 07-conventions.md, security-notes.md, spec + plan.

2026-09-14 - Creative CTR fixed (ClickUp 869evxq3f). CTR divided by `campaign_stages.delivered_count`, which API sends never write (0 on all 915 creative stages of the last 30 days, 1,706,771 messages sent), so it rendered "—" everywhere. It is now counted clickers ÷ messages sent (`stage_sends.status='sent'`; manual stages `sms_count` / `click_count`) at the stage's creative, over 7 days / 30 days / all time, both sides from ONE hourly snapshot: `operator_rollups` key `creative_ctr` ([lib/creatives/ctr-rollup.ts](../lib/creatives/ctr-rollup.ts)), built by `/api/cron/refresh-creative-lifetime` (one `stage_sends` pass, 11.5 s; the two snapshots fail independently) and merged into the creatives metrics cache. **No migration.** `/api/creatives/list` adds `sent`/`ctr_clickers`, `ctr_7d`/`sent_7d`/`ctr_clickers_7d`, `ctr_lifetime`/`sent_lifetime`/`ctr_clickers_lifetime`; `ctr` (30d, sortable) now populated; `delivered` kept. `/creatives` CTR tooltip shows clickers / sent. Stage creative picker: `CTR all time` + `CTR 7d` columns replace `CTR`, `table-fixed` layout with a narrower creative column and a wider dialog so all columns fit without horizontal scrolling. Verified: `scripts/verify-creative-ctr.ts` (snapshot = independent JS recount in one REPEATABLE READ tx, 396 × 6 exact; invariants; report-cohort comparison exact); tsc; eslint on changed files (no new problems). — docs updated: 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives.md, 04-features/crons.md, 03-data-model.md, 07-conventions.md, operator-api.md.

2026-09-14 - Stage status moves by itself between Draft and Pending (ClickUp 869evxbgb). Migration **0179** adds `campaign_stages.status_set_manually` (boolean, default false; backfilled true where `previous_status IS NOT NULL`). When materialization completes, the same transaction that stamps `materialized_at` moves a `draft` stage to `pending`. Cancelling the prepared send (`…/send/abort`) moves a `pending` stage back to `draft`. The status route and bulk-status set the flag, and [lib/stages/auto-status.ts](../lib/stages/auto-status.ts) never moves a stage carrying it. Each automatic move logs `stage_status_changed` with no actor and `metadata.automatic = true`. Drip stages are not moved. Verified: `scripts/test-stage-auto-status.ts` on the preview DB; tsc; eslint on changed files. — docs updated: 03-data-model.md, 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives.md, 04-features/sms-send-pipeline.md, 05-flows.md (flow D), 07-conventions.md.

2026-09-14 - Operator API pools, PR 2 of 2: `dimension=creative` on `GET /api/reports/performance` (operator API only, no Reports tab). **No migration** (reuses 0178 `operator_rollups`). One row per creative × offer (the stage's creative, the campaign's offer) with every performance column, the grading fields, `first_sent_date` / `last_sent_date` / `distinct_send_days` and `rpm`; human clickers deduped at creative × offer via `getCountedClickersByCreativeOffer()`. Creative-only params (400 on other dimensions): `range=lifetime` (all time, ignores the 92-day cap, served from an hourly snapshot built by `/api/cron/refresh-creative-lifetime` `14 * * * *` — one stage-metrics pass per basis for creative rows and per-offer totals, 37.8s measured; `computed_at` / `stale_seconds`, 503 before the first run), `offer_id` (totals = the `dimension=offer` row), `min_sent` (hides rows, `hidden_rows`), `sortBy=revenue|rpm|sent|click_to_reach_pct`. `StageMetrics.creative_id`; `getStageDimensionReports()` builds several groupings from one funnel pass ([lib/reporting/performance-report.ts](../lib/reporting/performance-report.ts), [lib/reporting/creative-lifetime.ts](../lib/reporting/creative-lifetime.ts), [lib/reporting/creative-rows.ts](../lib/reporting/creative-rows.ts)). 276 classified routes, 39 token pairs. Verified: pure helpers; lib vs independent SQL 18/18 (sent, sales, distinct clickers, send days per creative × offer; offer_id totals; send_date cohort); HTTP 282/282 incl. section 12 and the privacy sweep; grading lib + performance-report regression re-run. — docs updated: operator-api.md (§3 Creative bank + params, §7 `rpm`), 04-features/reports-rollup.md, 04-features/crons.md, 04-features/operator-api-tokens.md, plan pr2.

2026-09-14 - Operator API pools, PR 1 of 2: new `GET /api/audience/pools?offer_id&rest_days=0..30` and migration **0178** `operator_rollups` (applied to prod, integrity OK). Per active contact group plus org totals for one offer: `group_total_eligible`, `never_received`, `never_received_rested`, `received_not_clicked_rested`, `clickers_non_buyers`, `clickers_non_buyers_rested` — rest = whole days since the contact's last sent message of ANY offer, not campaign creation (unlike fresh-counts). Served from `/api/cron/refresh-audience-pools` (`29,59 * * * *`, lease, `maxDuration 300`): one org-wide statement per org (32–40s measured) stores rest-day histograms per group and offer in `operator_rollups`, so any `rest_days` is an exact bucket sum ([lib/audience/pools.ts](../lib/audience/pools.ts), [lib/audience/pool-math.ts](../lib/audience/pool-math.ts)); every offer in one pass, a never-sent offer answered exactly. Token-reachable pairs 38 → 39 (275 routes). Verified: pure math 15/15; rollup vs an independent recount in one REPEATABLE READ transaction 18/18 (2 offers × 2 groups × rest 0/7/30, exact); HTTP 219/219. — docs updated: operator-api.md (failure table, §2 Pools, §9), 03-data-model.md (+ERD), 04-features/crons.md, 04-features/operator-api-tokens.md, 07-conventions.md, spec + plan.

2026-09-14 - Operator API creative grading, PR 5 of 5: new `GET /api/campaigns/{campaignId}/stages/{stageId}/send-groups?groups=2..10`, and `GET /api/dashboard/daily-activity` documented as the tracker's daily sums. **No migration.** Send groups — `getStageSendGroups()` in [lib/reporting/grading.ts](../lib/reporting/grading.ts) — cut a stage's sent messages into equal groups by send order (`ntile` over `sent_at, id`) with per-group sent, reached, opt-outs, human clickers, `click_to_reach_pct`, `opt_rate` and first/last send time, plus `pending_sends` and `opt_outs_complete`. It replaces the spec's per-hour histogram: a ~4,500-send cell drains in 3–6 minutes, inside one or two clock hours. Token-reachable pairs 37 → 38 (273 routes). Verified: lib section H exact against an independent JS split of stage 4095 (groups 2 and 10), HTTP section 10 including daily-activity sales/revenue = tracker sums over 7 closed days. — docs updated: operator-api.md (Send groups, Revenue per day), 04-features/operator-api-tokens.md, 04-features/reports-rollup.md, 07-conventions.md, spec §3/§6 + plan pr5.

2026-09-14 - Operator API creative grading, PR 4 of 5: new `GET /api/creatives/{id}/usage` and `GET /api/campaigns/audit?status=active|paused|completed`. **No migration.** Usage — `getCreativeUsage()` in [lib/reporting/grading.ts](../lib/reporting/grading.ts) — returns one row per campaign + sending number + ET send day over a creative's sent stages: sends, `clicks_human` (distinct across the row's stages), `reached` (null only for an all-manual row), tracker conversions and the campaign's targeted group names; 404 outside the org; the most-reused creative (80 sent stages) measured 5.3s. Audit — `getCampaignAudit()` — returns every campaign in that status with its non-archived stages (`stage_seq`, `split_index`, `behavioral_tier`, stored `status`, scheduled / sent dates, lifetime sent / reached / conversions, `creative_slug`) plus campaign totals, in one request that costs 1 against the token rate limit (per-stage counts for 53 active campaigns measured 568ms). Token-reachable pairs 35 → 37 (272 classified routes). ⭐ THE FIRST VERIFICATION RUN RACED LIVE SENDS, NOT THE CODE: three audit stages read 6 / 25 / 5 sends below an independent count taken seconds later (4279: 2,795 vs 2,801; 4278: 2,300 vs 2,325; 4271: 1,322 vs 1,327), and a follow-up query showed all three mid-drain (2,717 / 300 / 48 rows still pending or sending, last send 0–19s earlier). Exact equality between two reads of a table the drain is writing is a race, so the check now demands exact equality only for SETTLED stages (no pending/sending rows) and lets a still-sending stage only have grown in the direction the two reads were ordered, with a control that the exact check still covers settled stages — rerun: 63 settled stages exact, 9 still sending within tolerance. ⭐ A 10-DIGIT TEST ID TRIPPED THE PHONE SWEEP: the 404 page for `/api/creatives/2147483000/usage` echoes the request path, so missing-entity probes now use a 9-digit id. ⭐ A 400 CHECK WAS VACUOUS: before the audit route existed, `/api/campaigns/[campaignId]` already answered `audit` with an invalid-id 400, so the check now requires the error to name `status`. Verified: `scripts/verify-operator-grading.ts` 321/321 against independent SQL on prod (A–E unchanged; F: usage sums, newest-first order, the largest row's distinct clickers, null outside the org; G: one entry per active campaign, stage counts, per-stage sent / reached / conversions, campaign totals and revenue); `scripts/verify-operator-grading-http.ts` 151/151 against a local server on the prod DB (adds the usage and audit shapes, the 404 and the 400, plus the recipient-phone / `contact_id` sweep); tsc exit 0; eslint 0 problems on all 6 changed code files; route-map coverage and the permission matrix ALL PASS (272 routes, 95 allowed routes wired, 37 token-reachable); `npm run check:docs` clean. - docs/operator-api.md, docs/04-features/operator-api-tokens.md, docs/04-features/reports-rollup.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-operator-api-grading-pr4.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-14 - Operator API creative grading, PR 3 of 5: new `GET /api/reports/opt-outs?dimension=number|campaign|stage|group&from&to&granularity=day` — send-day cohort opt-out rates per ET day. **No migration.** Each row is the STOPs credited to the messages SENT that day divided by those messages; a STOP credits the single most recent stage that messaged the person within 72h, so it lands on the day of the send that caused it. Per-day `totals` come from the sends themselves because group rows overlap (a contact in two targeted groups counts in both); `complete` flips once 72h have passed since the day ended. One statement over a `MATERIALIZED` send set, with sends and distinct STOPs aggregated separately, so there is no `count(DISTINCT send)` sort. Range capped at **14 days** — revised from the spec's 31, because the number dimension measured 11.8s for 7 days (group 4.8s) — with `maxDuration = 60`; token-reachable pairs 34 → 35 (270 classified routes). The two grading routes now share one `isCalendarDay()`. ⚠️ STOP-MODULE TOUCH, STATED PLAINLY: `OPT_OUT_ATTRIBUTION_WINDOW_HOURS` moved out of `lib/sends/poll-opt-outs.ts` into the zero-import `lib/sends/opt-out-window.ts`, so reporting reads it without loading the ingester's alerting, credential-decryption and drip dependencies. The poller imports and re-exports the same name with the same value, and `lib/sends/import-optout-attribution.ts` is untouched — a pure extraction; no opt-out handling behaviour changed. Verified: `scripts/verify-operator-grading.ts` 148/148 against independent SQL on prod (A–D unchanged; E over the 5 closed ET days ending yesterday: per-day totals, rows adding up for number / campaign / stage, the largest group against an independent membership count, `opt_rate` arithmetic, `complete` recomputed per day with a control that both values occur, and the poller's re-export equal to the leaf constant); `scripts/verify-operator-grading-http.ts` 136/136 against a local server on the prod DB (adds the opt-outs shape and five 400 cases, plus the recipient-phone / `contact_id` sweep); tsc exit 0; eslint 0 problems on all 8 changed files; route-map coverage and the permission matrix ALL PASS (270 routes, 93 allowed routes wired, 35 token-reachable); `npm run check:docs` clean. - docs/operator-api.md, docs/04-features/operator-api-tokens.md, docs/04-features/reports-rollup.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-operator-api-grading-design.md, docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-operator-api-grading-pr3.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-14 - Operator API creative grading, PR 2 of 5: `attribution=conversion_date|send_date` on `/api/reports/performance` and new `GET /api/reports/tails?date=`. **No migration.** `getStageMetricsInRange()` takes an optional `attribution`; `send_date` selects the COHORT (stages whose `sent_at` falls in the ET range) and drops every per-stage date window — Keitaro rows at every `stat_date`, all opt-out attributions, all sends, ever-reached, and lifetime manual sales via new `lifetimeManualSalesByStage()` — while the default path is untouched (Overview parity unchanged). A cohort's clickers are scoped by new `CountedClickerBounds.stageIds`, where an EMPTY list emits `AND false`: it must never widen to all stages. The route echoes `attribution`, rejects an unknown value and `hourly` + `send_date` with 400, and sets `maxDuration = 60` (a 7-day cohort measured ~10.5s). Tails — `getConversionTails()` in [lib/reporting/grading.ts](../lib/reporting/grading.ts) — splits one `stat_date`'s tracker conversions into same-day / tail (sent on an earlier day; rows by campaign + creative + send day) / unknown send date, which add up by construction; an impossible date is a 400; token-reachable pairs 33 → 34 (269 classified routes). ⭐ A NAME COLLISION WOULD HAVE 500'D EVERY `send_date` REQUEST: the new lifetime reader was first named `manualSalesByStage`, identical to the local destructured `Promise.all` result, so the call referenced that variable inside its own initializer (TDZ). `tsc` caught it (TS7022 / TS2448) and a pre-fix verification run crashed with `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'manualSalesByStage2' before initialization` — the default path never evaluates that branch, so nothing on `conversion_date` would have shown it. Verified: `scripts/verify-operator-grading.ts` 60/60 against independent SQL on prod (A–B unchanged; C: the `send_date` cohort's sent, reached, opt-outs, sales, revenue and deduped clickers on number, offer and sequence, plus a control that the two bases differ on sales; D: tails for 2026-09-14 — day total, tail split, rows, three-way sum); `scripts/test-performance-report.ts` Overview parity unchanged; `scripts/verify-operator-grading-http.ts` 116/116 against a local server on the prod DB (adds the `attribution` echo, both 400s, the tails shape and the impossible-date 400); tsc exit 0; eslint 0 problems on all 11 changed files; `npm run check:authz` ALL PASS (269 routes classified, 34 token-reachable); `npm run check:docs` clean. - docs/operator-api.md, docs/04-features/operator-api-tokens.md, docs/04-features/reports-rollup.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-operator-api-grading-pr2.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-14 - Operator API creative grading, PR 1 of 5: `reached`, `clicks_human` and grading rates on the performance report and campaign stages; scored-human fix + `clicks_human` on click-report; `creative_id` / `creative_slug` on today's sends. **No migration, no new route, no token-allowlist change.** New pure [lib/reporting/grading-rates.ts](../lib/reporting/grading-rates.ts): `pct()` in percent units (`3.04` = 3.04%) returning `null` when uncomputable, `gradingRates()`, and `addNullable()`. `getStageMetricsInRange()` gains per-stage `reached` — per-recipient `stage_sends.offer_reached_at` by REACH day, `null` for manual-mode stages — and `gradePerf()` adds `clicks_human` (= counted clickers), `click_to_reach_pct`, `reach_to_sale_pct` and `opt_rate` to every performance row and the totals; the stages route adds the same five per stage from its existing grouped queries. ⭐ THE FIRST NULL RULE WAS WRONG AND WAS CAUGHT BY THE VERIFICATION: "any manual stage nulls the row" nulled nearly every total, because old manual campaigns keep trickling Keitaro visits into any range (13 of 836 stages over 2026-09-07..13, carrying 7 of 12,769 visits and 0 sales). A row is now `null` only when ALL its stages are manual — which also means an accumulator must START from `null` (its identity), not 0, or a manual-only row silently reads 0. ⭐ `/api/reports/performance` TOTALS SUMMED per-stage clicker counts: `totals.counted_clickers` / `lifetime_clickers` are now distinct (campaign, contact), the Overview totals-card definition, narrowed by `provider_phone_id`; the Reports UI renders neither, so no on-screen number moved. Click-report `human` now uses the shared `HUMAN_CLICK` (scored only). ⚠️ PRE-EXISTING, NOT FIXED HERE: By Group's click-weight query walks every scored-human click (~50s at ANY range) and intermittently exceeds the 2-minute statement timeout (`57014`); `scripts/verify-lifetime-display.ts` times out on untouched origin/main `e1fff8e` as well (baseline run 2026-09-14). The new reach weights run AFTER that parallel batch so they add no contention to it. ⚠️ A phone-shaped sweep must skip a float's fraction: the bare pattern flagged `"cost":1234.9999999999995` on the first run (the new HTTP harness excludes digit runs after a digit or `.` and proves a real phone still goes red; `verify-operator-access.ts` still uses the bare pattern). Also fixed the stale `Route X` alias wording and the wrong dimension list in docs/operator-api.md. Verified: `scripts/test-grading-rates.ts` 17/17; `scripts/verify-operator-grading.ts` 29/29 against independent SQL on prod (stage and every dimension's `totals.reached` = 1,226 = direct count incl. By Group; deduped clicker totals unfiltered and filtered to one number; hourly); `scripts/test-performance-report.ts` Overview parity unchanged; `scripts/verify-operator-grading-http.ts` 96/96 over HTTP against a local server on the prod DB (per-stage fields vs SQL, click-report `human` / `clicks_human`, today's creative slugs, and a recipient-phone / `contact_id` sweep with can-go-red controls); tsc exit 0; eslint 0 problems on 11 changed files; `npm run check:authz` ALL PASS; `npm run check:docs` clean. - docs/operator-api.md, docs/04-features/reports-rollup.md, docs/04-features/epc-denominator.md, docs/04-features/tracking-attribution.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-operator-api-grading-design.md, docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-operator-api-grading-pr1.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-11 - Provider-name redaction REMOVED — every role now sees real provider names (owner decision). **No migration.** `lib/authz/redact.ts`, the `jsonForRole()` response helper and `scripts/seed-provider-route-aliases.ts` are deleted; 23 call sites across 9 API routes unwrapped back to plain `NextResponse.json(...)`, and `SendStateStripLoader` — a SERVER component that redacted by hand because it never crosses the API response boundary — loses both the redaction and its now-unused `role` prop (the protected layout stops passing it). Rationale: the registry name IS the display name, and the owner renames providers to whatever should be visible, so an alias layer has nothing left to hide. ⭐ THE GUARD WAS INVERTED, NOT DELETED: `scripts/verify-operator-access.ts` used to sweep every operator response, rendered page and token body for any string in `SELECT name FROM sms_providers` and FAIL on a hit; that sweep is replaced by a POSITIVE, can-go-red assertion — the operator must actually receive a real provider name from `providers/list`, and a surviving `"Route X"` alias fails the run. Deleting a redactor is only "done" if the thing it used to hide actually arrives. ⚠️ SCOPE, STATED PLAINLY: redaction was all-or-nothing per role, so operators now also see `adapter_code`, `connection_type` and the numeric `sms_provider_id`, not just names. **Contact confidentiality is untouched** — the audience block is still denied by `OPERATOR_ROUTE_MAP` and the permission matrix, and the verification script still sweeps responses, pages and token bodies for recipient phone numbers and `contact_id`. `provider_route_aliases` (7 rows) is RETAINED but unused, so the decision stays reversible; drop it in a later migration if that changes. `lib/permissions.ts` is untouched — no permission was widened. Verified: tsc exit 0; eslint 0 problems across all 14 changed files; `npm run check:authz` ALL PASS (permission matrix + route-map coverage, 268/268 routes classified); `npm run check:docs` clean. - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/04-features/multi-tenancy-auth.md, docs/04-features/operator-api-tokens.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/operator-api.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-11 - Operator role made usable: Reports Overview un-403'd, every timestamp un-corrupted, the send button un-hidden, and the sidebar gated by permission. **No migration.** All four surfaced within minutes of the FIRST real operator (bohdan@exuma.io) signing in, and none of them could have been seen before that: `redactForRole()` returns the payload untouched for every role except `operator`, and this org had exactly one Owner for a year - so the whole operator path was dead code in practice while every check stayed green. ⭐ THE WORST ONE WAS A TYPE, NOT A PERMISSION: `redactValue()` rebuilt every object from `Object.entries()`, and a Date has NO own enumerable properties, so EVERY timestamp in an operator payload became `{}`; `new Date({})` is Invalid Date, date-fns `format()` throws `RangeError` on it, and with NO error boundary anywhere in the app `/campaigns` died to a blank browser error page. Drizzle returns `timestamptz` as a Date and 7 of the 9 redacting routes select one, so it was all of them, not one page - fixed by recursing only into PLAIN objects and arrays (a Date serialises to the same ISO string the Owner gets, so passing it through is both the fix and the correct wire format). ⭐ REPORTS WAS A MISCLASSIFIED ROUTE: `keitaro/reports` sat in OPERATOR_ROUTE_MAP as `null` with the cron/import machinery, but it backs the /reports **Overview tab** - a screen the operator is meant to use - so the operator got "This route is not available to the operator role" while `reports/performance` and `reports/delivery` beside it worked; now `{ methods: ["GET"] }`, wired with its route key, and its responses go through `jsonForRole` (it previously did not redact at all). ⭐ THE SEND BUTTON WAS A PARAPHRASE, NOT A MIRROR: `approve-send` and `retry-failed` carve the operator out of the `campaigns.drain` check server-side (`&& role !== "operator"`), but stage-send-panel computed ONE `canSend = can("campaigns.drain")` and used it for three different things, so "Prepare & send now" was permanently disabled with "requires manager+" for an action the API would have accepted; split into `canDrain` (POST send/drain - genuinely manager+) and `canSendNow` (mirrors the server expression). Manager was never blocked - it holds `campaigns.drain`. ⭐ AND THE SIDEBAR NEVER OPTED IN: `SidebarNav` has always filtered on `!item.permission || can(item.permission)`, but only 2 of 34 items declared a permission, so every role saw the whole nav and clicking through hit a 403; all 33 non-Dashboard items now declare the permission that guards what the page does, cross-checked against the route map. Operator now sees 19/34 (Dashboard, Overview, all Reports, Campaigns, Today's sends, Creatives, Segments, view-only Registry) and no longer sees the audience block, SMS Providers or Settings. ⚠️ BEHAVIOUR CHANGE TO NOTE: Settings > Sending is now gated on `compliance.manage`, which is OWNER-ONLY, so manager and admin lose that nav item (no such users exist today); the route map already called it an Owner control. New `scripts/test-operator-redaction.ts` (the redactor had NO test at all, which is how this shipped) + `npm run check:authz` running the three authz suites together. Verified: redaction 26/26 incl. a can-go-red control reproducing the old branch; route-map coverage ALL PASS (91 allowed / 177 denied of 268, all 91 wired); operator permission matrix ALL PASS with viewer/manager/admin/owner byte-identical; nav visibility simulated per role from the real modules; tsc exit 0; eslint clean. - docs/04-features/operator-guardrails.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-11 - Google sign-in now returns to the hostname it started from, **no migration**. CamMan answers on two production hostnames, and `signInWithGoogleAction` / `linkGoogleIdentityAction` pinned `redirectTo` to `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` (`camman.vercel.app`). Anyone starting on `camman.exuma.io` was therefore sent back to a DIFFERENT ORIGIN - and the PKCE **code verifier is a cookie on the origin the flow began from**, so `exchangeCodeForSession()` failed on the way back. ⭐ THE FAILURE IS INVISIBLE IN BOTH SYSTEMS: supabase-js validates the verifier LOCALLY and never issues the request, so Supabase's auth log showed `/authorize` -> `/callback` with **no `/token` line at all** and no error, while the browser just landed on `/login`; the missing `/token` between `/authorize` and the next event is the diagnostic fingerprint. This is what blocked the first invited user (bohdan@exuma.io) even after the stale Google client secret was corrected - two independent root causes stacked behind one symptom. New `authCallbackOrigin(requestHost)` in [lib/app-origin.ts](../lib/app-origin.ts) resolves it. ⭐ IT IS A DELIBERATE, NARROW EXCEPTION TO "OUTBOUND URLS COME FROM ENV, NEVER THE REQUEST HOST", and the carve-out is "persisted vs consumed": a URL a PROVIDER STORES outlives the request and must stay on `appOrigin()` (that rule is unchanged and still guarded), whereas an OAuth callback is consumed inside the same browser flow and is never written down. The host also never SUPPLIES an origin - it only SELECTS one env already declares, so a spoofed `Host` or a preview URL matches nothing and falls back to the primary. Verified: `scripts/test-partner-host.ts` extended to 14 new checks incl. partner-host resolution, case-insensitivity, `x-forwarded-host` chains, preview + spoofed hosts refused, unset-primary -> null, and structural checks that BOTH OAuth call sites go through the helper - plus two new can-go-red controls proving a re-pinned `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` is detected; whole suite 45/45 with every pre-existing registration-route check still green; tsc exit 0; eslint 0 problems on all four changed files. Confirmed against prod that Supabase already honors `https://camman.exuma.io/auth/callback`, so no dashboard change is needed - and noted in docs/06-integrations.md that the same probe showed an ARBITRARY third-party origin also being honored, i.e. the Redirect URL allowlist is empty or wildcarded and should be narrowed (PKCE limits the impact). - docs/04-features/multi-tenancy-auth.md, docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-11 - Invited users no longer get a stray organization of their own, **migration 0177** (function replacement only, no schema change). `handle_new_user()` (`0001`) fires on EVERY `auth.users` INSERT and creates an org + `owner` membership - written when self-signup was the only way in, and never reconciled when `0175` added Owner-issued invites. ⭐ THE ORDERING MAKES IT UNAVOIDABLE, NOT UNLUCKY: Supabase creates the `auth.users` row inside its OWN `/auth/v1/callback`, BEFORE redirecting to `app/auth/callback/route.ts`, so the trigger has always already run by the time `resolveAllowlist()` looks - and that function checks `org_members` before `invites` and returns on the first hit, making the invite branch unreachable for every first-time Google user. The invitee signs in SUCCESSFULLY into an empty org as its owner, sees zero contacts and zero campaigns, and the invite stays `pending` forever; nothing errors and nothing logs, so it reads as the feature working. `0177` returns early when an open, unexpired `invites` row matches the address. ⭐ A TRIGGER IS A CALLER YOU FORGET YOU HAVE - the residue is still visible in the `camman-v2` project as a stray `operator-test's Organization` holding 0 contacts. ⭐ `LIMIT 1` WITHOUT `ORDER BY` IS A COIN FLIP THAT PICKS A TENANT: the same `camman-v2` account holds TWO `org_members` rows and `resolveAllowlist` had no ordering, so which org it resolved to was arbitrary - it now prefers the invite-provisioned row (`invited_email IS NOT NULL`) then oldest. Also fixed: the **Forgot password link 404'd** - `app/(auth)/login/page.tsx` pointed at `/auth/forgot-password` but `(auth)` is a route GROUP and contributes no URL segment, so the page has always been served at `/forgot-password`; it was the only reference in the codebase. Context (config, not code): the reported "Google login bounces back to the login screen" was a **stale Google OAuth Client Secret in the Supabase dashboard** - Supabase's auth log carries it verbatim as `oauth2: "invalid_client" "The provided client secret is invalid."` on `path=/callback`, and because Supabase 500s before creating the user there is no app log, no audit row and no user row to inspect; runbook added to docs/06-integrations.md. Verified: trigger tested end-to-end on the camman-v2 project inside a transaction aborted by a deliberate `RAISE EXCEPTION` (invited user -> 0 membership rows, self-signup user -> 1 membership row, exactly 1 org created), rollback confirmed clean (0 leftover users/invites, org + member counts restored, function reverted); guard predicate checked read-only against real prod data (bohdan's open invite -> skip, case-insensitive -> skip, uninvited -> org still created, NULL email -> org still created); tsc exit 0; eslint 0 problems on both changed files. - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/04-features/multi-tenancy-auth.md, docs/05-flows.md, docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-07 - `scripts/test-lane-send.ts` repaired: it had been 9 passed / 15 failed for ~2.5 months, **no migration, test-only**. Its fixture never set `scheduled_at`, and `kickoffStageSend` gained a `no_schedule` refusal on 2026-06-23 (11a1e12), so every kickoff in the test silently refused - taking the lane preview/materialization/row-shape assertions down with it. ⭐ A TEST THAT REFUSES EVERYTHING STILL EXITS "PASSED": the 9 survivors assert on an EMPTY result set (the SEND_ENABLED gate, the mock dispatcher never firing, "no duplicate rows"), which an all-refusing run satisfies perfectly - vacuous assertions are what let a dead test look alive, and the script was even edited on 2026-07-03 without anyone noticing. ⭐ FIXING IT STRANDED DATA IN PRODUCTION: with kickoff working the test created real rows and the teardown's `DELETE FROM contacts` hit the default `statement_timeout` and aborted part-way, leaving 1 org / 1 brand / 6 contacts / 1 creative in the live DB (found by marker name, removed BY ID, real org's 823,914 contacts verified intact). A contact delete cascades into 14 tables, five with no leading index on `contact_id`, so it seq-scans ~8.6M rows (~150s); the teardown now runs in a transaction with `SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '300s'`. ⭐ THE DRIFT CHECK ITSELF WAS WRONG FOR A LIVE DB: global before/after equality reported +1,368 clicks and +3,900 send_attempts that were genuine production traffic, burying the 6 rows that were actually the test's - split into "did I leave anything behind?" (per-org, exact) and "did I delete someone else's data?" (global, fail only on a DECREASE). Dating the fixture stages is safe on prod only because the fixture campaign is `link_mode='manual'` and both cron phases require `'tracked'`. Verified: 25 passed / 0 failed (was 9/15), zero fixture rows left for the test org, tsc clean, eslint 0 problems. - docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-07 - Behavioural split lanes are now INDEPENDENT: the all-or-nothing release gate is REMOVED and replaced by a structural sibling exclusion, **no migration**. 0174 made Phase B refuse to drain any lane until the whole group reached `materialized`. That coupling had no timeout: Phase A only selects `send_approved AND scheduled_at IS NOT NULL`, so ONE never-scheduled sibling could never materialize, the group could never settle, and every other lane was held forever WITHOUT being marked missed (Phase B never selected them, so nothing stamped `schedule_missed_at` - the stages neither sent nor expired). That froze 650 built messages for ~13h on 2026-09-05. Each lane now releases on its own merits; an unprepared, unscheduled or `failed` sibling holds nobody back, and the group state machine is observability only. ⭐ THE GATE WAS LOAD-BEARING FOR A SECOND, UNDOCUMENTED REASON: a lane's audience is an EXACT match on a HIGH-WATER tier that only rises, and each lane snapshots when IT materializes, so a contact who is tier 0 when lane A materializes and tier 2 when lane B does lands in BOTH - simultaneous release was the only thing keeping every such collision inside the drain's 1-hour dedup window. Measured before the change (all 77 groups): 73 scheduled every lane at the IDENTICAL time, 76 materialized within 5 min, and ZERO contacts had ever appeared in two lanes - a zero produced BY the coupling, and staggering (the whole point of independence) is exactly what would have made it reachable. ⭐ SO THE REPLACEMENT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT TIMING-BASED: `stageRecipientsSql` "Block 3" excludes any contact already claimed by a SIBLING lane of the same group - first lane to materialize a contact owns them at any stagger; `rejected` rows release the claim (cancel audit trail); a lane never excludes itself (without that, a materialized lane would zero out its OWN displayed count). ⭐ A DUPLICATED PREDICATE NEEDS A TEST THAT COMPARES THE COPIES: the exclusion also had to go into `computeLaneAudienceCountsBatch` so the lane-count preview keeps predicting what materializes, and verify-lane-batch.ts - the only thing comparing the two on real data - passed `splitGroupId` on NEITHER side until it was wired in. ⭐ GUARDS WERE INVERTED, NOT DELETED: verify-campaign-level-split §9 asserted "NO lane of a failed group is drainable" and now asserts the opposite plus the exact 2026-09-05 shape; its fixture mutation is restored afterwards because leaving the siblings unscheduled made §10's assertion pass for the WRONG reason. Also downgraded the `sweepStuckSplitGroups` Tier-1 "every lane is held unreleased" alert (now false) to an informational "lane never prepared", and fixed verify-lane-batch.ts's env loading (an inline dotenv `config()` ran AFTER ESM hoisted db/client, so it died 28P01 every run). Verified: new [scripts/test-lane-sibling-exclusion.ts](../scripts/test-lane-sibling-exclusion.ts) 8/8 incl. a counterfactual proving the guard is load-bearing, verify-campaign-level-split 58/58, verify-lane-batch 25 campaigns identical/0 mismatched WITH the exclusion active on both sides, test-recipients-lanes 12/12, test-lane-preview-count 10/10, test-behavioral-split 28/28, test-behavioral-split-lane-picker 26/26, test-split-manual-prepare 14/14, test-stage-copy-invariants 11/11, tsc clean. (test-lane-send is 9/15 on this branch AND byte-identically on unmodified origin/main - pre-existing: its fixture never sets `scheduled_at` and kickoff refuses `no_schedule`.) - docs/04-features/behavioral-lanes.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-06 - Behavioural split lanes are now PICKED at creation, `Ignored` (tier 0) OFF by default, **no migration**. A split always stamped three lanes and the operator deleted the tier-0 lane BY HAND every time - 73 of the first 77 groups have only two lanes, and `campaign_events` held 124 `stage_deleted` rows in 30 days (24 of the 25 most recent were "Stage 3 deleted" right after a split). That manual step was load-bearing and nothing enforced it. On 2026-09-05 it was skipped on three campaigns and **650 fully-materialized messages sat undelivered for ~13h** (campaigns 1151/1152/1171) while the drain was healthy - 13,225 messages went out on the SAME provider and the SAME two numbers in that hour. ⭐ A LEFTOVER LANE FREEZES ITS SIBLINGS FOREVER, NOT TEMPORARILY: a split group releases all-or-nothing, and Phase A only selects `send_approved = true AND scheduled_at IS NOT NULL`, so an unscheduled+unapproved lane can never materialize -> `settleSplitGroup` never flips the group -> Phase B holds every sibling AND never stamps `schedule_missed_at` (it never selects them), so the stages neither send nor expire. The 60-min `sweepStuckSplitGroups` alarm is measured from the LAST lane's due time, so it fired long after the send window closed. ⭐ THE FIX IS REMOVING THE MANUAL STEP, NOT ADDING A WARNING: `DEFAULT_LANE_TIERS = [1, 2]` + a per-tier checkbox in the confirm dialog, so the trap row is never created; ticking `Ignored` still yields the old trio. ⭐ CHANGING A DEFAULT SILENTLY CHANGED 11 CALLERS AND `tsc` COULD NOT SEE IT - `tiers` is optional, so every call site still type-checked while dropping 3 lanes to 2; the 10 script call sites were pinned to an explicit `tiers: [0,1,2]` rather than having their assertions rewritten down to 2, which would have deleted the only coverage the three-lane path has. ⭐ RECOVERY ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: reschedule the stuck lanes to a future in-window time FIRST, then remove the blocker - settling the group while the old date is past makes Phase B's day-anchored `decideScheduledSend` return `missed` and burn the send permanently. Validation lives in `resolveLaneTiers` in the lib (not only the route) so the script harnesses exercise it: `[]` => 400 `no_lanes_selected`, any tier outside {0,1,2} => 400 `invalid_lane_tier`, a body with a malformed `tiers` => 400 (never a silent fallback to the default), and a refused selection writes NO group row and NO lane rows. Verified: new [scripts/test-behavioral-split-lane-picker.ts](../scripts/test-behavioral-split-lane-picker.ts) 26/26 including the incident regression guard (a default 2-lane group reaches `materialized`), test-behavioral-split 28/28, test-split-manual-prepare 14/14, test-stage-copy-invariants 11/11, verify-campaign-level-split 56/56, tsc clean, eslint identical to baseline (7 pre-existing problems before and after). Production recovery applied the same day: the six frozen lanes rescheduled to 10:00 ET and the three leftover tier-0 lanes deleted through the real API; all three groups settled to `materialized`. - docs/04-features/behavioral-lanes.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-09-03 - Text Request opt-out intake was dark on two new numbers: `dashboard_id` is now REQUIRED and the message poll walks direction-major, **no migration**. `+18552245147` (Guide Kin) and `+18447434292` (Fits You) were added 2026-09-02 with the "Text Request dashboard ID" field blank, sent **16,918** messages on 2026-09-03, and recorded **zero** opt-outs while 265 contacts who replied STOP stayed sendable org-wide. ⭐ A NUMBER CAN BE HALF-CONFIGURED BECAUSE ONE PROVIDER SCOPES ITS API THREE WAYS: TR sends are ACCOUNT-scoped (`POST /messages` carries `from`/`to`), the per-message `status_callback` DLR is CREDENTIAL-scoped, and every INBOUND path is DASHBOARD-scoped - so with `dashboard_id` NULL the number sent at full rate (10,918 / 6,000) and returned DLRs normally (10,379 / 5,313 events) while `resolveTxrPollTargets` skipped it (`AND ph.dashboard_id IS NOT NULL`) and `register-textrequest-hooks`, which derives its dashboard list from numbers that already have one, never registered `msg_received` for it. Diagnosed by instrumenting each boundary rather than the symptom: sends ✓, DLRs ✓, inbound events **0 of 0** against 724 for the working number - which isolates the failing layer in one table. ⭐ THE PROVIDER'S OWN COMPLIANCE MADE IT LOOK HEALTHY: TR marks STOP repliers opted out on its side and rejects them with error 2100/30050, so nothing failed visibly - the 265 were only exposed through the OTHER providers (TextHub / Ahoi / Tells), where no suppression existed. ⭐ THE GUARD COULD NOT SEE THE BUG IT WAS WRITTEN FOR: the poll's `walk: inbound is walked FIRST` check passed under either flattening because the fixture had ONE dashboard; the real code was `targets.flatMap(t => ["R","S"])`, i.e. dashboard-major, so a second dashboard's inbound walk sat behind the first's 10K-row outbound walk and the shared 60s `maxDuration` died in it (and `resolveTxrPollTargets` has no `ORDER BY`, so which number lost was arbitrary). Fixed to `(["R","S"]).flatMap(d => targets.map(...))`; the test now adds a second fixture dashboard and asserts the invariant GLOBALLY (every inbound request precedes every outbound one), RED-PROVEN by restoring the old line. Also: `FieldSpec.required` added and set on txr's `dashboard_id`, enforced off the one flag in three places - the form's asterisk, the phones POST (missing ⇒ 400 `required_provider_setting_missing`) and the phones PATCH (clearing ⇒ the same 400) - keyed on the descriptor's `adapter_code`, never on `sms_provider_id`, so a second account of a type gets the rule and no new provider needs a branch. Remediation applied to production: both numbers bound (matched on TR's own dashboard phone digits, not by hand), 6 hooks registered via the real operator route (3 already present, 0 failed), and new [scripts/drain-textrequest-inbound.ts](../scripts/drain-textrequest-inbound.ts) (dry-run default, `--apply`) recovered the backlog by delegating to the production `pollTxrMessages` with the walk scoped to the named dashboards' inbound direction - no second intake implementation to drift. Preflighted against the opt-out-rate breaker first (worst stage 2.14% vs the 8%/10% thresholds) so the backfill could not pause a live campaign. Verified: 265 STOP repliers now suppressed with **0 remaining**, stage `inbound_opt_out_count` 44/38/36/60/46/41 (was 0 across all six), live `webhook_msg_received` events arriving for both new numbers, no campaign paused, `scripts/test-textrequest-poll.ts` and `scripts/test-textrequest-optout.ts` both ALL PASS, tsc clean, eslint 0 errors on changed files. - docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/04-features/crons.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-29 - Stall detector: the 30-minute grace now runs from when a stage became ELIGIBLE TO DRAIN, not from when it was materialized, **no migration**. A Telegram alert claimed `Send queue STALLED - 2934 pending, never sent` about stage 3500 (`8_58_082826_3_s2_c632`, Texthub 621637+TFN) that in fact drained completely - 2,760 sent + 174 filtered = the same 2,934 - between 14:01:08 and 14:26:36 UTC. ⭐ MATERIALIZATION MOVED AND SILENTLY EXPIRED THE GRACE: `findStalledStages` measured its "give a fresh stage time to get going" window from `campaign_stages.materialized_at`, which was fine while materialization happened at send time, but it is now a pre-pass running HOURS ahead (150 min ahead for this stage; 205 of the 207 stages materialized in the prior 7 days led their `scheduled_at` by >30 min). The stage therefore reached its due moment with the whole grace already spent, leaving "has a `sent` row in the trailing 30 min" as the only suppressor - which a never-sent stage cannot satisfy until the drain reaches it. ⭐ THE ALARM WAS A 29-SECOND RACE, WHICH IS WHY ONE STAGE WAS NAMED AND ITS THREE CO-DUE SIBLINGS WERE NOT: stages 3464/3465/3480/3500 all came due at 14:00:00 and were claimed together at 14:00:34.5; the drain serves due stages round-robin (20 s slices) so their first rows landed at 14:00:35.5 / 14:00:35.9 / 14:00:39.3 / 14:01:08.8, and the hourly cron (`0 * * * *`) ran inside the gap where the first three had proof of life and the fourth did not. Fix anchors the predicate on `GREATEST(materialized_at, COALESCE(scheduled_at, ...), COALESCE(sent_at, ...))` - due, or released by hand, whichever is later - so a genuinely wedged stage still alarms 30 min after it came due. Verified: 24/24 checks green in `scripts/test-stall-pacing-and-missed.ts` (section C added), and RED-PROVEN by restoring the old predicate, which fails exactly the two new positive checks while both controls stay green; `scripts/test-stall-detector.ts` 8/8 and `scripts/test-stall-detector-perf.ts` still pass (detector 70 ms on the live 4.05M-row table). - docs/04-features/crons.md, docs/04-features/sms-send-pipeline.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-28 - Today's Sends number grouping: the picker is a searchable dropdown, not a tab strip, **no migration**. Follow-up to the same day's grouping change after review. A `<SearchableSelect>` "Number" filter picks `All numbers` (default) or one number; unfiltered, every number renders as a stacked labelled section, and picking one renders just that section - same `PhoneStageGroup` both ways, so they cannot drift. Grouping, ordering, and the API are untouched. ⭐ SWAPPING TABS FOR A DROPDOWN SILENTLY DELETES AN AT-A-GLANCE SIGNAL: the tab strip showed a needs-action dot for EVERY number at once, while a dropdown's dots only exist while it is open. The dot alone therefore stopped being a monitoring affordance the moment the control changed, even though the code rendering it still worked. Compensated in two places - a `needs action` badge on each group header (what actually makes an unhappy number scannable down the unfiltered list) and an "N numbers need action" summary beside the trigger. ⭐ A SEARCH BOX THAT CANNOT MATCH THE THING IT FILTERS ON: `SearchableSelect` matched `label` only, and the label renders `formatPhoneInternational()` output - `+1 844 621 0404` - so typing the number the way anyone types a phone number, `8446210404`, returned "No number matches". Fixed by extending `SearchableSelectOption` with an optional `searchText`, matched alongside `label` and never rendered, carrying the raw E.164 digits; verified in-browser that `8446210404` resolves to the number displayed with spaces, and `ahoi` resolves by provider. Extended rather than forked, per the standing "don't add a fourth popover-search component" rule; the one other consumer (`components/segments/rules-panel.tsx`) is unaffected because the field is optional. ⭐ A MEASURED RANGE IS NOT AN OPERATING RANGE: 21 days of data showed 3-7 numbers, which I first wrote up as a deliberate exception BELOW the picker convention's "≤10 options stay a plain `<Select>`" bar. The operator then confirmed the real range exceeds 10, making it an ordinary application of the rule - the sample bounded what had happened, never what the screen must hold, and the note would have taught the next reader to "fix" the search away. Re-verified at scale rather than assumed: driven in-browser with a synthesized 22-number payload (22 sections render; the option list scrolls at ~9 of 23 rows; the popover stays inside the viewport; search still narrows by bare digits and by provider), and `scripts/test-today-grouping.ts` pins the ordering at 25 numbers including the case where the LAST-indexed number needs action. Because needs-action groups sort FIRST, the numbers that matter stay reachable without scrolling at any count - that ordering, not the dropdown, is what makes a long list usable. - docs/04-features/daily-volume-ui.md, docs/04-features/ui-system.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-28-todays-sends-group-by-number-design.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-28 - Today's Sends groups its stage list by the sending number, **no migration**. Tabs: `All` (default) then one per number, labelled `<number> (<provider>) <count>` with a dot when that number holds a needs-action stage; `All` stacks every number as a labelled section and a number tab shows that one section, both from the same `PhoneStageGroup` so they cannot drift. The grouping key is `campaign_stages.provider_phone_id` - one number per stage, set on the STAGE, so it is known BEFORE materialization and an unprepared stage still lands in its number's group (measured: 0 stages with a NULL phone in 21 days; 3-7 numbers and 18-42 stages per ET day). Ordering is a pure module, `lib/sends/group-stages-by-phone.ts`, guarded by `scripts/test-today-grouping.ts`: within a block the needs-action band comes first, then ascending `scheduled_at` inside each band. ⭐ THE BAND IS DERIVED AS `STAGE_STATUS_META[...].sortWeight === 0`, NOT A HARDCODED LIST OF THE FOUR STATES - so a future attention state joins it automatically and a benign one cannot wander in; the guard asserts the band's exact membership, so adding a `sortWeight: 0` state without meaning to fails it. ⭐ SEPARATE BUG THE GROUPING FORCED OUT: the candidate query never selected `skipped_empty_at` or `slip_hold_at`, so `deriveStageOperationalStatus` got `undefined` for both and could NEVER return `skipped_empty` / `held` on this screen. An empty behavioural lane (0174, shipped 2026-08-27) therefore fell through to `scheduled_unprepared` and rendered ORANGE with a Prepare button - a false alarm that this change would have PINNED TO THE TOP of its phone block permanently. Latent when found (0 such stages today and 0 in 14 days) precisely because the feature producing them shipped the day before, so it would have activated silently. Both columns are now selected and passed through. The top block (banners, tiles, "Prepared for today" + its per-number list, meter, stuck callout) is unchanged and stays org-wide; the per-row provider chip was dropped since the group header now owns number/provider identity; tab selection is deliberately not persisted. Cross-check on live data: the per-group `sent/prepared` aggregates (built stage->phone from `campaign_stages`) sum to 61,436 and match the pre-existing `prepared_by_phone` figures (built directly from `stage_sends.provider_phone_id`) number for number - two different aggregation paths agreeing, which is what confirms the stage->phone mapping. - docs/04-features/daily-volume-ui.md, docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-28-todays-sends-group-by-number-design.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-28 — Send-queue watching: pacing ceilings are a hold, not a stall; and stood-down stages now have an owner. **No migration.** Follow-up to the 2026-08-27 report outage, from investigating the 3,976 "stuck" Text Request messages it had been hiding. ⭐ THOSE MESSAGES WERE NEVER STUCK: txr sat at 30,038 against its configured `max_sends_per_24h = 30,000`, so the drain broke with `stopReason = "rate_24h"` on every tick — exactly as designed — while the stall detector, which models every OTHER hold (campaign pause, provider pause, org switch, org emergency stop, quiet hours) but not the pacing ceilings, reported it as 3,976 undraining messages and advised "check the send-scheduled cron / provider health". ET daily volume 08-21→08-27: 24,496 · 19,539 · — · 23,015 · 24,426 · **30,042** · **30,038**, i.e. pinned to the ceiling for two days, with the tell-tale shape of a ~20K burst at 14:00 UTC, a hard stop, then ~7K more at 22:00 as the previous day's burst aged out of the rolling window. `findStalledStages` now classifies each candidate `hold: "stalled" | "rate_24h" | "rate_minute"` by reusing the drain's OWN predicate (`countSentSince(org, provider, window) >= resolve*Cap(...)`, one query per distinct org+provider); the cron alerts only when `trulyStalled()` is non-empty, and capped stages appear in a separate per-provider "held at a pacing ceiling" section with the numbers. ⭐ SEPARATE AND WORSE, FOUND IN THE SAME PULL: `findStalledStages` excludes `schedule_missed_at IS NOT NULL` — correctly, a stood-down stage is not failing to drain — but NOTHING else watched that category, so stage 3272 (`142_118_082626_2_s1_c600`) held **2,000 undispatched messages for 35 hours** and would have held them forever; it was stood down because txr was at its ceiling when its slot came up and the ET window closed before headroom returned. New `lib/sends/missed-stages.ts` owns it, scoped to `campaigns.status='active'` (the drain filters the same value in both phases, so stages under completed campaigns can never send — permanent, not actionable) and LATCHED per stage via `alert_state` (`missed_stage:stage:<id>`), clearing any firing key absent from the current set so a recurrence is audible. `scripts/test-stall-pacing-and-missed.ts` — 19 checks, all four with controls: the same fixture under a roomy cap must read `stalled`, and the missed stage must go quiet on tick 2, clear on recovery, and speak again on recurrence. — docs/04-features/crons.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-27 — Hourly Telegram reports stopped for a full day; root cause was the stall watchdog, **no migration**. The route ran its two best-effort watches BEFORE deciding/building the report, and `findStalledStages` resolved `last_sent` with a correlated `(SELECT max(ss.sent_at) … WHERE ss.stage_id = s.id)`. ⭐ POSTGRES REWRITES A BARE `max()` INTO A BACKWARD INDEX WALK, AND THE ONLY INDEX LEADING WITH `sent_at` IS `stage_sends_sent_at_contact_idx`, WHERE `stage_id` IS A FILTER — so for a candidate stage that has NEVER SENT there is no first match and it scans the entire index: **48 s per candidate row**, 1,846,869 rows removed by filter, 3.15 M buffers, on a 3.86 M-row table. Stage 3314 (Text Request, 3,000 pending) materialized 11:47 UTC and never sent, becoming a candidate at 12:17 UTC; from 16:00 Warsaw every hourly tick took 96 s against `maxDuration = 60` and was killed. ⭐ AND `try/catch` CATCHES ERRORS, NOT SLOWNESS — the watch was "best-effort" but unbounded, so it starved the report AND the handler died upstream of its own "report failed" alert: a watchdog for stuck sends silently suppressed 8 hours of reports plus the alert saying so, exactly when the queue really had stalled (3,976 messages undrained, never reported). Fix is two-part: `stageSendStats()` resolves `pending`/`last_sent` in a grouped second pass over the surviving candidates (`GROUP BY stage_id` defeats the min/max rewrite, so the planner uses `stage_sends_stage_id_idx` as an Index Cond) — **96,024 ms → 402 ms** on identical input, whole detector **108 s → 0.9 s**, no new index; and the route now sends the report FIRST with each watch bounded at `CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS = 10 s`. `scripts/test-stall-detector-perf.ts` synthesises a never-sent stage id (max+1000, zero rows) so the guard models the pathological world-state permanently instead of expiring when the live queue unblocks; control run of the old shape on that same id: 96,024 ms. — docs/04-features/crons.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-27 — "Keitaro visits unavailable" was firing on campaigns whose visits were sitting in Keitaro; three fixes, **no migration**. Investigation first: for every stage sent since 2026-08-24 the stored `visit_clicks_raw/clean` matches a live `report/build` call per `sub_id_3` — **zero mismatches** — and account-wide visit volume was normal (482 visit clicks by 10:14 ET vs 506 / 517 / 331 on the three prior days). Nothing was being lost; the marker was wrong. ⭐ THE DISPLAY HALF OF THE TRACKING-GAP FEATURE SHIPPED WITHOUT THE MATURITY GATE THE ALERT HALF HAS ALWAYS HAD — measured against mature sends, the Keitaro clean-visit rate is 1–5% of recipients, so a 9-to-200-recipient late-sequence resend is *expected* to read zero for hours; six campaigns carried the marker 30–90 minutes after send and five of them had 6–15 clean visits at campaign level. The rule is now one exported function, `shouldSubstituteClickers()`, sharing `TRACKING_GAP_MATURITY_HOURS` with the alert, and `scripts/verify-clickers-fallback.ts` CALLS it instead of transcribing its clauses — the FRESH fixture is byte-identical to the GAP fixture except `sent_at`, so deleting the maturity clause makes the two agree and the guard goes red. ⭐ A GROUPED ROW IS NOT `some()`: one substituted stage marked a whole campaign and blanked its CR% and Redirect%, so five campaigns lost both rate columns over a single 9-recipient resend; `substitutionDominates()` marks a row only when the substitute is the MAJORITY of its clicker figure, which at stage grain (`substituted === total`) is exactly the old behaviour. Marked campaign rows: 11 → 4 over 30 days, 2 → 0 for 2026-08-27. ⭐ SEPARATE, LARGER BUG FOUND IN THE SAME PULL: `clicks.classification` holds a FIRST-PASS UA-only guess until the scoring job's MaxMind lookup overwrites it, and `HUMAN_CLICK` in the counted-clicker cache tested `classification = 'human'` with no `scored_at IS NOT NULL` — which `propagate-clickers.ts` has always required. Because the incremental refresh is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, provisional verdicts could only be withdrawn by the once-a-day full rebuild, so the EPC denominator on EVERY screen inflated through each morning: stage 3309 held 620 counted clickers against 28 contacts actually classified human. The correction is confined to the current day — every unscored human click in all of history was clicked today — removing 5,223 stage-grain rows (106,263 → 101,040) and changing no historical figure. `HUMAN_CLICK` is now exported because `verify-counted-clickers.ts`'s "independent recomputation" had inlined its own copy and would have agreed with a stale definition forever. ⚠️ `scripts/verify-epc-surface-grains.ts` fails identically on clean `origin/main` (stale `offer_report_*_mv`) — pre-existing, untouched here. — docs/04-features/epc-denominator.md, docs/04-features/tracking-attribution.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-26 — Telegram notification schedule moved out of code into a per-org table and onto its own Settings page, `/settings/notifications` (**migration 0173**, authored on the branch as 0145 and renumbered — main had taken 0145 for `contact_org_stats` in the six days the branch sat in review). Four type toggles (daily / hourly / stall / unjoinable), daily hour, hourly window from-to, hourly interval (1/2/3 h) and an active-weekday set, all Warsaw time; manager+ to edit via `GET/PUT /api/settings/notifications`, any member can view. The cron loads the row once per tick and passes it to `decideFormat()`; no row → the exported defaults. ⭐ THE DEFAULTS ARE A TRANSCRIPTION OF THE SCHEDULE THEY REPLACE, NOT A FRESH OPINION — the table ships INERT and applying the migration changes nothing until someone edits the row. Two of them read as typos until you know that: `hourly_window_to` is **1**, not 23, because the live window was 16:00–01:59 (the old `hour === 0 || hour === 1` branch reads like a separate rule but is the tail of the same evening window, and `from > to` is what marks the wrap); and `active_weekdays` is **1–6**, not 1–7, because Sunday evening never sent. ⭐ One weekday set has to reproduce BOTH halves of the old pair `isoDow !== 7` (no Sunday evening) and `isoDow !== 1` (no Monday 00:00–01:59), which it only does if the window is matched against the day it STARTED on — `hourlyOwningDow()`. Gate on the wall-clock day instead and the sole symptom is two surplus reports every Monday at 00:00 and 01:00, on a cron nobody watches until it misfires. ⭐ `active_weekdays` gates the HOURLY window ONLY; the daily 10:00 summary has never been weekday-gated and still isn't (the branch as written gated both, which would have silently killed the Sunday daily report). ⭐ The guard is an EQUIVALENCE SWEEP, not spot checks: `scripts/test-telegram-report-decision.ts` transcribes the pre-0173 function verbatim and compares it against the shipped defaults across all 336 hour × weekday × test combinations, plus a control proving the sweep goes red when a default is nudged (importing the new implementation on both sides would have compared a thing to itself). Behaviour verified identical on all 336. ⭐ SEPARATE BUG, FOUND ONLY BY RUNNING THE STATEMENT: the branch's `PUT` interpolated `active_weekdays` into a Drizzle `sql` template, which FLATTENS a JS array into positional params — the insert went out with eight params for three placeholders and Postgres rejected it with 42804 (`column is of type smallint[] but expression is of type integer`). Every save would have 500'd, and `tsc`, `eslint` and `next build` were all green while that was true. The upsert now goes through the query builder, which binds the array as an array; the read/write moved to `lib/reporting/notification-settings.ts` so it is reachable without HTTP, and `scripts/test-notification-settings-persistence.ts` executes the REAL exported function against the live table inside a rolled-back transaction. — docs/03-data-model.md, docs/04-features/crons.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-26 — Stage creative picker gained Quality and Funnel Stage multi-select filters alongside the existing Sequence one ([components/campaigns/creative-picker-dialog.tsx](../components/campaigns/creative-picker-dialog.tsx)); no migration, no API change.
