All three filter client-side over the already-fetched set — `/api/creatives/list` returns `quality` and `funnel_stage` today, so `PickerCreative` just widened to carry them and nothing re-queries on a filter change. Options are derived from `QUALITY_VALUES` / `FUNNEL_STAGE_VALUES` in `lib/validators/creatives`, not retyped, so a new enum member appears in the filter automatically. ⭐ Release note, not a code note: this branch sat blocked for six days on an automated "type-check failed on the merged result" refusal that was actually a 30-minute timeout — the autopilot's `execArgv` kills the child and resolves `{code: 1}` with no marker, so a killed `tsc` and a failing `tsc` are indistinguishable at the gate, and the tsc output is discarded rather than logged. Verified before this merge: `tsc --noEmit` exits 0 on the merged result against both the current main and the main as it stood at the refusal, and a cold run takes 50 s. — docs/04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-25 — Sidebar nav grouped into COLLAPSIBLE blocks; the menu had outgrown one screen (27 rows after the Delivery report + the four new Settings pages). Group labels in [components/protected/sidebar-nav.tsx](../components/protected/sidebar-nav.tsx) are now toggle buttons — Reports / Audience / Registry / Settings start COLLAPSED, Campaigns is `collapsible: false` and always expanded (new optional field on `NavGroup`). Open/closed persists per browser in localStorage under `sidebar:groups` via the existing `usePersistedFilters`; an unknown or newly-added group falls back to the collapsed default, so adding a group never resurrects stale storage. ⭐ The group holding the current route auto-expands ONLY when `pathname` actually changes (ref-guarded) — unguarded, the effect re-fired on every render and instantly reopened a group the user had just collapsed, so the active group's own toggle looked dead; `tsc` and `eslint` were both GREEN while that bug was live, it existed only in the browser. Consequence of the guard: you CAN collapse the group you are currently in, and it reopens on your next navigation into it. Reports > Overview is DELIBERATELY duplicated as a row in the top group next to Dashboard for one-click access while Reports is collapsed — both rows carry `exact: true`, so both highlight on `/reports` and neither does on `/reports/<dimension>`. Desktop and the mobile `<Sheet>` drawer share one `<SidebarNav>`, so both changed. No migration, no API change — docs/04-features/ui-system.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-25 — `camman.exuma.io` added as a PARTNER-FACING second hostname on the same deployment; new optional `NEXT_PUBLIC_PARTNER_HOST` (no migration). The primary host (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`) keeps all machine traffic — auth emails, alert deep-links, provider webhooks. ⭐ Every outbound URL now resolves through one module, `lib/app-origin.ts`, and NONE of them read the request `Host`. The two provider registration routes (`register-callback`, `register-textrequest-hooks`) used to PREFER the request host, on the reasonable-sounding logic that an admin clicking Register is on a reachable origin — but the provider PERSISTS that URL, so it outlives the request: registering from a preview deployment pinned STOP delivery to a URL that later 404s, and a second serving hostname would have quietly moved opt-out traffic off the primary name. `appOrigin()` takes zero arguments (no request can reach it) and an unset `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` now fails LOUDLY instead of falling back. ⭐ The partner intake URL in Settings had the mirror-image bug via `window.location.origin` — copied from a preview tab it handed a partner a URL that dies with the deployment. `partnerBase(currentOrigin)` takes the browser origin as an ARGUMENT so `scripts/test-partner-host.ts` feeds the real shipped expression an old-host and a preview URL rather than re-typing the template literal (a retyped copy passes forever no matter what the component does). ⭐ Nothing errors in ANY of these cases — the URL looks fine and fails weeks later, which is why this is a guard and not a code-review note; the guard carries a can-go-red control proving it detects a reintroduced host read, and is wired into `npm run check:docs` (which `vercel-build` runs before `next build`). ⚠️ `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is inlined at BUILD time, so setting the var needs a redeploy; until then the intake URL falls back to the current origin (existing behavior). ⚠️ New convention: never register a serving hostname as an active short domain — `app/page.tsx` redirects a non-app host's bare root to the matching brand's website, so `exuma.io` as a short domain would bounce operators to a marketing site with no error. Recon also confirmed the app has NO Host allow-list, CORS layer, or origin/CSRF check, and auth cookies set no `domain` — adding a hostname is otherwise pure infrastructure — docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-24 — Public partner API docs at `/docs/partner-api`, rendered from the SAME modules the intake endpoint validates against (`lib/intake/fields.ts` + new `lib/intake/api-contract.ts`) — auth, endpoint shape, single/batch/curl examples, field table, all 7 response codes with the partner action for each, batching, 429 + `Retry-After`, sandbox flow, and a dated changelog. ⭐ The examples are DERIVED from `LEAD_FIELDS`, not typed out, so a new field appears in the example payload too, not just the table. ⭐ `scripts/test-partner-docs-drift.ts` RENDERS the page and parses its output — comparing `LEAD_FIELDS` to `LEAD_FIELDS` is a tautology that passes whatever the page does; it also probes acceptance through the real `canonicalFieldKey()` and asserts every documented ALIAS resolves to its own field. ⭐ MEASURED GOTCHA: page-vs-endpoint alone CANNOT catch a removed field — both sides move together (deleted `state`, whole suite stayed green). The committed markdown is the independent anchor that makes a removal visible. A comparison between two things derived from one source proves nothing. ⭐ There is no GitHub Actions in this repo, so the guards are wired into `npm run check:docs`, called by `vercel-build` BEFORE `next build` — a failure blocks the deploy and reds the PR. ⭐ The proxy exclusion is `docs/partner-api/?$` — anchored with `$`, NOT a trailing slash, because this is a LEAF route: a trailing slash would exclude only non-existent children and leave the page gated, while a bare prefix would swallow `/docs/partner-api-internal`. New convention: any PR changing intake fields/validation/limits must add an `API_CHANGELOG` entry in the same PR — docs/07-conventions.md, docs/04-features/partner-lead-intake.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md
2026-08-24 — Hourly Telegram report: the send no longer retries on an UNKNOWN outcome, so a slow Telegram can't post the report twice, and a delivered report is no longer alerted as failed (no migration). ⭐ A client-side timeout says the CLIENT gave up — it is not evidence the server didn't act; `sendMessage` is not idempotent and has no idempotency key, so the old "2 attempts, 8 s each, retry on any error" loop delivered the Warsaw 22:00 report twice and then alerted `⚠️ … failed. The operation was aborted due to timeout` (Node's `AbortSignal.timeout` reason string, verbatim). Retry is now gated on a `TelegramHttpError` proving non-delivery (429/5xx); no-response errors stop at one POST and return a third terminal state, `unknown` — 200 + a distinct `ℹ️ delivery is UNKNOWN` notice, NOT the `⚠️ failed` alert, which is kept for proven failures and hung metrics builds. Per-attempt timeout 8 s → 20 s via new optional `TELEGRAM_SEND_TIMEOUT_MS`; one log line per attempt (number, duration, outcome) since Vercel retains no runtime logs. Retry policy moved from the route into `sendTelegramReport` so it is testable without the DB — docs/04-features/crons.md, docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-24 — Drip P7: partner reporting (partner × interest tag), signed report links, and the journey funnel (migrations 0171/0172). ⭐ Lookup cost is charged at a rate CALIBRATED FROM THE TELNYX LEDGER over a trailing 90 days ($0.001635 from $1,002.84 across 613,494 lookups), never from the per-batch delta — that spread is 0×–3.9× and reads $0.00 for exactly the small drip batches (4/15 batches are 0.0000; concurrent batches share one `balance_before` snapshot). `actual_cost_usd` equals `est_cost_usd` in 15/15 rows and is not read. The report shows the rate AND its calibration window so an invoice is explainable; it fails toward the $0.0015 flat rate, never toward zero. ⭐ Report rows key off a UNION of both sources, not one COALESCE'd onto the other — the pre-0171 counter row sits under tag `''` while its sends carry `medicare`, and a coalesced join silently dropped every send. ⭐ Signed links are OPAQUE DB-RESOLVED TOKENS (24 random bytes, SHA-256 at rest, plaintext returned once), not signed HMACs, because revocation is the requirement: a signed token needs a denylist — i.e. the very lookup signing was meant to avoid. Scope comes from the key row, never the URL; unknown/revoked/expired/archived all resolve to null indistinguishably. ⭐ The `partner-report/` proxy exclusion is an EXACT PATH SEGMENT — the trailing slash is load-bearing, and the matcher's lookahead is anchored at the path root (so `/settings/partners` was never reachable, but `/partners` and `/partner-keys` were). scripts/test-public-route-scope.ts diffs the matcher against origin/main across every real page route and asserts `/partner-report/*` is the ONLY path whose behaviour changed. ⭐ R4: the Ignored lane is TERMINAL — it closes the journey `completed`/`unengaged` in the SAME transaction as the lane send, and `unengaged` stays distinct from `all_stages_sent` so a campaign's bad news can't merge into its good news. The funnel's two blocks deliberately do NOT sum to each other: progression is nested/cumulative, outcomes are disjoint — docs/03-data-model.md (no ERD change: neither `partner_keys` nor `lead_intake_daily` appears in the diagram), docs/04-features/drip-partner-reporting.md (new), docs/04-features/drip-campaigns-routing.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md

2026-08-24 — Alerts latch only after Telegram confirms delivery; a failed send now retries on the next tick instead of being lost permanently (no migration). Follow-up: the intake route's per-request auth-failure alert is now gated at the 5th failure and every hundredth thereafter, not `failures >= 5`, so a pending alert can't be amplified 1:1 with request rate — docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/04-features/partner-lead-intake.md, docs/04-features/tracking-attribution.md
2026-08-24 — Tracking-gap monitor final-review fixes: shared `hasNoKeitaroVisits()` predicate (both visit columns) between the alert and the Overview display fallback — the clean-only display test had wrongly marked 56 of 58 flagged stages; `clearAlert` now passes org_id so latch rows don't go stale-NULL; tracking-monitors heartbeat wired into `/api/cron/tells-monitors`'s dead-man check — docs/04-features/tracking-attribution.md, docs/04-features/crons.md (blank-line table fix)
2026-08-24 — Keitaro tracking-gap monitor (hourly cron, per-stage latch) + read-time CamMan clickers fallback on the Overview tab — docs/04-features/tracking-attribution.md, docs/06-integrations.md, docs/04-features/crons.md, docs/07-conventions.md
2026-08-22 — Drip P1 1b: offer landing pages + MINT-TIME destination construction (migrations 0150/0151). A stage stores WHICH PAGE (landing_page_id) and the URL is built when links are minted, from the campaign's brand at that moment -- so re-branding a campaign self-corrects its links. Root cause: on 2026-08-22 campaigns 902 and 923 were re-branded in production and every stage kept the OLD brand's pages, because the destination was a frozen absolute URL. NEW brands.landing_host (backfilled) -- brands.website is unnormalized (mixed www/trailing slash) and is consumed verbatim by root-redirect. UTM tags are NEVER appended to /lp/ destinations (single-param rule; the one configured tag emits the literal subid3=sub_id3, the placeholder defect). Rebrand is allowed but BLOCKS approval of stages whose number no longer matches the brand (write-time only) -- closes the 1a gap. NULL landing_page_id = today's behaviour, no backfill — docs/03-data-model.md (+ERD), docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md
2026-08-22 — Drip P1 1c completed: /contacts/[id] detail page (the first per-contact page — /contacts was a list only), attribute CSV import with saved column->field mapping templates (migration 0149 contact_attribute_import_mappings), and nine contact_attributes segment rule types (migration 0148 widened segment_rules_rule_type_check 23->32). ⭐ A rule type must be registered in SEVEN places, not the four documented: RULE_TYPES, validateValueByShape, isRuleComplete, verifyValueOwnership, the SQL emitter, the DB CHECK, and its db/schema.ts mirror — scripts/test-segment-rule-type-registration.ts now asserts the three list-shaped ones agree. The import UPDATES existing contacts and never creates one. 1970-01-01 epoch-as-blank normalizes to NULL at the write boundary — docs/03-data-model.md (+ERD), docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md
2026-08-22 — contact_attributes (migration 0147): 1:1 with contacts, PK=contact_id, org-scoped SELECT-only RLS, 5 indexes. AGE IS NEVER STORED — age bands are a dob RANGE (ET calendar date), never a per-row age(), so the dob index stays sargable. Under-18 is scoped to the age_band rule (NULL dob matches nothing + a hard 18y floor); a GLOBAL minor gate is deliberately NOT built (0 of 815,426 contacts have a dob, so 'unknown = minor' would zero every audience). 1970-01-01 epoch-as-blank is normalized to NULL at the write boundary — the CHECK cannot catch it. No column added to contacts, no backfill — docs/03-data-model.md (+ERD), docs/CHANGELOG.md
2026-08-22 — Test-fixture rules after a live-data mutation during a prod smoke test: never mutate a live/active-campaign entity as a fixture (prove behaviour with a request that must be REJECTED, or use an archived entity with 0 send rows), and any smoke test that must WRITE runs against the camman-v2 preview DB, never production. Also records the trap that caused it — a 2xx is not evidence the guard ran, since a grandfathered path returns the same 200 as a working allow — docs/07-conventions.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md
2026-08-21 — Brand -> sending number enforced at WRITE time (Drip Phase 1 item 1a): a stage's `provider_phone_id` and a campaign's `default_provider_phone_id` must belong to the campaign's brand. One guard (`lib/api/brand-number-guard.ts`) called from 5 write paths incl. stage duplicate; grandfathered via `pairIsChanging` so existing mismatched pairs stay editable; absent brand on a number = shared/allowed. Both phone-list endpoints take an optional `brand_id`. NOT enforced at send and no DB constraint — 16 legacy stages incl. 33,578 pending rows are deliberately untouched. Also corrected the RUN_PREVIEW_MIGRATIONS scope note (now Preview-only) — docs/07-conventions.md, docs/preview-environment.md, docs/CHANGELOG.md
2026-08-21 — Only ONE Vercel project may run `db:migrate` on preview builds: `vercel-build` now also requires `RUN_PREVIEW_MIGRATIONS=1` (set on project `camman` -> Preview). Two projects build every PR commit and both raced to migrate the shared camman-v2 database; observed on PR #108 (migration 0146), where redeploying the identical commit passed. Also documents that a preview build alone applies a migration to the demo DB, and closes the campaign_circuit_events RLS "Known gap" (fixed by 0146) — docs/preview-environment.md (first committed here; was untracked), docs/CHANGELOG.md
2026-08-21 — Text Request messages poll walks inbound BEFORE outbound: the per-direction page budget did not protect STOP intake from the shared 60s function budget — docs/06-integrations.md, docs/04-features/crons.md
2026-08-21 — Text Request messages poll: page_size 500->1000 (TR clamps at 1000), explicit newest-first `sort=desc` with an unsorted fallback, and outbound/inbound walked separately so a large campaign cannot crowd STOP replies out of the page budget — docs/06-integrations.md, docs/04-features/crons.md
2026-08-19 — Reactivate from completed: added "Reactivate" to the campaigns-list 3-dots menu and fixed bulk-status to allow completed → active — docs/04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives.md
2026-08-19 — Completed campaigns can now be reactivated (completed → active); `completed` is no longer a terminal status — docs/04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives.md

2026-08-19 - Segment purchase rules + behavioural converted tier now use the same sale definition as the reports - docs/04-features/audience-segments.md, docs/04-features/behavioral-lanes.md, docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- ROOT CAUSE: `stage_sends.sale_status` stores the network's raw Keitaro postback status. The network fires `lead` for PAID conversions and effectively never `sale` (only 2 `sale` rows org-wide, both stage 587 on 2026-06-24). The reporting paths already compensate - lib/keitaro/poll.ts counts every conversion row as a sale, lib/reporting/rollup.ts uses `converted_at IS NOT NULL` - but lib/segment-rules-eval.ts and lib/campaign-tier.ts tested `sale_status = 'sale'`.
- IMPACT: the `Buyers_ALL` segment resolved to **2** contacts against **835** real buyers; `Clickers excl Buyers` excluded 2 instead of 837, so ~825 known buyers were being targeted as non-buyers. Campaign-tier lane 3 (Converted) was unreachable entirely. All 852 `lead` rows carry real payout revenue ($57,168 total, avg $67.10) - they are buyers.
- FIX: new `lib/sale-attribution.ts` exports `purchasedClause()` = `sale_status IN ('lead','sale')` as the single source of truth; applied to the three `made_purchase*` rules and the converted tier. `rejected` is deliberately excluded (refund/chargeback). No migration - the existing partial index `stage_sends_sale_status_idx` still serves it.
- VERIFIED via scripts/verify-purchase-rule-definition.ts against production through the REAL eval path (`buildSegmentAudienceClause`, `campaignTierExpr`): made_purchase 835 == live non-rejected-conversion count 835; Buyers_ALL 2 -> 835; Clickers excl Buyers 55,684 -> 54,857; campaign 755 now has 17 contacts at tier 3.
- GOTCHA (cost me a false PASS): an in-transaction assertion that reads through the global `db` pool lands on a DIFFERENT connection and cannot see the open transaction, so both sides of the comparison were trivially 0 and the bar passed for the wrong reason. Only the deliberate `rejected` -> `lead` control caught it. In-transaction reads must go through `tx`.
- Also updated: scripts/test-purchase-rules.ts expectations (P1 8->9, P2/P4 7->8; 'lead' now counts, 'rejected'/NULL still must not), the mirror query in scripts/test-segment-intersect-and-optout.ts (3 sites - it recomputes the rule's SQL against REAL org data and would have gone red), and the campaign activity badge, which showed inline revenue only for `sale` rows.
- NOT changed: scripts/probe-misclassification-targeting-scope.ts still uses `= 'sale'`. It is a closed-incident forensic probe whose numbers should stay reproducible as originally reported.

- Audited production for leftover test data. Everything created by the Q3/Q4/Q5 verification work was synthesized in transactions and rolled back - re-confirmed: 0 phone_carrier_limits rows, 0 'q5 probe' stage_sends, 0 number-level footers, 0 probe short domains, 0 test credentials.
- What HAD leaked was the `Stage Test *` cluster from interrupted runs of scripts/test-campaign-stages-api.ts (created 2026-08-18 09:14-10:57 UTC): 1 brand, 4 affiliate_networks, 4 offers, 4 segments, 4 contact_groups, 8 creatives + 8 creative_offers, 10 contacts, 10 segment_contacts. Nothing real referenced any of it (0 campaigns, 0 stages, 0 sends, 0 offer_exposures). Removed via scripts/cleanup-stage-test-fixtures.ts.
- Also removed sms_providers #948 'tls-t' (archived, 0 phones/credentials/stages/sends/opt-outs/circuit-events - the CASCADE deleted nothing). verify-adapter-code.ts and verify-provider-sends-enabled-migration.ts both re-run ALL PASS without it; migration 0135's backfill becomes a no-op and its guard is unaffected (it counts supports_api_send rows with NULL adapter_code).
- NEAR-MISS WORTH RECORDING: the fixture script mints contacts as +15107<unique><nn>, and `phone_number LIKE '+15107%'` matches 451 REAL contacts in area code 510 (Oakland), 400 of which have genuinely sent messages. A pattern-based cleanup would have destroyed live customer data. The cleanup deletes BY EXPLICIT ID ONLY and asserts that no target contact has ever had a stage_sends row.

Root cause was not captured (the error was truncated); the script now prints full errors, and its pre-flight was made re-runnable so it could finish its own job. Second apply: all types 0 remaining.
- GOTCHA: `ANY(${jsArray})` does not work through Drizzle's sql template - the array is flattened into positional params and postgres-js throws ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE; a ::int[] cast does not help. Render a validated literal list instead.
- Post-cleanup state verified: fixture families all 0; real data intact (811,921 contacts, 7 providers, 3 brands, 26 offers, 259 campaigns, 3,281,300 stage_sends, 141,273 opt_outs).

2026-08-18 - W2 rollup layer: contact_org_stats (migration 0145), segment audience single-eval — docs/04-features/w2-rollup-layer.md added
2026-08-18 - Minted-link count restored on /settings/short-domains (migration 0144) + stale-guard sweep - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- Migration 0144 adds links.short_domain_id (non-partial btree). #88 removed the per-domain minted-link count because links (3.28M rows) had nothing covering that column, making each count a parallel seq scan of the whole table.
- MEASURED, in-database, same query and same brand: BEFORE 12,574 ms (Seq Scan on links, 3.28M rows, once per domain); AFTER 623 ms warm (Index Only Scan using links_short_domain_id_idx). Through the real listBrandShortDomains(), slowest brand 731 ms local wall-clock - which includes ~400 ms of round-trip latency to eu-central-1, visible as brands with ZERO links costing about as much as brands with thousands. The deployed app runs in fra1, next to the database.
- Built CONCURRENTLY in 9.8s via scripts/apply-links-short-domain-index-concurrent.ts BEFORE db:migrate (links is written on every tracked send). verify-migration-integrity: chain clean through 0144.
- The count is back in the UI and pre-disables Delete again - as a COURTESY only. deleteShortDomain still re-checks server-side and refuses with domain_in_use, and that refusal is still surfaced verbatim, because the count can be stale by a link minted between the fetch and the click.
- The cost scales with LINK VOLUME, not domain count (gdkn.org alone carries 3,277,863 links). If it becomes slow again, CAP it (exact up to N, "N+" beyond) rather than removing it a second time - and re-measure, do not assume.
- STALE-GUARD SWEEP. scripts/verify-provider-sends-enabled-migration.ts was ALREADY RED on clean main: it asserted opt_out_footer IS NULL on every provider row, and Q3 went live with tls carrying "Reply STOP to quit". scripts/verify-q3-optout-footer.ts asserted the number-level column "ships NULL everywhere", which the per-number footer UI shipped this session makes a countdown.
- Both rewritten the same way: REPORT the live configuration; ASSERT the durable invariant instead (not "nobody has set a footer" but "anything set is sendable" - a footer with no STOP keyword REFUSES every stage on that account; not "sends_enabled is true everywhere" but "sends_enabled is a real boolean everywhere", which keeps IS NOT FALSE and = true agreeing across the six enforcement sites); and PROVE IT CAN GO RED from synthesized state in a ROLLED-BACK transaction, re-querying afterwards to confirm nothing survived. The durable form is stronger than the one it replaced in both cases.
- Convention recorded: a guard that goes red on correct use is a countdown, not a guard. Never bend the data back to satisfy a stale assertion - retire it and replace it with the invariant.
2026-08-18 - Per-number opt-out footer gets its UI (the 0141 column) - docs/07-conventions.md
- provider_phones.opt_out_footer shipped with Q3 (0141) and had NO UI for two phases: the chain's most specific slot existed but nothing could fill it. It is now a field on the number's Edit dialog.
- Built on the short-domain-override pattern: leaving it empty INHERITS, and the inherited value is SHOWN as the placeholder together with the account's name, rather than implied. The description names which level currently wins. When the connection type appends its own opt-out wording the field is disabled and says so - nothing stored anywhere would be sent. /api/providers/[providerId] now returns appends_own_opt_out, derived from the descriptor exactly as /api/providers/list does.
- "" and whitespace mean NO PREFERENCE, never an empty footer: an empty box submits null so the column clears and the chain falls through. Storing "" would read as set and behave as unset. Enforced in the validator (trim -> null) and asserted at source level.
- The stage form already ranked the number's footer into its preview and already NAMED the winning level via describeOptOutFooterLevel ("this sending number"); both are now source-guarded so they cannot regress silently.
- PROOF (scripts/verify-per-number-opt-out-footer.ts, end-to-end value SYNTHESIZED IN A ROLLED-BACK TRANSACTION, zero live configuration): a number-level value wins over the account and the stage and the rendered body carries it verbatim; the winning level is named as the sending number; clearing falls back to the account and then to the stage; "" and whitespace do not win; the COMPLIANCE GATE refuses a non-compliant number-level footer even when the account and stage are both compliant, and accepts a compliant one when neither lower level is. End to end on number #224 against the REAL tls account footer "Reply STOP to quit" - the synthesized value out-ranked it, and clearing handed the chain back to it. Rollback re-queried: 0 numbers carry a footer, same as before the run.
- The end-to-end target is chosen as a number whose ACCOUNT already carries a footer, and non-vacuity is asserted out loud: with an arbitrary number the account side is usually NULL and the bar would reduce to "a value beats nothing".
- Verified in a browser: the field shows "Reply STOP to quit  (from Tells)" as its placeholder and "Leave empty to use Tells's opt-out text"; typing a value flips the description to "This number's own opt-out text REPLACES the account's and the stage's". Cancelled without saving; re-queried prod: 0 of 35 numbers carry a footer.

2026-08-18 - Q5: per-carrier DAILY CAP enforced in the drain (migration 0143) - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- phone_carrier_limits.daily_limit (shipped inert with 0142) becomes enforceable. ET CALENDAR day, resetting at midnight America/New_York - NOT a rolling 24h, and deliberately distinct from the existing rate_24h rolling ceiling on the provider ACCOUNT. Separate stopReason (carrier_daily_cap) so the two never send an operator to the wrong control.
- Checked ONCE PER BATCH at the batch gate, before the claim, beside rate_minute/rate_24h. SOFT: absent from isHardStop, rows stay pending, next tick resumes. Surgical: fires only for a capped carrier that still has PENDING rows on the stage, so an exhausted cap on one carrier cannot halt a stage whose remaining rows are on another.
- BOUNDED OVERSHOOT, DOCUMENTED: mid-batch accounting is explicitly NOT built, so a carrier can exceed its limit by at most batchSize-1 (49 by default) on the batch that crosses it. DrainResult gains carrierCapDetail naming which carrier ran out and by how much.
- Migration 0143 adds the supporting partial index stage_sends (provider_phone_id, carrier_norm, sent_at) WHERE status = 'sent'. Built CONCURRENTLY on prod in 19.0s via scripts/apply-carrier-day-index-concurrent.ts, then db:migrate recorded it (the plain CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS no-opped, as designed). verify-migration-integrity: chain clean through 0143.
- UI: the number's Carrier policy section gains a per-carrier daily-cap box, hidden when the carrier is switched off. The summary names the ET calendar day and the soft ceiling.
- PROOF (scripts/verify-q5-carrier-daily-cap.ts, synthesized in a ROLLED-BACK transaction, zero live configuration and zero live sends): (0) an uncapped number reports no breach and a numberless stage is a literal no-op; (1) three sends just after ET midnight and three just before it move the counter by EXACTLY +3 (995 -> 998) where a rolling-24h counter would move it 6, and reaching the cap exactly is a breach; (2) an uncapped carrier is unaffected, one short of the cap does not stop, and a reached cap with no pending rows does not stop the stage; (3) carrier_daily_cap is not a hard stop; (4) EXPLAIN shows an Index Only Scan using stage_sends_phone_carrier_sent_day_idx. Fault injections 1-3 green. Rollback verified against a before-inventory.
- TWO HARNESS DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED, both general: (a) bar (1) first asserted sent_today === 3 and read 998, because number #224 had really sent 995 Verizon messages that day - the counter was right and the assertion assumed a clean slate; every claim is now a DELTA against real traffic. (b) the guard forbidding the functional day-boundary form failed against correct code because it matched carrier-policy.ts's own comment SAYING the boundary is never written that way - a checker must not read the prose about itself; strip comments first.
- Also fixed: the probe took its org from an unordered SELECT id FROM organizations LIMIT 1 and could name a different org than the stage it wrote against. It returned two different values on two consecutive runs, which is how it was noticed. Scope now comes from the subject.
- NOT PROVEN BY EXECUTION: an actual drain resuming on the next tick. Running one sends real SMS. Resume is established structurally instead - the check breaks BEFORE the claim (asserted by source order), so no row is claimed, and the reason is soft.

2026-08-18 - Q4 UI: the number-level carrier controls, and the AND statement on both screens - docs/07-conventions.md
- The Edit-number dialog gains a Carrier policy section: a per-carrier switch for each of the five NAMED_CARRIERS (AT&T, T-Mobile, Verizon, Other Mobile, VoIP) plus ONE switch for unknown carriers, which governs all three unknown-ish buckets together. A live summary line names what the number will not text.
- WRITE PATH: both halves travel in the existing phone PATCH and land in ONE db.transaction - the column and a replace-all of phone_carrier_limits. carrier_limits is pulled out before the column-update loop (it is a child table), and the empty-updates early return now accounts for a carrier_limits-only patch so an allow-list edit that touches no phone column still writes. Rows that neither deny nor cap are filtered out before insert: allowed-with-no-cap is identical to no row.
- READ PATH: the phones list returns allow_unknown_carrier and the aggregated carrier_limits with each phone, so the dialog hydrates from ONE request. A second fetch on dialog-open would race defaultValues - exactly how the short-domain override used to clear itself on open and then DELETE itself on save.
- The AND statement is now on BOTH screens (campaign audience filter + number Carrier policy), source-guarded on both.
- CAUGHT IN A BROWSER, NOT BY THE GUARD: the first version put the campaign-side sentence in components/campaigns/campaign-form-fields.tsx and the guard went green - but BOTH campaign routes render campaign-editor-page.tsx, which carries its own copy of the carrier filter. CampaignFormFields is DEAD render code (campaign-form.tsx is imported only for its AudienceFilters type), so the sentence was on a screen no operator can reach. The guard now targets the live editor AND asserts that app/(protected)/campaigns/new/page.tsx still renders CampaignEditorPage, so the pointer cannot rot silently. A source guard names a FILE; only the route proves it is the SCREEN.
- Q4 guard made DURABLE: it no longer asserts "phone_carrier_limits ships EMPTY" / "allow_unknown_carrier true everywhere". Those expire the first time the feature is used. It now REPORTS the live configuration and asserts what stays true - every stored row names a carrier in the normalized vocabulary, no stored row is a no-op, CARRIER_NORMS still matches the DB check constraint, and the rollback left the table exactly as the run found it (compared against a before-inventory, not against zero).
- PROOF (all synthesized in a ROLLED-BACK transaction; zero live configuration): (0) unconfigured number excludes nobody, 765,777 = 765,777; (1) restricting Verizon on number #24 gives 765,777 -> 457,506, delta 0, 0 leaked, number #1 unaffected; (2) preflight breakdown on stage 65 reports {"Verizon":60}; (3) AND-composition never widens and a campaign selecting a carrier the number forbids yields 0; (4) allow_unknown_carrier=false drops all three unknown buckets plus NULLs (8,546). Fault injections 1-3 green. 12 source guards green, and the new ones were shown to discriminate (they go false on files that lack the sentence/key).

2026-08-18 - Q3 bar (B) split by world-state; the go-live cohort asserts byte-identity - docs/07-conventions.md
- Bar (B) of scripts/verify-q3-optout-footer.ts went RED the moment Q3 went live, on the first 997 real tls sends. The product was correct; the ASSERTION modeled the pre-chain world. It demanded that a body on a footer-configured account differ from today's build only by a swapped footer substring - true of rows rendered BEFORE the chain shipped, false of rows rendered after, whose stored bytes already carry the resolved footer.
- FIX: bar (B) splits into two cohorts on an EXTERNAL boundary, Q3_LIVE_AT = 2026-08-18T11:01:47Z (the prod deployment of 7ec9c4a, GitHub deployment 5961313557, state success). Pre-chain keeps the swap-model; post-chain asserts plain byte-identity. Both cohort sizes are printed on every run, an empty cohort is reported as NOT OBSERVABLE rather than as a pass, and bar-B non-vacuity is itself a check().
- The discriminator is the RENDER time (stage_sends.created_at), not sent_at: rendered_text is INSERTed once at materialization and only READ at drain. 667 corpus rows were rendered before go-live and sent after it - the rows a sent_at split would misclassify. That count is measured and printed every run.
- Fault injections #5/#6/#7 added: byte-identity is shown to FAIL on a pre-chain body and the swap-model to FAIL on a post-chain one, from synthesized bodies so the proof holds when a cohort is empty.
- Re-run: ALL PASS. Corpus 31,207 actually-sent rows (grew from 30,795 mid-session - live sending continues); bar A 30,669 rows / 0 mismatches / 0 excluded; bar B pre-chain 0 (NOT OBSERVABLE), post-chain 1,009 rows / 0 mismatches.
- Convention recorded: a corpus assertion must name the world-state it models and select its cohort accordingly; a bar that cannot survive its own feature going live is modeling the past.
2026-08-18 - Q4: per-number carrier allow-list, enforced at materialization (migration 0142) - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- New phone_carrier_limits table (allowed + daily_limit, one row per number+carrier) and provider_phones.allow_unknown_carrier (default true). MACHINERY ONLY: table ships EMPTY, boolean true on all 35 numbers. ABSENT ROW = ALLOWED AND UNCAPPED, so the shipped state is a byte-for-byte no-op - and that is asserted as bar (0) of the guard, not assumed.
- Enforced at MATERIALIZATION via a single shared clause (lib/sends/carrier-policy.ts) wired into the recipients builder, so an excluded contact never becomes a stage_sends row. Passed by kickoff, preflight, the preflight breakdown AND the CSV export - the export especially, since an exported row is a manual send and omitting it there would route the restriction around itself.
- The clause is an ANTI-JOIN (NOT EXISTS a disallowing row), never a membership test: reading absence as denial would mute every number in the org.
- Preflight breakdown gains excluded.carrier, a per-carrier map rather than one total.
- PROOF (scripts/verify-q4-carrier-allowlist.ts, all synthesized in a ROLLED-BACK transaction, zero live configuration): (0) unconfigured number excludes nobody (765,777 = 765,777); (1) restricting Verizon on number #24 gives 765,777 -> 457,506, exactly the expected delta, 0 leaked, and another number is unaffected; (2) the preflight breakdown on real stage 65 reports {"Verizon":60}; (3) AND-composition never widens, and a campaign selecting a carrier the number forbids yields 0; (4) allow_unknown_carrier=false drops all three unknown-ish buckets plus NULLs (8,546). Three fault injections: a non-existent carrier changes nothing, an allowed=true row equals no row, and the comparison is proven sensitive. Rollback verified: 0 policy rows remain.
- No UI this run - see the open list; nothing is configured on any live number.

2026-08-18 - Corpus harnesses pinned to ACTUALLY-SENT rows (Q3 hard-stop follow-up) - docs/07-conventions.md
- The Q3 post-deploy re-run reported 77 byte-identical mismatches and the run hard-stopped. Root cause was the HARNESS, not the product: the corpus predicate was `rendered_text IS NOT NULL`, which also matches materialized-but-never-sent rows. Stage 2873 had a 77-recipient batch materialized, RECALLED (send_aborted, status='rejected', sent_at NULL), then re-pointed at a different creative and re-materialized at 3,400. Re-deriving those drafts from the stage's CURRENT creative produced phantom diffs. Footer line differed on 0 of 70,125 rows; link line on 0. Confirmed via campaign_events, not inference.
- FIX (pin, not snapshot - no schema change): both corpus harnesses now filter `sent_at IS NOT NULL`. Measured 75,125 materialized vs 29,396 actually sent in the same window. A second layer decomposes any residual mismatch: a difference confined to the brand+creative line is EXCLUDED as input drift and REPORTED with a count and reason; a difference in the link or footer line is never excused. Exclusions are printed alongside the result.
- A timestamp pin was the original plan and is NOT available: there is no updated_at on creatives, campaign_stages, brands or short_domains.
- Re-run post-deploy against current prod: Q3 harness ALL PASS (29,396 rows, 0 mismatches, 0 excluded); B2 harness ALL PASS (29,396 rows, segment distribution unchanged, 985 tracked stages).
- Convention recorded: corpus harnesses re-derive from FROZEN inputs only; a live FK an operator can edit is not a valid comparison source; exclusions are output; corpus size is re-stated every run.

2026-08-18 - Q3: per-provider opt-out footer chain goes LIVE (migration 0141) - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- Precedence, resolved by ONE function (lib/sends/opt-out-footer.ts): provider_phones.opt_out_footer (new, 0141) > sms_providers.opt_out_footer (0138) > campaign_stages.stop_text > 'Stop to END'. Creative excluded. descriptor.defaultOptOutFooter is a UI seed only, never a runtime candidate. descriptor.appendsOwnOptOut out-ranks everything and makes CamMan append NOTHING (mechanism shipped, no adapter sets it).
- THE GATE NOW VALIDATES THE WINNER. Before the chain, the rendered body always carried stop_text so checking the body and checking stop_text were the same thing; they are not any more. optOutGateSubject returns the rendered body, or the provider's KNOWN appended text when the provider appends - and fails CLOSED when a type claims to append but declares no known wording (no dry-run carve-out: an operator can fix missing stage wording, but not "this type appends something we have never seen").
- Migration 0141 ships NULL on all 35 numbers, no UI this run (per-number editing is Q4/Q5).
- Tells ("Reply STOP to quit", providers 855 + 948) goes LIVE - 2 tracked stages change wording. Everything else is untouched.
- PROOF (scripts/verify-q3-optout-footer.ts): (A) all 29,917 corpus rows re-derive BYTE-IDENTICAL, 0 mismatches; (B) reported NOT OBSERVABLE over the corpus (no historical send went through a footer-configured provider, so 0-of-0 would be vacuous) and carried instead by (B'), which renders 4 live tls stages before/after and confirms substring-only difference plus the gate passing on the NEW text; (C) a compliant stage field CANNOT rescue a non-compliant winning footer; (D) preview body and count match the send path under an override. Four fault injections, one of which caught a bad constant in my own probe (150-char base put BOTH footers over 160, so the "boundary crossing" tested two points on the same side - corrected to 145).
- The stage form now composes from the resolved footer and NAMES the winning level when the stage loses, so nobody edits a field that will not ship. The provider panel's "stored but not yet applied" caveat is gone; the field says it is live and that it must contain STOP.
- RIDER: deleted 3 orphaned "Stage Test Brand" rows (372/373/374) after verifying 0 references across ALL 8 FK columns to brands, with a post-delete re-query. These were left by MY earlier run of test-campaign-stages-api, whose cleanup crashed on `where id = undefined` and - having only a finally, no catch - aborted every remaining delete including brands. That suite's teardown now runs each step independently, validates every id, and verifies residue across ALL SEVEN entity types it creates (surviving rows fail the run). New convention recorded.

2026-08-18 - B2: segment length recalculated from the RESOLVED short domain - docs/07-conventions.md
- The tracked link sits INSIDE the counted SMS body, so the host length moves the GSM-7 segment boundary (gdkn.org 8 chars vs g.guidekn.com 13). Preview and kickoff each built the link themselves and disagreed twice: the preview used the BRAND's domain (blind to the stage's per-number override) and hardcoded the code length as the literal "XXXXXXX" with a comment promising it equalled CODE_LENGTH.
- New lib/links/tracked-link.ts is the single source of truth: buildTrackedLinkUrl / buildRepresentativeTrackedLinkUrl / pickEffectiveShortDomain / TRACKED_CODE_LENGTH. PURE and CLIENT-SAFE - mint-link.ts imports node:crypto + nanoid and cannot be pulled into a "use client" component, which is why the form had its own copy; mint-link now imports the constant from here.
- Precedence (number override > brand default > oldest active) is expressed ONCE. resolveShortDomainForSend runs the DB lookups then calls the shared ranker; the stage form gets both candidates over the API and calls the same function.
- Two API surfaces corrected to match the send path: campaign-detail brand.short_domain now orders is_default DESC (it ignored is_default, so a non-oldest default would have made the preview count against a different host); provider-phones list gained `short_domain`, the number's own ACTIVE override resolved to a string, without which the preview could not see the override at all.
- PROOF (scripts/verify-b2-segment-length.ts): all 29,917 corpus rows since the cutover re-derive BYTE-IDENTICAL, 0 mismatches, segment distribution unchanged (1seg:29,917 -> 1seg:29,917); a host change alters the body by exactly the domain substring (gdkn.org 104ch/1seg -> g.guidekn.com 109ch/1seg, delta 5ch); preview and send path resolve the same domain, body and segment count for all 964 tracked stages, 2 of which sit on a number WITH an active override (non-vacuous). Three fault injections all fire.
- CORPUS NOTE: the handoff described a single-domain corpus (txh 4,631 + txh2 40,120 under gdkn.org). Actual at run time: 29,917 rows across TWO hosts - gdkn.org 29,667 and g.lumzen.co 250 - because live tracked sending on a longer host had already happened via txr. Measured, not assumed.
- No schema change.

2026-08-18 - Post-ship fixes: brand fanout, phone override rehydrate, domain-page feedback + 77x load - docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- BUG 1 (brand duplication): /api/brands/list joined short_domains with a plain LEFT JOIN whose one-row-per-brand property rested on short_domains_brand_id_uniq - the index migration 0136 dropped. Guide Kin (2 domain rows) came back TWICE, so it appeared twice in every brand dropdown across NINE consumers of that endpoint, data.length (4) disagreed with totalCount (3), and LIMIT/OFFSET paged over duplicated rows. Fixed with leftJoinLateral(... LIMIT 1): cardinality is now structural, not index-dependent. A correlated sub-select is NOT usable in a single-FROM-table Drizzle query - it renders ${brands.id} unqualified and silently returns NULL; LATERAL supplies the second relation that makes the correlation legal.
- The same column also had NO status filter, harmless until B1 added `pending` - it could hand a never-mintable host to the campaign form's SMS preview. It now returns the EFFECTIVE domain (active only, explicit default first, then oldest), matching resolveShortDomainForSend.
- AUDIT of every query touching brands or short_domains: exactly ONE fanout site existed (brands/list). Every other join is X -> brands.id, many-to-one on a PK, which cannot fan out. Reported in full in the PR, including two correct-but-notable reads.
- BUG 2 (per-phone override didn't persist): the write ALWAYS landed (phone 224 really held short_domain_id=30). The Edit-phone modal's "clear a stale override" effect ran on mount with domains=[], concluded the saved id "isn't in the options", and wiped it - every open. Worse than cosmetic: the cleared NULL was a real form value, so pressing Save DELETED the override. Fixed by tracking which brand the loaded options describe, so "not loaded yet" is distinguishable from "loaded and absent". Proven by A/B in a real browser on the same DB row: without the fix the field reads "Brand default", with it "g.guidekn.com".
- BUG 3 + BUG 4 share a root cause. listBrandShortDomains selected a per-domain minted-link count over `links` (3,227,905 rows, NO index covering short_domain_id) - a parallel seq scan each, ~3.1s. One brand's list: 6,822ms vs 48ms without it; the page fetched brands SERIALLY, so ~20,500ms before anything painted, which is why a successful "Make default" click looked like it did nothing. The count was never load-bearing (deleteShortDomain re-checks server-side and refuses with domain_in_use), so it is dropped; fetches are now parallel; and mutations patch local state immediately instead of waiting on a refetch. Page data fetch measured 20,500ms -> 267ms.
- Restoring the count would need an index on links(short_domain_id) - deliberately NOT created; recommended as a separate migration if the figure is wanted back.
- Docs: 06-integrations.md said the STOP poller filters sms_provider_id IN ('txh','txh2'); it filters adapter_code = 'txh' since migration 0134.

2026-08-17 - Brand short domains: pending + DB-enforced default, and the write path rebuilt (migration 0140, B1) - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- FIXED A PRODUCTION DEFECT: saving a brand's short domain had 500'd since migration 0136. applyBrandShortDomain ended in ON CONFLICT (brand_id) DO UPDATE while 0136 dropped short_domains_brand_id_uniq, the index that conflict target infers from (Postgres 42P10). Its clear branch also ran DELETE ... WHERE org_id AND brand_id, which post-0136 wipes EVERY domain of the brand. scripts/verify-brand-domains.ts had been red on main since that merge and nobody re-ran it.
- The helper is retired. Brand domains are list-shaped, so the write surface is targeted operations, each keyed on ONE row id: add (always lands `pending`, conflict target (org_id, domain), REFUSES a taken hostname rather than adopting it), activate/deactivate (deactivate also clears is_default), set-default (refuses a pending row; clears the prior default in the same tx), delete (BY ID ONLY, minted-links guard preserved).
- NO BRAND-WIDE DELETE PATH REMAINS, and verify-brand-domains.ts asserts that at SOURCE level repo-wide (778 files scanned) with a self-test proving the scanner is not blind. The probe string is assembled from parts because as one literal it made the guard file its own offender.
- The brand form's single "Short domain" field is removed and the brand create/update routes no longer write the table (the key is dropped, not applied), so no second write path survives. Management moves to Settings -> Short domains.
- Migration 0140: 'pending' joins the status vocabulary; is_default + partial unique index short_domains_one_default_per_brand enforce AT MOST one default per brand in the DATABASE; the flag is backfilled onto the domain the pre-0140 rule already picked; g.guidekn.com / g.lumzen.co / g.fitsyou.net inserted as PENDING (matched by brands.brand_id, idempotent). NOT activated - that stays a deliberate operator act.
- Resolution extracted to lib/sends/resolve-short-domain.ts (per-number override > brand default > oldest active), byte-identical across the full 108-pair phone x brand matrix versus a separately transcribed copy of the old rule; a pending domain assigned as a per-number override is proven to fall through and never mint.
- ⚠️ Brands 142 (LumZen) and 143 (FitsYou) have no active domain at all; activating theirs enables tracked sending for those brands for the FIRST time.

2026-08-17 - Two stale send-path guards repaired (both were RED on main) - docs/07-conventions.md
- verify-drain.ts quiet-hours: built its "closed" window from now() INSIDE the suite's single transaction. now() IS transaction_timestamp() and is frozen at transaction start (measured: unchanged across 65s while clock_timestamp advanced), so `now_min + 1` was already in the past and the window was genuinely OPEN - the drain correctly sent and the assertion failed. The PRODUCT was right; the test was wrong. Fixed with clock_timestamp() plus 5 minutes of slack (a +1 boundary can still be crossed while fixture rows insert), and the case now PRINTS the band it built (`window built: [1047, 1439) ET - current minute 1042`) so a future failure names the window instead of only "something sent".
- test-scheduled-gate-stamp.ts: the fixture never set campaign_stages.materialized_at. Since migration 0089 that stamp - not the existence of stage_sends rows - is the completeness signal: Phase A selects only materialized_at IS NULL, Phase B drains only materialized_at IS NOT NULL, so the stage fell BETWEEN both phases and the scheduler considered 0. The fixture predates 0089 and was never updated with it.
- Neither was a product defect, and neither was caused by the R1-R4 work; both were reproduced on clean main in a separate worktree before being touched. No schema change, no product code change - test harness only.

2026-08-17 - /settings/providers panel + audited posture toggle (migration 0139, R4) - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/06-integrations.md
- New page /settings/providers (nav: Settings -> Providers) with one sectioned card per provider row, so per-provider limits and country restrictions slot in later without a redesign. Shows the three send-posture facts SEPARATELY - capability (supports_api_send) / posture (sends_enabled) / latch (send_paused) - because collapsing them into one "is it sending?" badge is exactly the confusion the panel exists to remove. Also: capability badges, accounts + numbers counts, and "About this provider" from descriptor.notes.
- Migration 0139 widens send_circuit_events_event_check with 'sends_enabled_on' / 'sends_enabled_off'. NEW verbs, not the breaker's 'paused'/'resumed': reusing those would make an automated trip and an operator decision indistinguishable in the history, which is the one thing that table exists to keep apart. Additive, writes no rows, same shape as 0131.
- POST /api/providers/[providerId]/sends-enabled - its own endpoint, own Zod schema, audit row naming the actor, and deliberately NOT on providerBaseSchema so the bulk PATCH cannot carry it. Same carve-out as supports_api_send and send_paused: a field that decides whether SMS goes out must not live on a whole-object form that re-submits every key with no concurrency check.
- POST /api/providers/[providerId]/opt-out-footer - empty string normalizes to NULL so "cleared" is ONE value, not two (NULL is what makes the future chain fall through to stage.stop_text). The field is editable but the UI states plainly that it is stored and NOT yet applied to outgoing messages; the precedence chain is Q3 (card 869ej8r1y). An editable field that silently does nothing is a trap, so it says so.
- GET /api/settings/providers - one call for the whole panel, secret-free (accounts are a COUNT, never a credential list). The descriptor is resolved by adapter_code, never sms_provider_id, so the SECOND account of a type (txh2) gets its type's notes and capabilities; an identity-keyed lookup returns nothing for it.
- Guard: scripts/test-settings-providers-panel.ts. Asserts every typed row resolves a descriptor and has notes; asserts the txh family is genuinely >1 row before asserting both resolve the same descriptor (a one-row pass would prove nothing); asserts the CHECK ACCEPTS both new verbs AND still REJECTS a bogus one (without the second, a dropped constraint would pass); asserts validator normalization; and verifies the rollback by re-querying. Its secret-column scan strips comments first - the first version failed on the route's own comment saying it never selects api_key, i.e. it could not tell a reassurance from a leak.

2026-08-17 - Operator notes on the connection-type descriptor (R3) - docs/06-integrations.md
- ProviderDescriptor.notes: string[], seeded for all four connection types and served as `notes` on GET /api/provider-types (always an array, never null). Code-owned rather than a DB column: a note describes how a connection TYPE behaves, which is a property of the adapter; a column would let the two drift, and an operator editing "TextHub returns failures as 404" would be editing a fact, not a preference.
- PER_NUMBER_RATE_NOTE is defined once in types.ts and spread into all four descriptors (asserted present on each) - the per-second rate lives on provider_phones.max_sends_per_second, not the provider row, and operators reliably look for it on the provider.
- Seed content is restricted to what is verifiable from code or a probe script. The handoff's "fabricated short-code DLRs" line for TextHub was NOT written as given: no source supports it as observed TextHub behaviour. The verifiable fact - TextHub delivery receipts are never collected (their endpoint exists but nothing polls or stores it, so the Delivery report renders a dash, not 0%) - is what shipped.
- Contract pinned by scripts/test-provider-descriptors.ts: every type declares notes, no note is blank or under 20 chars, every type carries the shared rate note, and no note contains a credential-shaped substring.
- RETIRED an obsolete assertion in the same suite: it asserted `phoneSettingFields unset` on all four adapters, which Q2 (#81) invalidated by giving txr a real dashboard_id field - the suite had been FAILING on main since that merge. Replaced with the precise current truth (txr declares exactly one field named dashboard_id; the other three declare none), per the convention to retire an expired invariant rather than null out the data.
- No schema change.

2026-08-17 - Enforce per-provider sends_enabled across the send path (R2) - docs/07-conventions.md
- Six enforcement points, all of them load-bearing: kickoff refusal `provider_sends_disabled` (before materializing, so a switched-off provider never inserts stage_sends rows it cannot dispatch); drain gate + a PER-BATCH re-read (isProviderSendsEnabled, mirroring isProviderPaused - the column is runtime-mutable, so switching a provider off is a TRUE mid-run kill); scheduled.ts BOTH phases; preflight blocker; send-state `disabled_providers` (a separate list from paused_providers - different cause, different fix, different words); and stall-detector, without which switching a provider off would fire a stall alert for every stage it owns.
- Scheduler predicates are `p.sends_enabled IS NOT FALSE`, not `= true`: LEFT JOIN, so a stage with no provider row stays selectable exactly as before (kickoff's no_provider refusal owns that case). isProviderSendsEnabled fails CLOSED on a missing row where isProviderPaused fails OPEN - a vanished provider is not evidence of a tripped breaker, but it is not permission to keep sending either.
- Refusal wording never says "paused": nothing tripped and there is nothing to resume. HTTP 409, "turn it back on in Settings -> Providers", in all three refusal maps (kickoff, drain route, retry-failed route).
- Proof 1 (scripts/test-provider-sends-enabled-enforcement.ts): flips one real provider off inside a rolled-back tx; Phase B drops 1 -> 0 stages on that provider while other providers' selections are byte-identical. Reports each phase as PROVEN or NOT OBSERVABLE rather than showing a green tick for a 0 -> 0 comparison - Phase A is routinely NOT OBSERVABLE here because its job is to materialize, so an eligible stage stops being eligible the moment it is materialized (measured: of 46 approved non-archived stages on active tracked campaigns, 3 unsent and all 3 already materialized). Source assertions cover what data cannot.
- Proof 2 (scripts/test-stop-intake-ungated.ts, extended): STOP intake selections are byte-identical with sends_enabled off on all 8 rows. The flip is asserted non-vacuous (8 of 8), and post-rollback flags are compared against a PRE-transaction snapshot instead of a hardcoded "all true".
- All 8 provider rows end the phase sends_enabled = true; behaviour unchanged until someone deliberately switches an account off.
- No schema change (columns landed in 0138).

2026-08-17 - Per-provider sends_enabled + opt_out_footer (migration 0138, R1) - docs/03-data-model.md
- sms_providers.sends_enabled boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true, sms_providers.opt_out_footer text. Additive and INERT: nothing reads either column in this deploy (R2 wires enforcement, R4 the panel). All 8 rows land sends_enabled=true / opt_out_footer=NULL, so behaviour is byte-identical.
- Three flags, three meanings, never collapsed: supports_api_send = CAPABILITY (can it API-send at all), sends_enabled = POSTURE (should it right now, a human's call), send_paused = LATCH (a breaker tripped, needs a conscious resume). Merging posture into the latch makes a breaker trip and an operator decision indistinguishable in the audit trail.
- opt_out_footer ships NULL everywhere and is deliberately NOT consumed by the send path; the precedence chain lands with Q3 (card 869ej8r1y).
- Guard: scripts/verify-provider-sends-enabled-migration.ts asserts structure, asserts every row is still enabled/NULL, and scans the seven send-path modules for a PROVIDER-QUALIFIED reference to either column (a bare `sends_enabled` token matches 10 legitimate org_settings lines - the two-switch gate - so the check keys on p.sends_enabled / sms_providers.sends_enabled / provider_sends_disabled / opt_out_footer and prints the excluded org-level count).
- Also corrects drift in db/schema.ts: the send_circuit_events_event_check declaration still read ('paused','resumed') after migration 0131 widened it with the api-send verbs.

2026-08-17 - Per-number provider settings come from the descriptor (Q2) - docs/06-integrations.md
- The phone form's hardcoded `providerKey === "txr"` gate is gone. Per-number fields are declared as `phoneSettingFields` on the adapter descriptor and resolved server-side from adapter_code, delivered as `phone_setting_fields` on the provider detail response.
- Keyed on adapter_code, never sms_provider_id: a SECOND account of a type (the txh2 row) now gets its type's fields, which the identity-keyed gate could not do. Adding a provider-specific field no longer means another hardcoded branch in the form.
- Resolved server-side because the phone form is a "use client" component - importing the adapter registry there would bundle every provider's HTTP client into the browser. Same reason connection_type rides on the credentials response.
- Proven as a differential against the OLD rule (scripts/verify-phone-setting-fields.ts): all 8 provider rows produce identical field sets under both rules; the comparison is asserted non-vacuous (txr declares dashboard_id); every declared field name is verified to be a real provider_phones column; and both TextHub rows resolve identical fields despite different identities.
- No schema change.

2026-08-17 - Per-phone short domain (migration 0137, Q1) - docs/03-data-model.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- provider_phones.short_domain_id, nullable FK to short_domains ON DELETE SET NULL (not CASCADE: removing a domain must never delete an externally-provisioned number; falling back to the brand default is the correct degradation).
- kickoff resolution is now phone override -> brand default. The override must be org-owned AND status=active, so B1 pending rows can never be minted under.
- BYTE-IDENTICAL for existing data, proven as a differential rather than asserted: 35 phones x 3 brands = 105 pairs resolved both ways (new two-branch logic vs the exact pre-0137 brand-only query), 0 differences; numberless stages identical across all brands; plus a rolled-back simulation proving the override is not dead code.
- Assignment guard shared by the phone create and update routes (lib/providers/short-domain-assignment.ts) so ownership and status rules cannot drift between them.
- New GET /api/short-domains/list?brand_id= feeds the picker. Returns pending rows WITH their status rather than filtering them out - an omitted row reads as "my domain did not save" and gets re-added.
- Convention added: verification tooling gets guard-grade treatment - print input scope, assert non-empty, fail loudly on a missing binary or empty variable.

2026-08-17 - txh2 registry alias retired; COALESCE fallbacks removed (869ej8qzk Part A, step 3) - docs/07-conventions.md
- Gate met by 40,120 live txh2 sends on the adapter_code path with zero adapter-resolution errors (247 errored attempts, all TextHub suppression, a normal `filtered` outcome).
- Registry alias `txh2 -> texthubAdapter` deleted; ALIAS_KEYS now empty. adapter_code carries the type, so a second account of an existing type is just another row with the same adapter_code - nothing to alias.
- COALESCE(adapter_code, sms_provider_id) fallbacks dropped from drain, kickoff (x2) and the opt-out poller. A NULL adapter_code on an api-send row is a DATA ERROR (0135 guards creation); a hard unknown_provider refusal surfaces it where a silent fallback would mask it.
- Both "does this type already exist?" lookups (create route + /api/provider-types) switched from enumerating registry keys to querying adapter_code. Without that the collision steer would have silently stopped naming the txh2 row.
- STOP-intake predicate `adapter_code = 'txh'` PROVEN before the change: selected credential set [2,462] under the original, interim COALESCE, and final forms - identical and non-empty. Now a permanent assertion in test-stop-intake-ungated.ts, with a guard that it spans more than one provider row.
- registryKeysForType() removed (no consumers). verify-adapter-code-switch.ts retired - it compared against a code path that no longer exists; its two durable assertions were migrated to test-stop-intake-ungated.ts and verify-adapter-code.ts rather than deleted.
- verify-adapter-code.ts's txh2 assertion INVERTED: the bare identity string must now NOT resolve, so an alias creeping back fails the guard.

2026-08-17 - A brand may have more than one short domain (migration 0136, Q0) - docs/03-data-model.md
- Drops short_domains_brand_id_uniq (0052). That constraint, not any hardcoded hostname, is what made the short domain feel fixed: short_domains, links.short_domain_id and app/r/[code] were already domain-agnostic (the redirect resolves by globally-unique code and never reads the Host header).
- Removes a restriction; touches no rows. The single row and all 3,197,988 links verified unchanged after apply.
- Behaviour unchanged today: kickoff's pick is already deterministic and status-scoped (WHERE brand_id = .. AND status = 'active' ORDER BY created_at, id LIMIT 1), so one row per brand still resolves identically - and B1's future pending rows are excluded by a clause that already exists.
- The cross-brand guard short_domains_org_id_domain_unique (org_id, domain) STAYS: two brands must never share a hostname.
2026-08-17 - campaign segments INTERSECT instead of UNION + segment-page counts exclude opt-outs - CLAUDE.md, docs/07-conventions.md, docs/04-features/audience-snapshot.md, docs/04-features/audience-segments.md
- Selecting 2+ include-segments UNION'd them, so adding a "filter-shaped" segment (only rule an `is_not`, matching nearly the whole org) GREW the audience: 44,480 -> 507,870 measured. `buildAudienceSourceClause` is shared with `snapshotAudience`, so activation would have frozen and sent to the inflated pool - a live-send hazard, not a display bug. Segments now INTERSECT; adding one can only narrow. UNION still lives inside a segment (rule combinator `or`).
- Two independent implementations had to change together: the INTERSECT chain in `buildAudienceSourceClause` (snapshot + draft-stage counts) and `previewAudience`, which can no longer `bool_or` a `from_segment` flag - it tags each branch with its ordinal and tests `segments_matched = <n>`. Drift between them would mean the preview no longer predicts what activation freezes.
- Segment-page audience reads now exclude opt-outs (header tile, Audience tab list + counts, rules preview, contacts CSV export) via `excludeOptOutsFromAudience()`. The page previously advertised an audience no campaign could draw - 54,356 on the segment page vs 44,480 as a campaign's "From segments", the gap being exactly its 9,876 opt-outs. Export excludes them for compliance, not just arithmetic. The campaign path is deliberately unchanged (it needs opt-outs in the source to report `excluded_for_optout`, and the snapshot anti-joins them once against an ANALYZE'd temp table).
- `segment_stats` opt_out/opt_in/clicker counters now computed over the full audience instead of manual `segment_contacts` membership, which was empty for most rule-based segments: a segment named "Clickers excl Buyers" showed `Clickers 0` and `Opt-Outs 0` while 9,876 of its audience were suppressed - which is why the loss was invisible. Reconciles as sendable + opt_outs = full audience. No-rule segments and timed-out queries keep the manual figures (columns are NOT NULL).
- Guard: scripts/test-segment-intersect-and-optout.ts - 14 assertions, expectations computed from raw SQL in-run so they can't drift; asserts both code paths land on the same count and that adding a segment never grows the audience. No migration.

2026-08-17 - adapter_code persisted on provider create + tls-t backfill (migration 0135) - docs/07-conventions.md
- POST /api/providers never wrote adapter_code, so every provider created through the connection-type picker landed NULL and could not send (the send path resolves getAdapter(COALESCE(adapter_code, sms_provider_id))). The picker shipped before 0134 added the column; neither change was wrong alone.
- Route now sets adapter_code from the chosen type: derived path = canonical; separate-row = distinct identity + canonical type (the point of that path); custom/no-API = NULL.
- Migration 0135 backfills provider 948 `tls-t` -> 'tls'. Additive, idempotent, targeted on sms_provider_id. Row stays INERT (supports_api_send untouched). snx/smpl stay NULL - legitimately custom. Migration ends with a guard that RAISEs if any api-send row lacks a resolvable adapter_code.
- Three new acceptance cases prove a CREATED provider resolves an adapter - the blind spot that let this ship, since P3 proved every refusal but never the usable happy path.
- verify-adapter-code.ts repurposed from a one-shot cutover gate to a standing invariant. Its original "both columns resolve identically" rule was correct only during the migration moment; post-cutover the columns legitimately differ (tls-t identity vs tls type), so it now asserts the EFFECTIVE key resolves and that a non-NULL adapter_code is never unregistered.
- Convention added: an unexplained detail in a passing run is a finding, not noise.

2026-08-17 — STOP intake un-coupled from sending capability (R0, provider-connections panel card) — docs/07-conventions.md
- `selectPollableCredentials` (TextHub opt-out poller) no longer filters on `supports_api_send = true`. Receiving an opt-out does not depend on being able to send.
- This was a LIVE compliance gap, not hypothetical: the provider page exposes `supports_api_send` as a one-click "Disable", and TextHub's push callback is broken on their side, so this poller is its ONLY intake. Pressing Disable silently stopped opt-out ingestion.
- Selected credential set is UNCHANGED today ([2,462]) — every TextHub row currently has the flag on. The change is about what happens when someone turns it off.
- New `scripts/test-stop-intake-ungated.ts` pins the invariant across all three intake selections (TextHub poller, Ahoi CDR poller, webhook token resolution): flips every sending flag off inside a rolled-back transaction and asserts the selections are identical, plus a source check that no sending predicate reappears.
- Convention added: STOP intake is never gated on a sending flag.

2026-08-17 — Send path switched to read `adapter_code` (869ej8qzk Part A, step 2) — docs/07-conventions.md
- Four type-meaning read sites moved off `sms_provider_id`: the drain's `provider_key` select, the kickoff API-capability gate, the kickoff opt-out-guard key, and the TextHub-family filter in the opt-out poller (`IN ('txh','txh2')` → `adapter_code = 'txh'`).
- Identity-meaning reads deliberately UNCHANGED — circuit breakers, send windows, per-provider reporting and cost attribution are per-ACCOUNT and must not merge two accounts' counters.
- Proven by `scripts/verify-adapter-code-switch.ts`: old vs new query run side by side against production. Opt-out poller selects the identical credential set [2,462]; every send-eligible stage group resolves (txh 607, txh2 423 → txh, ahi 4, txr 1, tls 1); `snx`/`smpl` collapse from "unregistered code throws" to "NULL throws" — the SAME `unknown_provider` refusal, and both are non-API rows.
- `scripts/test-provider-registry-db-keys.ts` updated to the new contract: it now asserts every api-send row's `adapter_code` resolves, and no longer pins the `txh2` registry alias — pinning it would have failed the guard on exactly the deploy that removes it.
- Convention added: which column answers which question, and why the cutover was staged across three deploys.
- The registry alias is NOT removed in this step; that follows once this is verified in production.

2026-08-17 — `sms_providers.adapter_code` split out of `sms_provider_id` (migration 0134, 869ej8qzk Part A) — docs/03-data-model.md
- Strictly additive: one nullable column + per-row backfill (`txh`/`txh2` → `txh`; `ahi`/`txr`/`tls` → self; `snx`/`smpl` → NULL). No DROP, nothing rewritten, `sms_provider_id` untouched.
- Backfill is spelled out per row rather than copying `sms_provider_id`, because `txh2` is exactly what a blind copy gets wrong — its adapter is TextHub's, not one called "txh2".
- New `scripts/verify-adapter-code.ts` gates the cutover: proves resolution via `adapter_code` reaches the object-identical adapter to resolution via `sms_provider_id`, for every row. Ran green on all 7 before any code was switched.
- Part B (collapsing the `txh2` row into `txh`) remains REJECTED — 412 campaign stages, 1.38M stage_sends, and merged circuit-breaker counters. `txh2` is a legitimate multi-account row under the 0110 model.

2026-08-17 — Connection-type picker for provider creation, with explicit collision handling (869egmakh P3 — closes the card) — docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- `POST /api/providers` now takes `connection_type`; the provider code is DERIVED from the adapter registry instead of typed. Shown read-only but visible, because the code appears in drain errors and reports.
- ANTI-DRIFT: picking a type that already has a provider row returns 409 `connection_type_exists` with the existing rows attached — never a silently auto-suffixed code, which is exactly how `txh2` came to exist. The UI steers to "add an account to that provider". A separate row stays possible but requires `create_separate_row: true` AND an explicit distinct code, rejected if it collides with a registry key.
- The existence check is alias-aware (`registryKeysForType`): asking for TextHub reports both `txh` and `txh2`. A canonical-code-only check would have missed txh2 and offered a third TextHub row.
- `GET /api/provider-types` gained org-scoped `existing_providers`; it is no longer a purely static endpoint and its header comment was corrected to say so.
- New `POST /api/provider-types/[key]/validate` checks credential values not yet stored (provider-create time). Same descriptor check, same three states, nothing persisted.
- SCOPE CHANGE: "Test before saving" on the create form is NOT built. Provider creation and credential creation stay separate — the operator lands on the provider page where Test connection works against a real stored account. Avoids coupling two creates in one transaction for a second way to add a key.
- "Custom / no API" escape hatch retained for adapter-less rows (snx, smpl).
- Conventions added: cleanup must assert rather than hope (a teardown that fails after ALL PASS is invisible); probes that WRITE run against the camman-v2 demo DB by default, prod writes need explicit approval like any other production data write.
- No schema change, no migration.

2026-08-17 — Uniform Test connection + server-side gates on the two provider-specific actions (869egmakh P2) — docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- New `POST /api/providers/[id]/credentials/[credId]/test-connection`: one descriptor-driven, non-sending check for every connection type. Returns three states (`valid`/`invalid`/`unknown`) plus optional `discovered` items. Admin+, read-only at the provider, NOT gated by SEND_ENABLED.
- Deleted the txr-only `…/healthcheck` endpoint it supersedes. Text Request's dashboard ids moved into `discovered`, so binding a `dashboard_id` to a number still works from the same dialog — no capability lost.
- SECURITY: `…/credentials/test` (spends money) and `…/register-callback` (mutates remote state) were gated ONLY in the client component; the routes accepted any provider's credential and fired it at TextHub's API. Both now re-check `supportsTestSend` / `supportsOptOutCallbackRegistration` from the descriptor. Proven by scripts/test-provider-action-gates.ts with real authenticated requests against Ahoi, Text Request and Tells credentials.
- The send-test route now checks the connection type BEFORE SEND_ENABLED: a misrouted request gets the right error instead of "turn sending on", and the gate is provable without arming sending.
- UI: `unknown` renders as its own amber state ("Couldn't verify"), never folded into pass or fail. A type with no non-sending check (Tells) shows explanatory text instead of a button — not a disabled control, and never a check that cannot fail.
- Conventions added: date stamps come from the current date, never from data timestamps; `gh pr checks` exits non-zero while pending, so never wrap it in a fallback that maps failure to an empty result.
- No schema change, no migration.

2026-08-17 — Provider connection-type descriptors (869egmakh P1): static `descriptor` on every SMS adapter (displayName, blurb, credentialFields, three-state `validateCredentials`), `GET /api/provider-types`, registry split into alias-tolerant `getDescriptor()` + canonical `listConnectionTypes()` — docs/06-integrations.md, docs/07-conventions.md
- Non-sending credential checks reuse existing clients rather than adding new ones: TextHub via `fetchInbox`, Text Request via `textrequestHealthcheck`, Ahoi via a same-day CDR pull. Tells deliberately has NO `validateCredentials` — its only endpoint sends.
- `ValidateCredentialsResult` is three-state (`valid`/`invalid`/`unknown`); `unknown` must never collapse into pass or fail. Ahoi and Tells answer HTTP 200 on auth failure, so an envelope change has to degrade to "couldn't verify", not to a false green.
- Ahoi classifier pinned to bodies measured by `scripts/probe-ahoi-badkey.ts` (kept as the regression reference): HTTP 200 + `text/html` for every case, so the body is the only discriminator; key off JSON `status`, never the message; never classify on CSV row count.
- Convention added: run git as `git -C <absolute worktree path>` — the agent shell's cwd is not reliably persistent between calls and a relative command can execute against the shared checkout.
- No schema change, no migration. Behavior additive: the descriptor field is optional and nothing reads it yet outside the new endpoint.

2026-08-14 — Fresh pool restored to its offer-scoped, sendable-only definition (migration 0133, not yet applied to prod): 0126 had silently replaced "sendable and never sent this offer" with a 90-day/all-offer/no-opt-out-filter count, and 0128/0132 carried the regression forward while the feature doc was edited to match the broken SQL. Added verify-offer-group-attribution.ts criterion 7 (7a recomputes fresh_pool from contacts/opt_outs/offer_exposures and asserts it against the stored matview value; 7b asserts a group's fresh_pool differs across the offers it appears under) as a regression guard against reintroducing either defect; both currently fail by design pending 0133's apply. Corrected the Fresh pool row and surrounding prose in the feature doc, and added a doc↔code drift entry to conventions alongside the sibling security_invoker case — scripts/verify-offer-group-attribution.ts, docs/04-features/offer-group-report.md, docs/07-conventions.md

## 2026-08-14 — Delivery report breaks down by phone number; grain widened to (stage, phone) — docs: 04-features/delivery-report, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **One provider can run several numbers with very different deliverability.** `/reports/delivery` provider rows now expand into **per-number sub-rows** (same columns, plus the number and its type). The motivating case is live: `txh2` runs short code `621637` (308,828 sends/7d) AND toll-free `+18446210404` (36,802) — collapsed into a single row until now. Sub-rows sum exactly to their parent.
- **⚠️ Grain widened from `(stage)` to `(stage, provider_phone)`, and the number is attributed from THE SEND, not the stage.** No stage currently sends from more than one number (0 of 882, all 2,954,934 sent rows) — but that is **incidental, not structural**: `stage_sends.provider_phone_id` is stamped from the stage row read **once per materialization INVOCATION**, materialization is **resumable across invocations**, and **nothing guards `campaign_stages.provider_phone_id` against being edited in between** (the stage PATCH locks `scheduled_at` only). Partially-materialized stages genuinely occur (2 at time of writing). Deriving the number from the stage would silently credit all of a split stage's sends to whichever number the stage holds *now* — precisely on the number whose deliverability someone was investigating. The split-stage case is pinned as a unit regression.
- The **campaign** is still derived from the stage — that link IS structural (FK). `rollupByStage` now SUMS a stage's number-rows instead of assuming one.
